\section{Topological properties of finitely presented flat morphisms. Flatness criteria}
\label{section:IV.11}


\subsection{Flatness loci (Noetherian case)}
\label{subsection:IV.11.1}

\oldpage[IV-3]{117}

\begin{theorem}[11.1.1]
\label{IV.11.1.1}
Let $\mathrm{Y}$ be a locally Noetherian prescheme, $f:\mathrm{X}\to\mathrm{Y}$ a morphism locally of finite type, $\sh{F}$ an $\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}$-Module coherent. Then the set $\mathrm{U}$ of $x\in\mathrm{X}$ such that $\sh{F}$ is $f$-flat at the point $x$ is open in $\mathrm{X}$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The question being evidently local on $\mathrm{X}$ and $\mathrm{Y}$, we can suppose $\mathrm{Y}=\Spec(\mathrm{A})$, $\mathrm{X}=\Spec(\mathrm{B})$, $\mathrm{A}$ being Noetherian and $\mathrm{B}$ an A-algebra of finite type. We then have $\sh{F}=\widetilde{\mathrm{M}}$, where $\mathrm{M}$ is a B-module of finite type. Applying the criterion $(\mathbf{0}_{\mathrm{III}},~9.2.6)$: it suffices therefore to prove the following assertion:

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{(11.1.1.1)}
\emph{Let $\mathrm{A}$ be a Noetherian ring, $\mathrm{B}$ an $\mathrm{A}$-algebra of finite type, $\mathrm{M}$ a $\mathrm{B}$-module of finite type, $\mathfrak{q}$ a prime ideal of $\mathrm{B}$, $\mathfrak{p}$ its inverse image in $\mathrm{A}$. Suppose that $\mathrm{M}_\mathfrak{q}$ is a flat $\mathrm{A}_\mathfrak{p}$-module. Then there exists $g\in\mathrm{B}-\mathfrak{q}$ such that, for every prime ideal $\mathfrak{q}'\supset\mathfrak{q}$ of $\mathrm{B}$ such that $g\notin\mathfrak{q}'$, $\mathrm{M}_{\mathfrak{q}'}$ is a flat $\mathrm{A}_{\mathfrak{p}'}$-module, where $\mathfrak{p}'$ denotes the inverse image of $\mathfrak{q}'$ in $\mathrm{A}$ (it amounts to the same $(\mathbf{0}_\mathrm{I},~6.3.1)$ to say that $\mathrm{M}_{\mathfrak{q}'}$ is a flat $\mathrm{A}$-module).}
\medskip

Consider for this $\mathrm{B}_{\mathfrak{q}'}$ as an A-algebra; one has evidently $\mathfrak{p}\mathrm{B}_{\mathfrak{q}'}\subset\mathfrak{q}'\mathrm{B}_{\mathfrak{q}'}$.
We know then $(\mathbf{0}_{\mathrm{III}},~10.2.2)$ that for $\mathrm{M}_{\mathfrak{q}'}$ to be a flat A-module, it is necessary and sufficient that $\mathrm{M}_{\mathfrak{q}'}/\mathfrak{p}\mathrm{M}_{\mathfrak{q}'}$ be a flat $(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{p})$-module and that $\operatorname{Tor}_1^\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{M}_{\mathfrak{q}'},\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{p})=0$.
On the other hand, as $\mathrm{M}_\mathfrak{q}$ is an $\mathrm{A}_\mathfrak{p}$-module flat, one has $\operatorname{Tor}_1^\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{M}_\mathfrak{q},\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{p})=0$, which can also be written $(\operatorname{Tor}_1^\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{M},\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{p}))_\mathfrak{q}=0$.
But as $\mathrm{A}$ and $\mathrm{B}$ are Noetherian, $\operatorname{Tor}_1^\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{M},\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{p})$ is a B-module of finite type, hence $(\mathbf{0}_\mathrm{I},~5.2.2)$ there exists $g\in\mathrm{B}-\mathfrak{q}$ such that $(\operatorname{Tor}_1^\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{M},\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{p}))_g=0$ and $hg\in\mathrm{B}-\mathfrak{q}$ since $h\in\mathrm{A}-\mathfrak{p}$.
Moreover, on the other hand, $(\mathrm{M}/\mathfrak{p}\mathrm{M})_{\bar{h}}$ is still a flat $(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{p})$-module, car it is written $(\mathrm{M}/\mathfrak{p}\mathrm{M})_{\overline{hg}}$, where $\overline{hg}$ is the canonical image of $hg$ in $\mathrm{B}/\mathfrak{p}\mathrm{B}$, and it suffices to apply $(\mathbf{0}_\mathrm{I},~6.3.2)$; finally, on the other hand, $(\operatorname{Tor}_1^\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{M},\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{p}))_{hg}=0$ since $hg\in\mathrm{B}-\mathfrak{q}$.
Taking into account $(\mathbf{0}_\mathrm{I},~6.3.2)$, we see that we are reduced to proving the

\begin{lemma}[11.1.1.2]
\label{IV.11.1.1.2}
Under the conditions of \emph{(11.1.1.1)}, there exists $g\in\mathrm{B}-\mathfrak{q}$ such that:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\emph{\roman*)}]
  \item $(\mathrm{M}/\mathfrak{p}\mathrm{M})_g$ is a flat $(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{p})$-module;
  \item $(\operatorname{Tor}_1^\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{M},\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{p}))_g=0$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\oldpage[IV-3]{118}

By virtue of the generic flatness theorem $(6.9.1)$ applied to the integral ring $\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{p}$, to the $(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{p})$-algebra of finite type $\mathrm{B}/\mathfrak{p}\mathrm{B}$, and to the $(\mathrm{B}/\mathfrak{p}\mathrm{B})$-module of finite type $\mathrm{M}/\mathfrak{p}\mathrm{M}$, there exists $h\in\mathrm{A}-\mathfrak{p}$ such that, if $\bar{h}$ is its canonical image in $\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{p}$, $(\mathrm{M}/\mathfrak{p}\mathrm{M})_{\bar{h}}$ be a flat $(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{p})$-module.
On the other hand, as $\mathrm{M}_\mathfrak{q}$ is an $\mathrm{A}_\mathfrak{p}$-module flat $(\mathbf{0}_\mathrm{I},~6.3.1)$, on the other hand $\operatorname{Tor}_1^\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{M}_\mathfrak{q},\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{p})=0$, which can also be written $(\operatorname{Tor}_1^\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{M},\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{p}))_\mathfrak{q}=0$. But as $\mathrm{A}$ and $\mathrm{B}$ are Noetherian, $\operatorname{Tor}_1^\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{M},\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{p})$ is a B-module of finite type, hence $(\mathbf{0}_\mathrm{I},~5.2.2)$ there exists $g\in\mathrm{B}-\mathfrak{q}$ such that $(\operatorname{Tor}_1^\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{M},\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{p}))_g=0$ and $hg\in\mathrm{B}-\mathfrak{q}$ since $h\in\mathrm{A}-\mathfrak{p}$. Moreover, $(\mathrm{M}/\mathfrak{p}\mathrm{M})_{hg}$ is still a flat $(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{p})$-module, since it is written $(\mathrm{M}/\mathfrak{p}\mathrm{M})_{\overline{hg}}$, where $\overline{hg}$ is the canonical image of $hg$ in $\mathrm{B}/\mathfrak{p}\mathrm{B}$, and it suffices to apply $(\mathbf{0}_\mathrm{I},~6.3.2)$; finally, $(\operatorname{Tor}_1^\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{M},\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{p}))_{hg}=0$ since $hg\in\mathrm{B}-\mathfrak{q}$. Taking into account $(\mathbf{0}_\mathrm{I},~6.3.2)$, we see that we are reduced to proving the following:
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[11.1.2]
\label{IV.11.1.2}
Let $\mathrm{Y}$ be a locally Noetherian prescheme, $f:\mathrm{X}\to\mathrm{Y}$ a morphism locally of finite type, $\sh{F}$, $\sh{F}'$ two $\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}$-Modules coherent, $u:\sh{F}'\to\sh{F}$ a homomorphism of $\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}$-Modules. Suppose that $\sh{F}$ is $f$-flat. Then the set $\mathrm{U}$ of $x\in\mathrm{X}$ such that, setting $y=f(x)$, the homomorphism $u_x\otimes 1:\sh{F}'_x\otimes_{\sh{O}_y}\boldsymbol{k}(y)\to\sh{F}_x\otimes_{\sh{O}_y}\boldsymbol{k}(y)$ is injective, is open in $\mathrm{X}$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Indeed, let $\sh{N}$ (resp.\ $\sh{P}$) be the kernel (resp.\ the cokernel) of $u$; applying $(\mathbf{0}_{\mathrm{III}},~10.2.4)$\footnote{Here is a proof of $(\mathbf{0}_{\mathrm{III}},~10.2.4)$, which was not published in the \emph{Alg\`ebre commutative} of N.~Bourbaki. Set $\mathrm{P}=\operatorname{Im}(u)$, $\mathrm{Q}=\operatorname{Coker}(u)$, $\mathrm{R}=\operatorname{Ker}(u)$. The composite $\mathrm{M}\otimes k\to\mathrm{P}\otimes k\to\mathrm{N}\otimes k$ is injective, and $\mathrm{M}\otimes k\to\mathrm{P}\otimes k$ is surjective, hence $\mathrm{P}\otimes k\to\mathrm{N}\otimes k$ is injective and $\mathrm{M}\otimes k\to\mathrm{P}\otimes k$ is bijective. The exact sequence $0\to\mathrm{P}\to\mathrm{N}\to\mathrm{Q}\to 0$ gives the exact sequence
\[
0=\operatorname{Tor}_1^\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{N},k)\to\operatorname{Tor}_1^\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{Q},k)\to\mathrm{P}\otimes k\to\mathrm{N}\otimes k\to\mathrm{Q}\otimes k\to 0
\]
and since $\mathrm{P}\otimes k\to\mathrm{N}\otimes k$ is injective, one has $\operatorname{Tor}_1^\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{Q},k)=0$; as $\mathrm{Q}$ is a B-module of finite type, $(\mathbf{0}_{\mathrm{III}},~10.2.2)$ shows that $\mathrm{Q}$ is also an A-module flat by $(\mathbf{0}_\mathrm{I},~6.1.2)$. The sequence $0\to\mathrm{R}\to\mathrm{M}\to\mathrm{P}\to 0$ is exact, hence by $(\mathbf{0}_\mathrm{I},~6.1.2)$, $\mathrm{R}=0$, and hence $\mathrm{R}\otimes k=0$; but as $\mathrm{B}$ is Noetherian, $\mathrm{R}$ is a B-module of finite type, hence $\mathrm{R}=0$ by virtue of Nakayama's lemma.}
to the local rings $\sh{O}_x$ and $\sh{O}_y$, and to the $\sh{O}_x$-modules $\sh{F}'_x$ and $\sh{F}_x$: saying that $u_x\otimes 1$ is injective is equivalent to saying that $\sh{P}$ is $f$-flat at the point $x$. Now as $\sh{N}$ and $\sh{P}$ are coherent, the corollary follows from \sref{IV.11.1.1} and from $(\mathbf{0},~15.2.4)$.

In particular:
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[11.1.3]
\label{IV.11.1.3}
Let $\mathrm{Y}$ be a locally Noetherian prescheme, $f:\mathrm{X}\to\mathrm{Y}$ a morphism locally of finite type, $g$ a section of $\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}$ above $\mathrm{X}$. The set of $x\in\mathrm{X}$ such that $g_x\otimes 1$ is a non-zero-divisor in $\sh{O}_x\otimes_{\sh{O}_{f(x)}}\boldsymbol{k}(f(x))$ is open in $\mathrm{X}$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
It suffices to apply \sref{IV.11.1.2} to the endomorphism of $\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}$ defined by $g$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[11.1.4]
\label{IV.11.1.4}
Let $\mathrm{Y}$ be a locally Noetherian prescheme, $f:\mathrm{X}\to\mathrm{Y}$ a morphism locally of finite type, $\sh{F}$ an $\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}$-Module coherent and $f$-flat. Let $(g_i)_{1\leqslant i\leqslant n}$ be a sequence of sections of $\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}$ above $\mathrm{X}$. The set $\mathrm{U}$ of $x\in\mathrm{X}$ such that the sequence $((g_i)_x\otimes 1)$ is $(\sh{F}_x\otimes_{\sh{O}_{f(x)}}\boldsymbol{k}(f(x)))$-regular is open in $\mathrm{X}$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
\oldpage[IV-3]{119}
As $\sh{F}$ is $f$-flat, the corollary follows from \sref{IV.11.1.1} and from $(\mathbf{0},~15.2.4)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[11.1.5]
\label{IV.11.1.5}
Let $\mathrm{X}$, $\mathrm{Y}$, $\mathrm{Z}$ be three locally Noetherian preschemes, $f:\mathrm{X}\to\mathrm{Y}$, $g:\mathrm{Y}\to\mathrm{Z}$ two morphisms of finite type, $\sh{F}$ an $\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}$-Module coherent. Then the set $\mathrm{U}$ of $y\in\mathrm{Y}$ such that, for every generization $y'$ of $y$, $\sh{F}$ is $(g\circ f)$-flat at all the points of $f^{-1}(y')$ (i.e.\ such that $\sh{F}\otimes_\mathrm{Y}\Spec(\sh{O}_{\mathrm{Y},y})$ is flat relative to the morphism $\mathrm{X}\times_\mathrm{Y}\Spec(\sh{O}_{\mathrm{Y},y})\to\mathrm{Z}$) is open in $\mathrm{Y}$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
If $\mathrm{V}$ is the set of $x\in\mathrm{X}$ where $\sh{F}$ is $(g\circ f)$-flat, $\mathrm{U}$ is the set of $y\in\mathrm{Y}$ such that for every generization $y'$ of $y$, one has $f^{-1}(y')\subset\mathrm{V}$. Now $\mathrm{V}$ is open \sref{IV.11.1.1} hence locally constructible in $\mathrm{X}$, and the set $\mathrm{W}$ of $y\in\mathrm{Y}$ such that $f^{-1}(y)\subset\mathrm{V}$ is also locally constructible in $\mathrm{Y}$ by virtue of the Chevalley theorem $(1.8.4)$. But it follows then from $(\mathbf{0}_{\mathrm{III}},~9.2.5)$ that the interior points of $\mathrm{U}$ are the interior points of $\mathrm{W}$, whence the conclusion.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[11.1.6]
\label{IV.11.1.6}
Let $\mathrm{A}$ be a Noetherian ring, $\mathrm{B}$ an $\mathrm{A}$-algebra of finite type, $\mathrm{C}$ a $\mathrm{B}$-algebra of finite type, $\mathrm{M}$ a $\mathrm{C}$-module of finite type. Then the set of $\mathfrak{q}\in\Spec(\mathrm{B})$ such that $\mathrm{M}_\mathfrak{q}$ is a flat $\mathrm{A}$-module is open in $\Spec(\mathrm{B})$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Taking into account $(2.1.2)$, this is a consequence of \sref{IV.11.1.5} applied to $\mathrm{Z}=\Spec(\mathrm{A})$, $\mathrm{Y}=\Spec(\mathrm{B})$, $\mathrm{X}=\Spec(\mathrm{C})$, $\sh{F}=\widetilde{\mathrm{M}}$.

The results of this number will be freed from the Noetherian hypotheses in \sref{IV.11.3}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Flatness of a projective limit of preschemes}
\label{subsection:IV.11.2}

\oldpage[IV-3]{119}

\begin{env}[11.2.1]
\label{IV.11.2.1}
Let $\mathrm{A}$ be a ring, $\mathrm{M}$, $\mathrm{N}$ two A-modules, $\mathrm{A}'$ an A-algebra; set $\mathrm{M}'=\mathrm{M}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'$, $\mathrm{N}'=\mathrm{N}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'$. Recall $(\textbf{III},~6.3.8)$ that one defines, for every $i$, a canonical homomorphism of A-modules
\begin{equation}
  \psi_i:\operatorname{Tor}_i^\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{M},\mathrm{N})\to\operatorname{Tor}_i^{\mathrm{A}'}(\mathrm{M}',\mathrm{N}')
  \tag{11.2.1.1}
\end{equation}
in the following way: one considers a left resolution of $\mathrm{M}$ by free A-modules
\begin{equation}
  \ldots\longrightarrow\mathrm{L}_{i+1}\xrightarrow{f_i}\mathrm{L}_i\longrightarrow\ldots\longrightarrow\mathrm{L}_0\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}\mathrm{M}\longrightarrow 0
  \tag{11.2.1.2}
\end{equation}
whence one deduces by tensoring with $\mathrm{A}'$ a complex of $\mathrm{A}'$-modules
\begin{equation}
  \ldots\longrightarrow\mathrm{L}'_{i+1}\xrightarrow{f'_i}\mathrm{L}'_i\longrightarrow\ldots\longrightarrow\mathrm{L}'_0\xrightarrow{\varepsilon'}\mathrm{M}'\longrightarrow 0
  \tag{11.2.1.3}
\end{equation}
where we have set $\mathrm{L}'_i=\mathrm{L}_i\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'$, $f'_i=f_i\otimes 1_{\mathrm{A}'}$, $\varepsilon'=\varepsilon\otimes 1_{\mathrm{A}'}$. Consider on the other hand a left resolution of $\mathrm{M}'$ by free $\mathrm{A}'$-modules
\begin{equation}
  \ldots\longrightarrow\mathrm{L}''_{i+1}\xrightarrow{f'_i}\mathrm{L}''_i\longrightarrow\ldots\longrightarrow\mathrm{L}''_0\xrightarrow{\varepsilon''}\mathrm{M}'\longrightarrow 0
  \tag{11.2.1.4}
\end{equation}
\end{env}

\oldpage[IV-3]{120}

As the $\mathrm{L}'_i$ are free $\mathrm{A}'$-modules, we know $(\mathrm{M},~\mathrm{V},~1.1)$ that there are $\mathrm{A}'$-homomorphisms $u_i$ forming a commutative diagram
\[
\xymatrix{
  \ldots \ar[r] & \mathrm{L}'_{i+1} \ar[r]^{f'_i} \ar[d]^{u_{i+1}} & \mathrm{L}'_i \ar[r] \ar[d]^{u_i} & \ldots \ar[r] & \mathrm{L}'_0 \ar[r]^{\varepsilon'} \ar[d]^{u_0} & \mathrm{M}' \ar[r] \ar[d]^{1_{\mathrm{M}'}} & 0 \\
  \ldots \ar[r] & \mathrm{L}''_{i+1} \ar[r]^{f''_i} & \mathrm{L}''_i \ar[r] & \ldots \ar[r] & \mathrm{L}''_0 \ar[r]^{\varepsilon''} & \mathrm{M}' \ar[r] & 0
}
\]

If one composes the homomorphism $u_\bullet:\mathrm{L}'_\bullet\to\mathrm{L}''_\bullet$ of complexes thus defined with the canonical homomorphism $\mathrm{L}_\bullet\to\mathrm{L}'_\bullet$ of A-modules, one obtains a homomorphism of complexes of A-modules $\mathrm{L}_\bullet\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{N}\to\mathrm{L}''_\bullet\otimes_{\mathrm{A}'}\mathrm{N}'$; noting that $(\mathrm{L}_i\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{N})\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'=\mathrm{L}'_i\otimes_{\mathrm{A}'}\mathrm{N}'$, one deduces a homomorphism of complexes of A-modules $\mathrm{L}_\bullet\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{N}\to\mathrm{L}''_\bullet\otimes_{\mathrm{A}'}\mathrm{N}'$, whence, passing to homology, the canonical homomorphisms \sref{IV.11.2.1}[IV.11.2.1.1]. As the $u_i$ are well-determined up to homotopy $(\mathrm{M},~\mathrm{V},~1.1)$, the homomorphisms \sref{IV.11.2.1}[IV.11.2.1.1] do not depend on the choice of the $u_i$ nor on the choice of the free resolutions $\mathrm{L}_\bullet$ and $\mathrm{L}''_\bullet$.

As the $\operatorname{Tor}_i^{\mathrm{A}'}(\mathrm{M}',\mathrm{N}')$ are $\mathrm{A}'$-modules, one deduces canonically from \sref{IV.11.2.1}[IV.11.2.1.1] $\mathrm{A}'$-homomorphisms
\begin{equation}
  \varphi_i:\operatorname{Tor}_i^\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{M},\mathrm{N})\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'\to\operatorname{Tor}_i^{\mathrm{A}'}(\mathrm{M}',\mathrm{N}').
  \tag{11.2.1.5}
\end{equation}

Consider now two ring homomorphisms
\[
  \rho:\mathrm{A}\to\mathrm{A}^{(1)},\quad \sigma:\mathrm{A}^{(1)}\to\mathrm{A}^{(2)},
\]
and their composite $\sigma\circ\rho:\mathrm{A}\to\mathrm{A}^{(2)}$; set $\mathrm{M}^{(j)}=\mathrm{M}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}^{(j)}$, $\mathrm{N}^{(j)}=\mathrm{N}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}^{(j)}$ for $j=1,2$. Then the canonical composite homomorphism
\[
  \operatorname{Tor}_i^\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{M},\mathrm{N})\to\operatorname{Tor}_i^{\mathrm{A}^{(0)}}(\mathrm{M}^{(1)},\mathrm{N}^{(1)})\to\operatorname{Tor}_i^{\mathrm{A}^{(0)}}(\mathrm{M}^{(2)},\mathrm{N}^{(2)})
\]
is the same as the canonical homomorphism deduced from $\sigma\circ\rho$; this follows from the fact that, if $\mathrm{L}_\bullet^{(j)}$ is a free resolution of $\mathrm{M}^{(j)}$, the diagram
\[
\xymatrix{
  \mathrm{L}_\bullet \ar[r] & \mathrm{L}_\bullet\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}^{(1)} \ar[r] \ar[d] & \mathrm{L}_\bullet^{(1)} \ar[d] \\
  & \mathrm{L}_\bullet^{(1)}\otimes_{\mathrm{A}^{(1)}}\mathrm{A}^{(2)} \ar[r] & \mathrm{L}_\bullet^{(2)}
}
\]
is commutative.

\begin{env}[11.2.2]
\label{IV.11.2.2}
The notations being those of \sref{IV.11.2.1}, consider now an inductive system of A-algebras $(\mathrm{A}_\alpha,g_{\beta\alpha})$, and for every index $\alpha$, set $\mathrm{M}_\alpha=\mathrm{M}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}_\alpha$, $\mathrm{N}_\alpha=\mathrm{N}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}_\alpha$; if one denotes by $g_\alpha:\mathrm{A}_\alpha\to\mathrm{A}'$ the canonical homomorphism, one deduces canonical homomorphisms \sref{IV.11.2.1}[IV.11.2.1.1] $\psi_{i\beta\alpha}:\operatorname{Tor}_i^{\mathrm{A}_\alpha}(\mathrm{M}_\alpha,\mathrm{N}_\alpha)\to\operatorname{Tor}_i^{\mathrm{A}'}(\mathrm{M}',\mathrm{N}')$ which (always by virtue of \sref{IV.11.2.1}[IV.11.2.1.1]) form an inductive system of homomorphisms; we propose to complete the result of $(\mathrm{M},~\mathrm{V},~9.4^*)$ by showing that the
\begin{equation}
  \psi_i=\varinjlim\psi_{i\alpha}:\varinjlim_\alpha\operatorname{Tor}_i^{\mathrm{A}_\alpha}(\mathrm{M}_\alpha,\mathrm{N}_\alpha)\to\operatorname{Tor}_i^{\mathrm{A}'}(\mathrm{M}',\mathrm{N}')
  \tag{11.2.2.1}
\end{equation}
are \emph{isomorphisms of $\mathrm{A}'$-modules}. To this end, we proceed as in $(\mathrm{M},~\mathrm{V},~9.5')$, associating to each $\mathrm{M}_\alpha$ its \emph{canonical free resolution}. It amounts (taking into account the exactness of the functor $\varinjlim$) to proving the
\end{env}

\oldpage[IV-3]{121}

\begin{lemma}[11.2.2.2]
\label{IV.11.2.2.2}
Let $(\mathrm{A}_\alpha,g_{\beta\alpha})$ be an inductive system of rings, $((\mathrm{M}_\alpha,h_{\beta\alpha})$ an inductive system of sets, $\mathrm{A}'=\varinjlim\mathrm{A}_\alpha$, $\mathrm{M}'=\varinjlim\mathrm{M}_\alpha$, $g_\alpha:\mathrm{A}_\alpha\to\mathrm{A}'$, $h_\alpha:\mathrm{M}_\alpha\to\mathrm{M}'$ the canonical maps. For every $\alpha$, let $\mathrm{F}(\mathrm{M}_\alpha)$ be the $\mathrm{A}_\alpha$-module of formal linear combinations of elements of $\mathrm{M}_\alpha$; or equivalently the $\mathrm{A}'$-module of formal linear combinations of elements of $\mathrm{M}'$; if $h_{\beta\alpha}^0:\mathrm{F}(\mathrm{M}_\alpha)\to\mathrm{F}(\mathrm{M}_\beta)$ (for $\alpha\leqslant\beta$) and $h'_\alpha:\mathrm{F}(\mathrm{M}_\alpha)\to\mathrm{F}(\mathrm{M}')$ are the $\mathrm{A}_\alpha$-homomorphisms deduced from $h_{\beta\alpha}$ and $h_\alpha$ respectively, $(\mathrm{F}(\mathrm{M}_\alpha),h^0_{\beta\alpha})$ is an inductive system of $\mathrm{A}_\alpha$-modules and $(h'_\alpha)$ an inductive system of homomorphisms. Then
\[
  h''=\varinjlim h'_\alpha:\varinjlim\mathrm{F}(\mathrm{M}_\alpha)\to\mathrm{F}(\mathrm{M}')=\mathrm{F}(\varinjlim\mathrm{M}_\alpha)
\]
\emph{is an isomorphism.}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For the proof, see Bourbaki, \emph{Alg.}, chap.~II, 3\textsuperscript{e}~\'{e}d., \textsection\,6, n\textsuperscript{o}\,6, cor.\ de la prop.~10.
\end{proof}

\begin{env}[11.2.3]
\label{IV.11.2.3}
Let us resume the notations of \sref{IV.11.2.1} and consider in particular the case $i=1$; set $\mathrm{T}=\operatorname{Tor}_1^\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{M},\mathrm{N})$, $\mathrm{T}'=\mathrm{T}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'$, $\mathrm{T}''=\operatorname{Tor}_1^{\mathrm{A}'}(\mathrm{M}',\mathrm{N}')$; then $\psi_1$ is the homomorphism $\operatorname{Ker}(f_0\otimes 1_\mathrm{N})/\operatorname{Im}(f_1\otimes 1_\mathrm{N})\to\operatorname{Ker}(f''_0\otimes 1_{\mathrm{N}'})/\operatorname{Im}(f''_1\otimes 1_{\mathrm{N}'})$ which is deduced by passage to the quotients from the restriction of $u_1\otimes 1:\mathrm{L}_0\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{N}\to\mathrm{L}''_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}'}\mathrm{N}'$.

Set $\mathrm{R}=\operatorname{Ker}(\varepsilon)$, $\mathrm{R}''=\operatorname{Ker}(\varepsilon'')$, so that one has the exact sequences
\begin{equation}
  0\longrightarrow\mathrm{R}\xrightarrow{j}\mathrm{L}_0\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}\mathrm{M}\longrightarrow 0
  \quad\text{and}\quad
  0\longrightarrow\mathrm{R}''\xrightarrow{j''}\mathrm{L}''_0\xrightarrow{\varepsilon''}\mathrm{M}'\longrightarrow 0,
\end{equation}
whence one deduces the exact homology sequences
\begin{equation}
  0=\operatorname{Tor}_1^\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{L}_0,\mathrm{N})\to\mathrm{T}\xrightarrow{\partial}\mathrm{R}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{N}\xrightarrow{j\otimes 1}\mathrm{L}_0\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{N}\to\mathrm{M}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{N}\to 0
  \tag{11.2.3.1}
\end{equation}
\begin{equation}
  0=\operatorname{Tor}_1^{\mathrm{A}'}(\mathrm{L}''_0,\mathrm{N}')\to\mathrm{T}''\xrightarrow{\partial''}\mathrm{R}''\otimes_{\mathrm{A}'}\mathrm{N}'\xrightarrow{j''\otimes 1}\mathrm{L}''_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}'}\mathrm{N}'\xrightarrow{\varepsilon''\otimes 1}\mathrm{M}'\otimes_{\mathrm{A}'}\mathrm{N}'\to 0
  \tag{11.2.3.2}
\end{equation}

On the other hand there is a homomorphism of $\mathrm{A}'$-modules
\[
  v:\mathrm{R}'=\operatorname{Ker}(\varepsilon)\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'\longrightarrow\operatorname{Ker}(\varepsilon')\xrightarrow{u_0}\operatorname{Ker}(\varepsilon'')=\mathrm{R}''.
\]
\end{env}

\oldpage[IV-3]{122}

Let us show that the diagram
\begin{equation}
\xymatrix{
  \mathrm{T}' \ar[r]^-{\partial\otimes 1} \ar[d]^{\varphi_1} & \mathrm{R}'\otimes_{\mathrm{A}'}\mathrm{N}' \ar[r] \ar[d]^{v\otimes 1} & \mathrm{L}'_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}'}\mathrm{N}' \ar[r] \ar[d]^{u_0\otimes 1} & \mathrm{M}'\otimes_{\mathrm{A}'}\mathrm{N}' \ar[r] \ar@{=}[d] & 0 \\
  0 \ar[r] & \mathrm{T}'' \ar[r]^-{\partial''} & \mathrm{R}''\otimes_{\mathrm{A}'}\mathrm{N}' \ar[r] & \mathrm{L}''_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}'}\mathrm{N}' \ar[r] & \mathrm{M}'\otimes_{\mathrm{A}'}\mathrm{N}' \ar[r] & 0
}
  \tag{11.2.3.3}
\end{equation}
is \emph{commutative}. For this, one verifies immediately $(\mathrm{M},~\mathrm{IV},~1)$ that the homomorphism $\partial:\mathrm{T}\to\mathrm{R}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{N}$ comes (in the present case) by passage to the quotient from the homomorphism $w:\operatorname{Ker}(f_0\otimes 1_\mathrm{N})\to\mathrm{R}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{N}$, restriction of the homomorphism $g_0\otimes 1_\mathrm{N}:\mathrm{L}_1\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{N}\to\mathrm{R}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{N}$, where $g_0:\mathrm{L}_1\to\mathrm{R}$ is such that $f_0=j\circ g_0$; similarly for $\partial''$. It therefore suffices to see that the diagram
\[
\xymatrix{
  \operatorname{Ker}(f_0\otimes 1_\mathrm{N}) \ar[r]^{w} \ar[d] & \mathrm{R}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{N} \ar[r] & \mathrm{R}'\otimes_{\mathrm{A}'}\mathrm{N}'=(\mathrm{R}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{N})\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}' \ar[d] \\
  \operatorname{Ker}(f''_0\otimes 1_{\mathrm{N}'}) \ar[rr] & & \mathrm{R}''\otimes_{\mathrm{A}'}\mathrm{N}'
}
\]
is commutative, which follows from the commutativity of the diagram
\[
\xymatrix{
  \mathrm{L}_1 \ar[r] & \mathrm{R} \\
  \mathrm{L}''_1 \ar[r] & \mathrm{R}''
}
\]

\begin{lemma}[11.2.4]
\label{IV.11.2.4}
Let $\mathrm{A}$ be a ring, $\mathrm{C}$ an $\mathrm{A}$-algebra, $\mathrm{M}$ an $\mathrm{A}$-module, $\mathrm{N}$ a $\mathrm{C}$-module, $\mathrm{A}'$ an $\mathrm{A}$-algebra. Set $\mathrm{C}'=\mathrm{C}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'$, $\mathrm{M}'=\mathrm{M}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'$, $\mathrm{N}'=\mathrm{N}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'=\mathrm{N}\otimes_\mathrm{C}\mathrm{C}'$. Suppose that $\mathrm{M}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{N}$ is a flat $\mathrm{C}$-module. Then the canonical homomorphism
\[
  \varphi_1:\operatorname{Tor}_1^\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{M},\mathrm{N})\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'\to\operatorname{Tor}_1^{\mathrm{A}'}(\mathrm{M}',\mathrm{N}')
\]
(cf.\ \sref{IV.11.2.1}[IV.11.2.1.5]) \emph{is surjective}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Let us keep the notations of \sref{IV.11.2.3}; the exactness on the right of the tensor product, applied to the hypothesis that $\mathrm{M}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{N}$ is a flat C-module, shows that the sequence $\mathrm{L}'_1\xrightarrow{f'_0}\mathrm{L}'_0\xrightarrow{\varepsilon'}\mathrm{M}'\to 0$ is exact; as $\mathrm{L}'_0$ and $\mathrm{L}'_1$ are free $\mathrm{A}'$-modules, \emph{one may suppose that $\mathrm{L}''_0=\mathrm{L}'_0$, $\mathrm{L}''_1=\mathrm{L}'_1$}, $u_0$ and $u_1$ being the identity maps, and $f''_0=f'_0$. As $\mathrm{R}=\operatorname{Im}(f_0)$ and $\mathrm{R}''=\operatorname{Im}(f''_0)=\operatorname{Im}(f'_0)$, the homomorphism $v$ is surjective, and so it follows similarly for $v\otimes 1$. The proof will therefore be complete if one proves that the first line of \sref{IV.11.2.3}[IV.11.2.3.3] is \emph{exact}, $v\otimes 1$ being surjective and $u_0\otimes 1$ injective (Bourbaki, \emph{Alg.\ comm.}, chap.~I\textsuperscript{er}, n\textsuperscript{o}\,4, cor.~2 de la prop.~2). Let us use for this
\end{proof}

\oldpage[IV-3]{123}

the hypothesis that $\mathrm{M}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{N}$ is a flat C-module. Setting $\mathrm{Q}=\operatorname{Ker}(\varepsilon\otimes 1)=\operatorname{Im}(j\otimes 1)$, by the exact sequence \sref{IV.11.2.3}[IV.11.2.3.1], one has the two exact sequences $0\to\mathrm{Q}\to\mathrm{L}_0\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{N}\to\mathrm{M}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{N}\to 0$ and $\mathrm{T}\to\mathrm{R}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{N}\to\mathrm{Q}\to 0$, where the homomorphisms are homomorphisms of \emph{C-modules}; using the hypothesis of flatness, one deduces $(\mathbf{0}_\mathrm{I},~6.1.2)$ the exact sequence
\[
  0\to\mathrm{Q}\otimes_\mathrm{C}\mathrm{C}'\to(\mathrm{L}_0\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{N})\otimes_\mathrm{C}\mathrm{C}'\to(\mathrm{M}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{N})\otimes_\mathrm{C}\mathrm{C}'\to 0
\]
and on the other hand, the tensor product being right exact, one has the exact sequence
\[
  \mathrm{T}\otimes_\mathrm{C}\mathrm{C}'\to(\mathrm{R}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{N})\otimes_\mathrm{C}\mathrm{C}'\to\mathrm{Q}\otimes_\mathrm{C}\mathrm{C}'\to 0
\]
whence finally the exact sequence
\[
  \mathrm{T}\otimes_\mathrm{C}\mathrm{C}'\to(\mathrm{R}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{N})\otimes_\mathrm{C}\mathrm{C}'\to(\mathrm{L}_0\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{N})\otimes_\mathrm{C}\mathrm{C}'\to(\mathrm{M}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{N})\otimes_\mathrm{C}\mathrm{C}'\to 0
\]
But by definition, for every C-module $\mathrm{P}$, $\mathrm{P}\otimes_\mathrm{C}\mathrm{C}'=\mathrm{P}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'$, whence the conclusion.

\begin{lemma}[11.2.5]
\label{IV.11.2.5}
Let $\mathrm{A}$ be a ring, $\mathfrak{J}$ an ideal of $\mathrm{A}$, $\mathrm{B}$ an $\mathrm{A}$-algebra, $\mathrm{M}$ a $\mathrm{B}$-module, $\mathrm{A}\to\mathrm{A}'$ a homomorphism of rings. Set $\mathfrak{J}'=\mathfrak{J}\mathrm{A}'$, $\mathrm{B}'=\mathrm{B}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'$, $\mathrm{M}'=\mathrm{M}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'=\mathrm{M}\otimes_\mathrm{B}\mathrm{B}'$. Suppose verified one of the following hypotheses:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\emph{\alph*)}]
  \item $\mathfrak{J}$ is nilpotent.
  \item $\mathrm{A}'$ is Noetherian, $\mathrm{B}'$ is an $\mathrm{A}'$-algebra of finite type, $\mathrm{M}'$ a $\mathrm{B}'$-module of finite type.
\end{enumerate}
Under these conditions, suppose the two following properties are verified:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\emph{(\roman*)}]
  \item $\mathrm{M}\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J})$ is a flat $(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J})$-module.
  \item The canonical composite homomorphism
\[
  \operatorname{Tor}_1^\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{M},\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J})\xrightarrow{\psi_1}\operatorname{Tor}_1^{\mathrm{A}'}(\mathrm{M}',\mathrm{A}'/\mathfrak{J}')\xrightarrow{\theta}(\operatorname{Tor}_1^{\mathrm{A}'}(\mathrm{M}',\mathrm{A}'/\mathfrak{J}'))_{\mathfrak{p}'}
\]
\end{enumerate}
(where $\psi_1$ is the homomorphism \sref{IV.11.2.1}[IV.11.2.1.1] and $\theta$ the canonical homomorphism of a $\mathrm{B}'$-module localised at $\mathfrak{p}'$) is zero.

Then $\mathrm{M}'_{\mathfrak{p}'}$ is a flat $\mathrm{A}'$-module.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Note that under hypothesis $b)$, $\mathrm{M}'_{\mathfrak{p}'}$ is a $\mathrm{B}'_{\mathfrak{p}'}$-module of finite type, $\mathrm{B}'_{\mathfrak{p}'}$ is an $\mathrm{A}'$-algebra Noetherian, and $\mathfrak{J}'\mathrm{B}'_{\mathfrak{p}'}$ is contained in the radical $\mathfrak{p}'\mathrm{B}'_{\mathfrak{p}'}$ of $\mathrm{B}'_{\mathfrak{p}'}$; under hypothesis $a)$, $\mathfrak{J}'$ is nilpotent; one can therefore apply the criterion of flatness $(\mathbf{0}_{\mathrm{III}},~10.2.1)$ or $(\mathbf{0}_{\mathrm{III}},~10.2.2)$ according to whether $a)$ or $b)$ is verified. In the first place, $\mathrm{M}'_{\mathfrak{p}'}\otimes_{\mathrm{A}'}(\mathrm{A}'/\mathfrak{J}')=(\mathrm{M}'\otimes_{\mathrm{A}'}(\mathrm{A}'/\mathfrak{J}'))_{\mathfrak{p}'}=((\mathrm{M}\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J}))\otimes_{\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J}}(\mathrm{A}'/\mathfrak{J}'))_{\mathfrak{p}'}$; hypothesis~(i) implies that $\mathrm{M}'_{\mathfrak{p}'}\otimes_{\mathrm{A}'}(\mathrm{A}'/\mathfrak{J}')$ is a flat $(\mathrm{A}'/\mathfrak{J}')$-module, by $(\mathbf{0}_\mathrm{I},~6.2.1$ and $6.3.2)$. It remains therefore to show that $\operatorname{Tor}_1^{\mathrm{A}'}(\mathrm{M}'_{\mathfrak{p}'},\mathrm{A}'/\mathfrak{J}')=0$; now this module is equal to $(\operatorname{Tor}_1^{\mathrm{A}'}(\mathrm{M}',\mathrm{A}'/\mathfrak{J}'))_{\mathfrak{p}'}$ by virtue of the flatness of $\mathrm{B}'_{\mathfrak{p}'}$ over $\mathrm{B}'$. But by hypothesis~(ii), the homomorphism composed $\theta\circ\psi_1:\operatorname{Tor}_1^\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{M},\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J})\to(\operatorname{Tor}_1^{\mathrm{A}'}(\mathrm{M}',\mathrm{A}'/\mathfrak{J}'))_{\mathfrak{p}'}$ is zero; moreover, by \sref{IV.11.2.4}, applied to $\mathrm{C}=\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J}$ and $\mathrm{N}=\mathrm{C}$, \sref{IV.11.2.4}[IV.11.2.4] shows (taking into account hypothesis~(i)) that $\varphi_1$ is \emph{surjective}; hence the homomorphism $\theta$ is zero, and therefore the image of $\theta$ in the $\mathrm{B}'_{\mathfrak{p}'}$-module $(\operatorname{Tor}_1^{\mathrm{A}'}(\mathrm{M}',\mathrm{A}'/\mathfrak{J}'))_{\mathfrak{p}'}$, which is this last module, is zero.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[11.2.6]
\label{IV.11.2.6}
The notations being those of $(8.5.1)$ and $(8.8.1)$, suppose $\mathrm{S}_\alpha$ quasi-compact, $\mathrm{X}_\alpha$ and $\mathrm{Y}_\alpha$ of finite presentation over $\mathrm{S}_\alpha$; let $f_\alpha:\mathrm{X}_\alpha\to\mathrm{Y}_\alpha$ be an $\mathrm{S}_\alpha$-morphism, $\sh{F}_\alpha$ an $\sh{O}_{\mathrm{X}_\alpha}$-Module quasi-coherent of finite presentation.
\end{theorem}

\oldpage[IV-3]{124}

\begin{enumerate}[label=\emph{(\roman*)}]
  \item \emph{Let $x$ be a point of $\mathrm{X}$, $x_\lambda$ its canonical projection in $\mathrm{X}_\lambda$. For $\sh{F}$ to be $f$-flat at the point $x$, it is necessary and sufficient that there exist $\lambda\geqslant\alpha$ such that $\sh{F}_\lambda$ is $f_\lambda$-flat at the point $x_\lambda$.}
  \item \emph{For $\sh{F}$ to be $f$-flat, it is necessary and sufficient that there exist $\lambda\geqslant\alpha$ such that $\sh{F}_\lambda$ is $f_\lambda$-flat.}
\end{enumerate}

\begin{proof}
One may suppose that $\mathrm{S}_\alpha=\mathrm{S}_0$; as $\mathrm{Y}_0$ is of finite presentation over $\mathrm{S}_0$, $\mathrm{Y}_0$ is quasi-compact, and $f_0:\mathrm{X}_0\to\mathrm{Y}_0$ is a morphism of finite presentation $(1.6.2,~(\mathrm{v}))$, hence one can also restrict to the case where $\mathrm{S}_0=\mathrm{Y}_0$. Moreover, by the quasi-compactness of $\mathrm{S}_0$ and of the fact that the set of indices $\mathrm{L}$ is filtered, one can restrict by the same reasoning to the case where $\mathrm{S}_0=\Spec(\mathrm{A}_0)$ is affine. Furthermore $\mathrm{X}_0$ is also quasi-compact, hence by the same reasoning one can also suppose $\mathrm{X}_0=\Spec(\mathrm{B}_0)$ affine; one then has $\sh{F}_0=\widetilde{\mathrm{M}}_0$, where $\mathrm{M}_0$ is a $\mathrm{B}_0$-module of finite presentation, and the statement \sref{IV.11.2.6} is in this case equivalent to the following (by $(\mathbf{0}_\mathrm{I},~6.3.1)$):

\begin{corollary}[11.2.6.1]
\label{IV.11.2.6.1}
Let $\mathrm{A}_0$ be a ring, $(\mathrm{A}_\lambda)_{\lambda\in\mathrm{L}}$ an inductive filtrant system of $\mathrm{A}_0$-algebras, $\mathrm{B}_0$ an $\mathrm{A}_0$-algebra of finite presentation, $\mathrm{M}_0$ a $\mathrm{B}_0$-module of finite presentation. Set $\mathrm{B}_\lambda=\mathrm{B}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}_\lambda$, $\mathrm{M}_\lambda=\mathrm{M}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}_\lambda$, $\mathrm{A}=\varinjlim\mathrm{A}_\lambda$, $\mathrm{B}=\varinjlim\mathrm{B}_\lambda=\mathrm{B}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}$, $\mathrm{M}=\varinjlim\mathrm{M}_\lambda=\mathrm{M}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\emph{(\roman*)}]
  \item \emph{Let $\mathfrak{p}$ be a prime ideal of $\mathrm{B}$, and for every $\lambda$, let $\mathfrak{p}_\lambda$ be its inverse image in $\mathrm{B}_\lambda$. For $\mathrm{M}_\mathfrak{p}$ to be a flat $\mathrm{A}$-module, it is necessary and sufficient that there exist $\lambda$ such that $(\mathrm{M}_\lambda)_{\mathfrak{p}_\lambda}$ be a flat $\mathrm{A}_\lambda$-module.}
  \item \emph{For $\mathrm{M}$ to be a flat $\mathrm{A}$-module, it is necessary and sufficient that there exist $\lambda$ such that $\mathrm{M}_\lambda$ be a flat $\mathrm{A}_\lambda$-module.}
\end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}

One needs only prove that the conditions are necessary $(2.1.4)$. We will proceed in several steps.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{I)} \emph{Reduction to the case where the $\mathrm{A}_\lambda$ are Noetherian.} --- By virtue of $(8.9.1)$, there exists a subring $\mathrm{A}'_0$ of $\mathrm{A}_0$ that is a $\mathbf{Z}$-algebra of finite type, and a $\mathrm{A}'_0$-algebra of finite type $\mathrm{B}'_0$ and a $\mathrm{B}'_0$-module of finite type $\mathrm{M}'_0$ such that $\mathrm{B}_0=\mathrm{B}'_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}'_0}\mathrm{A}_0$ and $\mathrm{M}_0=\mathrm{M}'_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}'_0}\mathrm{A}_0$; as $\mathrm{B}_\lambda=\mathrm{B}'_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}'_0}\mathrm{A}_\lambda$ and $\mathrm{M}_\lambda=\mathrm{M}'_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}'_0}\mathrm{A}_\lambda$, one can replace $\mathrm{A}_0$, $\mathrm{B}_0$, $\mathrm{M}_0$ by $\mathrm{A}'_0$, $\mathrm{B}'_0$, $\mathrm{M}'_0$, and hence one may already suppose that $\mathrm{A}_0$ is \emph{Noetherian}. Let $\mathrm{H}$ be the set of couples $(\lambda,\mathrm{C}_\lambda)$, where $\mathrm{C}_\lambda$ is a sub-$\mathrm{A}_0$-algebra of \emph{finite type} of $\mathrm{A}_\lambda$; order $\mathrm{H}$ by setting $(\lambda,\mathrm{C}_\lambda)\leqslant(\mu,\mathrm{D}_\mu)$ if $\lambda\leqslant\mu$ and $\varphi_{\mu\lambda}(\mathrm{C}_\lambda)\subset\mathrm{D}_\mu$; for this order $\mathrm{H}$ is filtered, since if $(\lambda,\mathrm{C}_\lambda)$ and $(\mu,\mathrm{D}_\mu)$ are any two elements of $\mathrm{H}$, one can majorise them by a $(\nu,\mathrm{E}_\nu)$ in $\mathrm{L}$, taking $\nu\geqslant\lambda$, $\nu\geqslant\mu$ and then $\mathrm{E}_\nu$ equal to the sub-$\mathrm{A}_0$-algebra of $\mathrm{A}_\nu$ generated by $\varphi_{\nu\lambda}(\mathrm{C}_\lambda)$ and $\varphi_{\nu\mu}(\mathrm{D}_\mu)$. For an element $\xi=(\lambda,\mathrm{C}_\lambda)$ of $\mathrm{H}$, set $\mathrm{A}_\xi=\mathrm{C}_\lambda$ and if $\eta=(\mu,\mathrm{D}_\mu)$ (hence $\lambda\leqslant\mu$ and $\varphi_{\mu\lambda}(\mathrm{C}_\lambda)\subset\mathrm{D}_\mu$), $\varphi_{\eta\xi}:\mathrm{A}_\xi\to\mathrm{A}_\eta$ will be the restriction to $\mathrm{C}_\lambda$ of $\varphi_{\mu\lambda}$, considered as a homomorphism to $\mathrm{D}_\mu$). One thus obtains an inductive filtrant system of $\mathrm{A}_0$-algebras. Set $\mathrm{B}_\xi=\mathrm{B}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}_\xi$, $\mathrm{M}_\xi=\mathrm{M}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}_\xi$; this being so, the $\mathrm{A}_\xi$ are \emph{Noetherian}; moreover, by the double limit inductive formula (Bourbaki, \emph{Alg.}, chap.~II, 3\textsuperscript{e}~\'{e}d., \textsection\,6, n\textsuperscript{o}\,4, prop.~7) one has $\varinjlim_{\mathrm{H}}\mathrm{A}_\xi=\mathrm{A}$, $\varinjlim_{\mathrm{H}}\mathrm{B}_\xi=\mathrm{B}$, $\varinjlim_{\mathrm{H}}\mathrm{M}_\xi=\mathrm{M}$. Suppose \sref{IV.11.2.6.1} proved for the inductive system $(\mathrm{A}_\xi)_{\xi\in\mathrm{H}}$; let $\mathfrak{p}$ be a prime ideal of $\mathrm{B}$, tel that $\mathrm{M}_\mathfrak{p}$ be a flat $\mathrm{A}$-module; there exists then $\xi\in\mathrm{H}$ such that
\oldpage[IV-3]{125}
$(\mathrm{M}_\xi)_{\mathfrak{p}_\xi}$ be a flat $\mathrm{A}_\xi$-module. Let $\xi=(\lambda,\mathrm{C}_\lambda)$, and as $\mathfrak{p}_\lambda$ is the inverse image of $\mathfrak{p}$ in $\mathrm{B}_\lambda$, $\mathfrak{p}_\xi$ is the inverse image of $\mathfrak{p}_\lambda$ in $\mathrm{B}_\xi$; $(\mathrm{M}_\xi)_{\mathfrak{p}_\xi}=((\mathrm{M}_\xi\otimes_{\mathrm{C}_\lambda}\mathrm{A}_\lambda)_{\mathfrak{p}_\lambda}=((\mathrm{M}_\xi)_{\mathfrak{p}_\xi}\otimes_{\mathrm{C}_\lambda}\mathrm{A}_\lambda)_{\mathfrak{p}_\lambda}$, hence $(\mathrm{M}_\lambda)_{\mathfrak{p}_\lambda}$ is a flat $\mathrm{A}_\lambda$-module, by $(\mathbf{0}_\mathrm{I},~6.2.1$ and $6.3.2)$. One thus treats case (ii) in a similar manner. We can therefore from now on suppose the $\mathrm{A}_\lambda$ Noetherian for $\lambda\in\mathrm{L}$ (but not necessarily $\mathrm{A}$ itself).

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{II)} \emph{Reduction of the global statement (ii) to the pointwise statement (i).} --- Suppose $\sh{F}$ is $f$-flat. For every $\lambda$, let $\mathrm{U}_\lambda$ be the set of $x_\lambda\in\mathrm{X}_\lambda$ such that $\sh{F}_\lambda$ is $f_\lambda$-flat at the point $x_\lambda$; we know that $\mathrm{U}_\lambda$ is open in $\mathrm{X}_\lambda$, since $\mathrm{S}_\lambda$ is Noetherian and $f_\lambda$ is of finite type \sref{IV.11.1.1}; let $\mathrm{V}_\lambda=v_\lambda^{-1}(\mathrm{U}_\lambda)$ its inverse image in $\mathrm{X}$. By hypothesis, for every $x\in\mathrm{X}$, the projection of $x$ in $\mathrm{X}_\lambda$ belongs to $\mathrm{U}_\lambda$ for some $\lambda$, which means that $\mathrm{X}=\bigcup\mathrm{V}_\lambda$. Moreover, by $(2.1.4)$, for $\lambda\leqslant\mu$, $\mathrm{V}_\lambda\subset\mathrm{V}_\mu$, hence, since $\mathrm{X}$ is quasi-compact, there exists an index $\mu$ such that $\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{V}_\mu$. As the $\mathrm{X}_\lambda$ are quasi-compact, it follows from $(8.3.4)$ that there exists a $\nu\geqslant\mu$ such that $v_{\nu\mu}^{-1}(\mathrm{U}_\mu)=\mathrm{X}_\nu$; but by $(2.1.4)$, this implies that $\sh{F}_\nu$ is $f_\nu$-flat.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{III)} \emph{End of the proof.} --- It remains to prove (i) supposing $\mathrm{S}_0$ affine and Noetherian; if $y_0$ is the projection of $x$ in $\mathrm{S}_0$, one can, moreover, by virtue of $(2.1.4)$ and de $(\mathbf{I},~3.6.5)$, otherwise restrict to the case where $\mathrm{A}_0$ is a \emph{local} Noetherian ring, with maximal ideal $\mathfrak{m}_0=\mathfrak{i}_{y_0}$; by definition, $\mathfrak{m}_0$ is the inverse image of the prime ideal $\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{i}_x$ of $\mathrm{B}$, and $\mathrm{M}_\mathfrak{p}$ is supposed to be a flat A-module; one therefore has in particular $\operatorname{Tor}_1^\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{m}_0\mathrm{A},\mathrm{M})=0$, which can also be written $\operatorname{Tor}_1^\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{m}_0\mathrm{A},\mathrm{M})_\mathfrak{p}=0$.
Note now that $\operatorname{Tor}_1^{\mathrm{A}_\lambda}(\mathrm{A}_\lambda/\mathfrak{m}_0\mathrm{A}_\lambda,\mathrm{M}_\lambda)$ are B-modules of finite type, and the $\operatorname{Tor}_1^{\mathrm{A}_\lambda}(\mathrm{A}_\lambda/\mathfrak{m}_0,\mathrm{M}_0)$ is a $\mathrm{B}_0$-module \emph{of finite type}, since $\mathrm{B}_0$ is Noetherian; by $(11.2.2.1)$ $\operatorname{Tor}_1^\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{m}_0\mathrm{A},\mathrm{M})=\varinjlim\operatorname{Tor}_1^{\mathrm{A}_\lambda}(\mathrm{A}_\lambda/\mathfrak{m}_0\mathrm{A}_\lambda,\mathrm{M}_\lambda)$; there exists then $\lambda$ such that the images of $t_i^0$ in $\operatorname{Tor}_1^{\mathrm{A}_\lambda}(\mathrm{A}_\lambda/\mathfrak{m}_0\mathrm{A}_\lambda,\mathrm{M}_\lambda)$, are zero, i.e., $h_\lambda t_i^0=0$ for $1\leqslant i\leqslant m$. Or, on $h_\lambda\in\mathrm{B}_\lambda-\mathfrak{p}_\lambda$ the prime ideal of $\mathrm{B}_\lambda$ inverse image of $\mathfrak{p}$; hence the canonical images of $t_i^0$ in $(\operatorname{Tor}_1^{\mathrm{A}_\lambda}(\mathrm{A}_\lambda/\mathfrak{m}_0\mathrm{A}_\lambda,\mathrm{M}_\lambda))_{\mathfrak{p}_\lambda}$ are zero, and hence by the homomorphism $\operatorname{Tor}_1^{\mathrm{A}_0}(\mathrm{A}_0/\mathfrak{m}_0,\mathrm{M}_0)\to(\operatorname{Tor}_1^{\mathrm{A}_\lambda}(\mathrm{A}_\lambda/\mathfrak{m}_0\mathrm{A}_\lambda,\mathrm{M}_\lambda))_{\mathfrak{p}_\lambda}$ is zero. The conditions of the lemma \sref{IV.11.2.5} are therefore satisfied ($\mathrm{A}_0/\mathfrak{m}_0$ being a field, hence the condition \sref{IV.11.2.5}[(i)] is trivially verified) and $(\mathrm{M}_\lambda)_{\mathfrak{p}_\lambda}$ is a flat $\mathrm{A}_\lambda$-module, which completes the proof of the theorem.

\begin{corollary}[11.2.7]
\label{IV.11.2.7}
Let $\mathrm{S}=\Spec(\mathrm{A})$ be an affine scheme, $f:\mathrm{X}\to\mathrm{S}$ a morphism, $\sh{F}$ an $\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}$-Module quasi-coherent, $x$ a point of $\mathrm{X}$. The following conditions are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\emph{\alph*)}]
  \item $f$ is a morphism of finite presentation, $\sh{F}$ is an $\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}$-Module of finite presentation and $\sh{F}$ is $f$-flat at the point $x$ (resp.\ $f$-flat).
\oldpage[IV-3]{126}
  \item There exists an affine Noetherian scheme $\mathrm{S}_0=\Spec(\mathrm{A}_0)$, a morphism of finite type $f_0:\mathrm{X}_0\to\mathrm{S}_0$, an $\sh{O}_{\mathrm{X}_0}$-Module coherent $\sh{F}_0$, a morphism $\mathrm{S}\to\mathrm{S}_0$ such that the S-prescheme $\mathrm{X}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{S}_0}\mathrm{S}$ is S-isomorphic to $\mathrm{X}$ and that $\sh{F}$ is isomorphic to $\sh{F}_0\otimes_{\sh{O}_{\mathrm{S}_0}}\sh{O}_\mathrm{S}$, and $\sh{F}_0$ is $f_0$-flat at the point $x_0$ the projection of $x$ in $\mathrm{X}_0$ (resp.\ $f_0$-flat).
  \item The conditions of $b)$ are satisfied and in addition $\mathrm{A}_0$ is a sub-$\mathbf{Z}$-algebra of finite type of $\mathrm{A}$, the morphism $\mathrm{S}\to\mathrm{S}_0$ corresponding to the canonical injection $\mathrm{A}_0\to\mathrm{A}$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
It is clear that $c)$ implies $b)$ and $b)$ implies $a)$ by virtue of $(2.1.4)$. On the other hand, one can consider $\mathrm{A}$ as the inductive limit of its sub-$\mathbf{Z}$-algebras of finite type, and by $(8.9.1)$ we know that there is a sub-$\mathbf{Z}$-algebra $\mathrm{A}_0$ and a morphism of finite type $f_0:\mathrm{X}_0\to\mathrm{S}_0$ with $\sh{F}_0$ an $\sh{O}_{\mathrm{X}_0}$-Module coherent such that $\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{X}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}$, $\sh{F}=v^*_\lambda(\sh{F}_\lambda)$, and $\sh{F}_\lambda$ is $f_\lambda$-flat; this can be verified via \sref{IV.11.2.6}, and the sub-ring $\mathrm{A}_0$ of $\mathrm{A}$ containing $\mathrm{A}_0$, and $v_\lambda:\mathrm{X}\to\mathrm{X}_\lambda$ is the canonical projection, and $\sh{F}_\lambda=\sh{F}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}_\lambda$. It follows from \sref{IV.11.2.6} that there exists $\lambda$ such that $\sh{F}_\lambda$ is $f_\lambda$-flat (resp.\ $f_\lambda$-flat at the point $x_\lambda=v_\lambda(x)$); then the sub-ring $\mathrm{A}_\lambda$ of $\mathrm{A}$ satisfies the conditions of $c)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[11.2.8]
\label{IV.11.2.8}
Let $g:\mathrm{X}\to\mathrm{S}$, $h:\mathrm{Y}\to\mathrm{S}$ be two morphisms of finite presentation, $f:\mathrm{X}\to\mathrm{Y}$ an S-morphism, $\sh{F}$ an $\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}$-Module quasi-coherent of finite presentation; for every $s\in\mathrm{S}$, let $\mathrm{X}_s=g^{-1}(s)=\mathrm{X}\times_\mathrm{S}\Spec(\boldsymbol{k}(s))$, $\mathrm{Y}_s=h^{-1}(s)=\mathrm{Y}\times_\mathrm{S}\Spec(\boldsymbol{k}(s))$, $f_s$ the morphism $f\times 1:\mathrm{X}_s\to\mathrm{Y}_s$, $\sh{F}_s=\sh{F}\otimes_{\sh{O}_\mathrm{S}}\boldsymbol{k}(s)$. Then the set $\mathrm{E}$ of $s\in\mathrm{S}$ such that $\sh{F}_s$ is $f_s$-flat is locally constructible.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The property that we want to prove satisfies condition $(9.2.1,~(\mathrm{i}))$, by virtue of $(2.2.13)$ and $(2.5.1)$. Taking into account $(9.2.3)$, one can therefore restrict to the case where $\mathrm{S}$ is affine, Noetherian, and integral with generic point $\eta$. If $\eta\in\mathrm{S}-\mathrm{E}$, this follows immediately from the lemma $(9.4.7.1)$. If on the contrary $\eta\in\mathrm{E}$, it follows immediately from \sref{IV.11.2.6}, that there is a neighbourhood open $\mathrm{U}$ of $\eta$ in $\mathrm{S}$ such that $\sh{F}|\mathrm{U}$ is $h^{-1}(\mathrm{U})$-flat; \emph{a fortiori} $(2.1.4)$ $\sh{F}_s$ is $f_s$-flat for every $s\in\mathrm{U}$, which completes the proof.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[11.2.9]
\label{IV.11.2.9}
(Raynaud). --- Let $\mathrm{A}_0$ be a ring, $(\mathrm{A}_\lambda)_{\lambda\in\mathrm{L}}$ an inductive filtrant system of $\mathrm{A}_0$-algebras, $\mathrm{B}_0$ an $\mathrm{A}_0$-algebra of finite presentation, $\mathfrak{J}_0$ an ideal of finite type of $\mathrm{B}_0$, $\mathrm{M}_0$ a $\mathrm{B}_0$-module of finite presentation. Set $\mathrm{B}_\lambda=\mathrm{B}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}_\lambda$, $\mathfrak{J}_\lambda=\mathfrak{J}_0\mathrm{B}_\lambda$, $\mathrm{M}_\lambda=\mathrm{M}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}_\lambda=\mathrm{M}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{B}_0}\mathrm{B}_\lambda$, $\mathrm{A}=\varinjlim\mathrm{A}_\lambda$, $\mathrm{B}=\varinjlim\mathrm{B}_\lambda=\mathrm{B}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}$, $\mathfrak{J}=\mathfrak{J}_0\mathrm{B}$, $\mathrm{M}=\varinjlim\mathrm{M}_\lambda=\mathrm{M}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}=\mathrm{M}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{B}_0}\mathrm{B}$. For $\operatorname{gr}^*_\mathfrak{J}(\mathrm{M})$ to be a flat $\mathrm{A}$-module, it is necessary and sufficient that there exist $\lambda$ such that $\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}_\lambda}(\mathrm{M}_\lambda)$ be a flat $\mathrm{A}_\lambda$-module; the canonical homomorphisms
\begin{equation}
  \begin{cases}
    \operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}_\lambda}(\mathrm{M}_\lambda)\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_\lambda}\mathrm{A} \to \operatorname{gr}^*_\mathfrak{J}(\mathrm{M}) \\
    \operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}_\lambda}(\mathrm{M}_\lambda)\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_\lambda}\mathrm{A}_\mu \to \operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}_\mu}(\mathrm{M}_\mu)
  \end{cases}
  \qquad\text{for }\lambda\leqslant\mu
  \tag{11.2.9.1}
\end{equation}
are then bijective and $\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}_\mu}(\mathrm{M}_\mu)$ is a flat $\mathrm{A}_\mu$-module for $\lambda\leqslant\mu$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Let us first show that the conditions are sufficient, which amounts to proving the bijectivity of \sref{IV.11.2.9}[IV.11.2.9.1]. This follows from the following lemma:

\begin{lemma}[11.2.9.2]
\label{IV.11.2.9.2}
Let $\mathrm{A}$ be a ring, $\mathrm{B}$ an $\mathrm{A}$-algebra, $\mathrm{M}$ a $\mathrm{B}$-module, $\mathfrak{J}$ an ideal of $\mathrm{B}$. Let $\mathrm{A}'$ be an $\mathrm{A}$-algebra; set $\mathrm{B}'=\mathrm{B}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'$, $\mathrm{M}'=\mathrm{M}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'=\mathrm{M}\otimes_\mathrm{B}\mathrm{B}'$, $\mathfrak{J}'=\mathfrak{J}\mathrm{B}'$. If $\operatorname{gr}^*_\mathfrak{J}(\mathrm{M})$ is a flat $\mathrm{A}$-module, the canonical homomorphism
\[
  (\operatorname{gr}^*_\mathfrak{J}(\mathrm{M}))\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'\to\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}'}(\mathrm{M}')
\]
\emph{is bijective.}
\end{lemma}

\oldpage[IV-3]{127}

Indeed, by induction on $k$, the hypothesis that the $\mathfrak{J}^k\mathrm{M}/\mathfrak{J}^{k+1}\mathrm{M}$ are flat A-modules implies first, by $(\mathbf{0}_\mathrm{I},~6.1.2)$ that $\mathrm{M}/\mathfrak{J}^{k+1}\mathrm{M}$ is a flat A-module; moreover $(\mathbf{0}_\mathrm{I},~6.1.2)$, the sequence
\[
  0\to(\mathfrak{J}^{k+1}\mathrm{M})\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'\to\mathrm{M}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'\to(\mathrm{M}/\mathfrak{J}^{k+1}\mathrm{M})\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'\to 0
\]
is then exact, in other words $(\mathfrak{J}^{k+1}\mathrm{M})\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'$ identifies with its canonical image $\mathfrak{J}'^{k+1}\mathrm{M}'$ in $\mathrm{M}'=\mathrm{M}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'$. On the other hand, always by $(\mathbf{0}_\mathrm{I},~6.1.2)$, the sequence
\[
  0\to(\mathfrak{J}^{k+1}\mathrm{M})\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'\to(\mathfrak{J}^k\mathrm{M})\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'\to(\mathfrak{J}^k\mathrm{M}/\mathfrak{J}^{k+1}\mathrm{M})\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'\to 0
\]
is exact, which proves the lemma.

To prove the necessity of the conditions of \sref{IV.11.2.9}, we proceed in several steps.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{I)} \emph{Reduction to the case where the $\mathrm{A}_\lambda$ are Noetherian.} --- One proceeds as in the reduction I) of \sref{IV.11.2.6.1}, keeping the notations; it suffices simply to begin by replacing $\mathrm{A}'_0$ by a sub-$\mathrm{A}'_0$-algebra $\mathrm{A}''_0$ of $\mathrm{A}_0$ such that, if one sets $\mathrm{B}''_0=\mathrm{B}'_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}'_0}\mathrm{A}''_0$, there is in $\mathrm{B}''_0$ a finitely generated ideal $\mathfrak{J}''_0$ such that $\mathfrak{J}''_0\mathrm{B}_0=\mathfrak{J}_0$. One considers then all sub-$\mathrm{A}'_0$-algebras $\mathrm{A}'_\alpha$ of $\mathrm{A}_0$ of type fini, which form a filtered family, and $\mathrm{B}_0=\varinjlim\mathrm{B}'_\alpha$, where $\mathrm{B}'_\alpha=\mathrm{B}'_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}'_0}\mathrm{A}'_\alpha$; there is therefore an index $\beta$ such that a finite system of generators of $\mathfrak{J}_0$ is formed of images in $\mathrm{B}_0$ of elements of $\mathrm{B}'_\beta$; one takes then $\mathrm{A}''_0=\mathrm{A}'_\beta$, $\mathrm{B}''_0=\mathrm{B}'_\beta$ and for $\mathfrak{J}''_0$ the ideal generated by these elements. One defines then as in $\emph{loc.\ cit.}$ the filtrant set $\mathrm{H}$ and the $\mathrm{A}_\xi$, $\mathrm{B}_\xi$, $\mathrm{M}_\xi$ for $\xi\in\mathrm{H}$; and also $\mathfrak{J}_\xi=\mathfrak{J}''_0\mathrm{B}_\xi$ for all $\xi\in\mathrm{H}$. Suppose that one has shown that there exists $\xi=(\lambda,\mathrm{C}_\lambda)$ such that $\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}_\xi}(\mathrm{M}_\xi)$ be a flat $\mathrm{C}_\lambda$-module. As $\mathrm{M}_\lambda=\mathrm{M}_\xi\otimes_{\mathrm{C}_\lambda}\mathrm{A}_\lambda$, it follows from \sref{IV.11.2.9.2} that $\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}_\lambda}(\mathrm{M}_\lambda)$ is a flat $\mathrm{A}_\lambda$-module. We can therefore from now on suppose the $\mathrm{A}_\lambda$ Noetherian for $\lambda\in\mathrm{L}$ (but not necessarily $\mathrm{A}$ itself).

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{II)} \emph{Preliminary lemmas.}

\begin{lemma}[11.2.9.3]
\label{IV.11.2.9.3}
Let $\mathrm{A}$ be a ring, $\mathrm{B}=\bigoplus_{i\geqslant 0}\mathrm{B}_i$ an A-algebra graded in non-negative degrees. Suppose that $\mathrm{B}_0$ is a local ring, and that each $\mathrm{M}_i$ is a $\mathrm{B}_0$-module of finite type. For $\mathrm{M}$ to be a flat $\mathrm{A}$-module, it is necessary and sufficient that, if $\mathfrak{q}$ is the inverse image in $\mathrm{A}$ of the maximal ideal $\mathfrak{m}$ of $\mathrm{B}_0$, $\mathrm{M}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\boldsymbol{k}(\mathfrak{q})$ be a $(\mathrm{B}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\boldsymbol{k}(\mathfrak{q}))$-module of finite type.
\end{lemma}

\oldpage[IV-3]{128}

\begin{proof}
The condition being evidently necessary, let us prove that it is sufficient. As $\mathrm{B}_0$ is a local ring, so is $\mathrm{A}$; by Nakayama's lemma applied on the $\mathrm{B}_0$-modules $\mathrm{M}_i$, if there exists a graded B-module \emph{free} of finite base and a surjective graded homomorphism of degree $0$, $u:\mathrm{L}\to\mathrm{M}$. Set $\mathrm{R}=\operatorname{Ker}(u)$, which is a B-module graded; there are then a finite number of integers $m_j$ ($1\leqslant j\leqslant r$) such that for each integer $i$, $\mathrm{R}_i$ is the kernel of a surjective homomorphism $\bigoplus_{1\leqslant j\leqslant r}\mathrm{B}_{i+m_j}\to\mathrm{M}_i$; one concludes then from the hypothesis on the $\mathrm{M}_i$ and the $\mathrm{B}_i$ that $\mathrm{R}_i$ is a $\mathrm{B}_0$-module of finite type (Bourbaki, \emph{Alg.\ comm.}, chap.~I, \textsection\,2, n\textsuperscript{o}\,8, lemma~9). To prove that $\mathrm{R}$ is a B-module of finite type, let us note that by virtue of the hypothesis of flatness and of $(\mathbf{0}_\mathrm{I},~6.1.2)$ the sequence
\[
  0\to\mathrm{R}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\boldsymbol{k}(\mathfrak{q})\to\mathrm{L}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\boldsymbol{k}(\mathfrak{q})\to\mathrm{M}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\boldsymbol{k}(\mathfrak{q})\to 0
\]
is exact, and the hypothesis implies (Bourbaki, \emph{loc.\ cit.}) that $\mathrm{R}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\boldsymbol{k}(\mathfrak{q})$ is a $(\mathrm{B}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\boldsymbol{k}(\mathfrak{q}))$-module of finite type; it therefore suffices to apply $\mathrm{R}$ the lemma \sref{IV.11.2.9.3}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[11.2.9.4]
\label{IV.11.2.9.4}
Under the hypotheses of \sref{IV.11.2.9.3}, suppose moreover that each of the $\mathrm{B}_i$ and each $\mathrm{M}_i$ is a $\mathrm{B}_0$-module of finite presentation, and that $\mathrm{M}$ is a flat $\mathrm{A}$-module. For $\mathrm{M}$ to be a $\mathrm{B}$-module of finite type, it is necessary and sufficient that $\mathrm{M}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\boldsymbol{k}(\mathfrak{q})$ be a $(\mathrm{B}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\boldsymbol{k}(\mathfrak{q}))$-module of finite presentation.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
By virtue of \sref{IV.11.2.9.3}, if the condition of \sref{IV.11.2.9.3} is verified, $\mathrm{M}$ is a B-module of finite type, hence (having therefore a finite base formed of homogeneous elements, and a surjective graded homomorphism of degree $0$), $u:\mathrm{L}\to\mathrm{M}$. Set $\mathrm{R}=\operatorname{Ker}(u)$, which is a B-module graded; as above, the $\mathrm{R}_i$ are $\mathrm{B}_0$-modules of finite type.
\end{proof}

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{III)} \emph{Reduction to the case where the homomorphisms of transitions $\varphi_{\mu\lambda}:\mathrm{A}_\lambda\to\mathrm{A}_\mu$ (for $\lambda\leqslant\mu$) are injective.} --- Let $\mathrm{A}'_\lambda$ be the image of $\mathrm{A}_\lambda$ by the canonical homomorphism $\varphi_\lambda:\mathrm{A}_\lambda\to\mathrm{A}$; it is clear that the $\mathrm{A}'_\lambda$ form an inductive system of sub-rings \emph{Noetherian} of $\mathrm{A}$, and the homomorphisms of transition $\mathrm{A}'_\lambda\to\mathrm{A}'_\mu$ (for $\lambda\leqslant\mu$) are injective. Set $\mathrm{B}'_\lambda=\mathrm{B}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}'_\lambda$, $\mathfrak{J}'_\lambda=\mathfrak{J}_0\mathrm{B}'_\lambda$, $\mathrm{M}'_\lambda=\mathrm{M}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}'_\lambda=\mathrm{M}_\lambda\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_\lambda}\mathrm{A}'_\lambda$; one has still $\mathrm{B}=\varinjlim\mathrm{B}'_\lambda$, $\mathrm{M}=\varinjlim\mathrm{M}'_\lambda$. Suppose one has proved that there exists $\lambda$ such that, for $\mu\geqslant\lambda$, $\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}'_\mu}(\mathrm{M}'_\mu)$ is a flat $\mathrm{A}'_\mu$-module. Let $a_\lambda$ be the kernel of the homomorphism $\varphi_\lambda$, which is an ideal of the Noetherian ring $\mathrm{A}_\lambda$. It follows from the definition of the inductive limit that there exists an index $\mu\geqslant\lambda$ such that $\varphi_{\mu\lambda}(a_\lambda)=0$, in other words $\varphi_{\mu\lambda}$ factorises as $\varphi_{\mu\lambda}:\mathrm{A}_\lambda\to\mathrm{A}'_\lambda\to\mathrm{A}_\mu$, and one can write $\mathrm{B}_\mu=\mathrm{B}'_\lambda\otimes_{\mathrm{A}'_\lambda}\mathrm{A}_\mu$, $\mathfrak{J}_\mu=\mathfrak{J}'_\lambda\mathrm{B}_\mu$ and $\mathrm{M}_\mu=\mathrm{M}'_\lambda\otimes_{\mathrm{A}'_\lambda}\mathrm{A}_\mu$; one deduces then from \sref{IV.11.2.9.2} that $\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}_\mu}(\mathrm{M}_\mu)$ is a flat $\mathrm{A}_\mu$-module.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{IV)} \emph{Reduction to the case where $\mathrm{B}_0=\mathrm{A}_0[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n]$ and $\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}_0}(\mathrm{B}_0)=\mathrm{B}_0$.} --- By virtue of the hypothesis, there is a system $(t_i)_{1\leqslant i\leqslant n}$ of generators of $\mathfrak{J}_0$ of finite type of the $\mathrm{A}_0$-algebra $\mathrm{B}_0$, such that the $t_i$ for $i\leqslant m$ engender the ideal $\mathfrak{J}_0$ of $\mathrm{B}_0$. Set $\mathrm{B}'_0=\mathrm{A}_0[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n]$, and let $\mathfrak{J}'_0$ be the ideal of $\mathrm{B}'_0$ generated by the $\mathrm{T}_i$ for $i\leqslant m$; then $u_0:\mathrm{B}'_0\to\mathrm{B}_0$ is an $\mathrm{A}_0$-homomorphism surjective transforming each $\mathrm{T}_i$ in $t_i$ ($1\leqslant i\leqslant n$), and such that $u_0(\mathfrak{J}'_0)=\mathfrak{J}_0$; this homomorphism allows one to consider $\mathrm{M}_0$ as a $\mathrm{B}'_0$-module of finite type. One then sets $\mathrm{B}'_\lambda=\mathrm{B}'_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}_\lambda=\mathrm{A}_\lambda[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n]$, $\mathfrak{J}'_\lambda=\mathfrak{J}'_0\mathrm{B}'_\lambda$,
\oldpage[IV-3]{129}
so that $\mathfrak{J}'$ is the ideal of $\mathrm{B}'$ generated by the $\mathrm{T}_i$ for index $i\leqslant m$; moreover, it is clear that for every integer $k\geqslant 0$, one has $\mathfrak{J}'^k\mathrm{M}=\mathfrak{J}^k\mathrm{M}$ and $\mathfrak{J}'^k_\lambda\mathrm{M}_\lambda=\mathfrak{J}^k_\lambda\mathrm{M}_\lambda$ for every $\lambda$; hence $\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}'}(\mathrm{M})=\operatorname{gr}^*_\mathfrak{J}(\mathrm{M})$ as A-module and $\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}'_\lambda}(\mathrm{M}_\lambda)=\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}_\lambda}(\mathrm{M}_\lambda)$ as $\mathrm{A}_\lambda$-module; one can therefore substitute $\mathrm{B}'$, $\mathrm{B}'_\lambda$, $\mathfrak{J}'$, $\mathfrak{J}'_\lambda$ for $\mathrm{B}$, $\mathrm{B}_\lambda$, $\mathfrak{J}$, $\mathfrak{J}_\lambda$ respectively in the proof; one will note moreover that by construction $\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}'}(\mathrm{B}')$ identifies with $\mathrm{B}'$ and $\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}'_\lambda}(\mathrm{B}'_\lambda)$ with $\mathrm{B}'_\lambda$.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{V)} \emph{Proof that $\operatorname{gr}^*_\mathfrak{J}(\mathrm{M})$ is a $\operatorname{gr}^*_\mathfrak{J}(\mathrm{B})$-module of finite presentation.} --- We thus suppose from now on $\mathrm{A}_0$ and the $\mathrm{A}_\lambda$ Noetherian, the homomorphisms of transition $\mathrm{A}_\lambda\to\mathrm{A}_\mu$ injective, $\mathrm{B}_0=\mathrm{A}_0[\mathrm{T}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{T}_n]$, $\mathfrak{J}_0$ being generated by the $\mathrm{T}_i$ for index $i\leqslant m$; the ring $\mathrm{C}_0=\operatorname{gr}^0_{\mathfrak{J}_0}(\mathrm{B}_0)$ identifies to $\mathrm{B}_0$ and $\mathrm{C}=\operatorname{gr}^*_\mathfrak{J}(\mathrm{B})$ identifies to $\mathrm{B}$; we will use only in the sequel the fact that $\mathrm{C}\cong\mathrm{C}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}$.

We will need the following variant of $(6.9.3)$:

\begin{lemma}[11.2.9.5]
\label{IV.11.2.9.5}
Let $\mathrm{A}_0$ be a Noetherian ring, $\mathrm{B}_0$ an $\mathrm{A}_0$-algebra of finite type, $\mathfrak{J}_0$ an ideal of $\mathrm{B}_0$, $\mathrm{M}_0$ a $\mathrm{B}_0$-module of finite type. There exists a sequence $(\mathrm{S}_{0i})_{1\leqslant i\leqslant m}$ of sub-preschemes of $\mathrm{S}_0=\Spec(\mathrm{A}_0)$ having the following properties:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\arabic{enumi}\textsuperscript{o}]
  \item The underlying spaces of the $\mathrm{S}_{0i}$ are pairwise disjoint and form a covering of $\mathrm{S}_0$.
  \item For each $i$, the set $\mathrm{S}_{0i}$ is open in $\bigcup_{j\geqslant i}\mathrm{S}_{0j}$.
  \item Each scheme $\mathrm{S}_{0i}$ is affine.
  \item If $\mathrm{A}_{0i}$ is the ring of $\mathrm{S}_{0i}$ and one sets $\mathrm{B}_{0i}=\mathrm{B}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}_{0i}$, $\mathfrak{J}_{0i}=\mathfrak{J}_0\mathrm{B}_{0i}$, then $\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}_{0i}}(\mathrm{M}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}_{0i})$ is a flat $\mathrm{A}_{0i}$-module.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
One proceeds as in $(6.9.3)$ by Noetherian induction, supposing the lemma is true when $\mathrm{A}_0$ is replaced by $\mathrm{A}'_0=\mathrm{A}_0/a_0$, where $a_0$ is an ideal such that $\mathrm{V}(a_0)\neq\mathrm{S}_0$, and considers the nilradical $\mathfrak{R}_0$ of $\mathrm{A}_0$. In other words, one may suppose that $\mathrm{A}_0$ is \emph{reduced}; on the other hand, $\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}_0}(\mathrm{B}_0)$ is an $\mathrm{A}_0$-algebra of finite type and $\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}_0}(\mathrm{M}_0)$ a $\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}_0}(\mathrm{B}_0)$-module of finite type; by the generic flatness theorem $(6.9.1)$, there exists an open $\mathrm{D}(t_0)\subset\mathrm{S}_0$ such that, if $\mathrm{A}_{01}=(\mathrm{A}_0)_{t_0}$, $\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}_0}(\mathrm{M}_0)\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}_{01}$ is a flat $\mathrm{A}_{01}$-module; but since $\mathrm{A}_{01}$ is a flat $\mathrm{A}_0$-module, $\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}_0}(\mathrm{M}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}_{01})$ identifies with $\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}_{01}}(\mathrm{M}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}_{01})$, where $\mathrm{B}_{01}=\mathrm{B}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}_{01}$, $\mathfrak{J}_{01}=\mathfrak{J}_0\mathrm{B}_{01}$. The complement of $\mathrm{D}(t_0)$ in $\mathrm{S}_0$ is of the form $\mathrm{V}(a_0)$, and one concludes by applying the induction hypothesis.
\end{proof}

Apply this lemma to the present situation, keeping the same notations; set $\mathrm{U}_{0i}=\bigcup_{j\leqslant i}\mathrm{S}_{0j}$, which is an open quasi-compact of $\mathrm{S}_0$. There is then a family $(f_{ik})_{1\leqslant i\leqslant m,\,1\leqslant k\leqslant n}$ of elements of $\mathrm{A}_0$ such that for each $i$, $\mathrm{U}_{0i}$ is the union of the $\mathrm{D}(f_{ik})$ ($1\leqslant k\leqslant n$).

\oldpage[IV-3]{130}

The $\mathrm{C}_0$-module $\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}_0}(\mathrm{M}_0)$ is generated by a finite number of homogeneous elements of degree $1$, so there is an epimorphism of $\mathrm{C}_0$-graded modules $u_0:\mathrm{C}'_0\to\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}_0}(\mathrm{M}_0)$. As $\mathrm{C}=\mathrm{C}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}$ and the canonical homomorphism $\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}_0}(\mathrm{M}_0)\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}\to\operatorname{gr}^*_\mathfrak{J}(\mathrm{M})$ is surjective, one deduces therefore from $u_0$ an epimorphism of C-graded modules
\[
  u:\mathrm{C}''\xrightarrow{u_0\otimes 1_{\mathrm{A}'}}\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}_0}(\mathrm{M}_0)\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}\to\operatorname{gr}^*_\mathfrak{J}(\mathrm{M}),
\]
and it comes down to seeing that the graded C-module $\mathrm{Q}=\operatorname{Ker}(u)$ is \emph{of finite type}.

\begin{lemma}[11.2.9.6]
\label{IV.11.2.9.6}
Under the preceding hypotheses, let $\mathfrak{R}$ be an ideal of $\mathrm{A}_0$; set $\mathrm{A}'_0=\mathrm{A}_0/\mathfrak{R}$, $\mathrm{B}'_0=\mathrm{B}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}'_0$, $\mathfrak{J}'_0=\mathfrak{J}_0\mathrm{B}'_0$, $\mathrm{A}'=\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{R}$, and suppose moreover that $\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}_0}(\mathrm{M}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}'_0)$ is a flat $\mathrm{A}'_0$-module. Then there exist a finite number of elements $q_h$ ($1\leqslant h\leqslant s$) of $\mathrm{Q}$ such that, for every prime ideal $\mathfrak{p}\supset\mathfrak{R}\mathrm{B}$ of $\mathrm{B}$, the canonical images of the $q_h$ in $\mathrm{Q}_\mathfrak{p}$ engender the $\mathrm{C}_\mathfrak{p}$-module $\mathrm{Q}_\mathfrak{p}$.
\end{lemma}

It remains to prove the lemma \sref{IV.11.2.9.6}. Set $\mathrm{C}'=\mathrm{C}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'=\mathrm{C}/\mathfrak{R}\mathrm{C}$, $\mathrm{B}'=\mathrm{B}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'$, $\mathfrak{J}'=\mathfrak{J}\mathrm{B}'$, $\mathrm{M}'=\mathrm{M}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'$. By hypothesis $\operatorname{gr}^*_\mathfrak{J}(\mathrm{M})$ is an A-module flat, hence $\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}'}(\mathrm{M}')$ identifies with $\operatorname{gr}^*_\mathfrak{J}(\mathrm{M}')$ \sref{IV.11.2.9.2}, and one has the exact sequence $(\mathbf{0}_\mathrm{I},~6.1.2)$
\begin{equation}
  0\to\mathrm{Q}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'\to\mathrm{C}''\xrightarrow{u\otimes 1_{\mathrm{A}'}}\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}'}(\mathrm{M}')\to 0.
  \tag{11.2.9.7}
\end{equation}

Set $\mathrm{C}'_0=\mathrm{C}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}'_0$ and consider the epimorphism of $\mathrm{C}'_0$-graded modules
\[
  u'_0:\mathrm{C}''_0\xrightarrow{u_0\otimes 1_{\mathrm{A}'}}\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}_0}(\mathrm{M}_0)\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}'_0\to\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}_0}(\mathrm{M}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}'_0).
\]

Let $\mathrm{Q}_0=\operatorname{Ker}(u'_0)$; using the fact that $\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}_0}(\mathrm{M}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}'_0)$ is a flat $\mathrm{A}'_0$-module and that $\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}_0}(\mathrm{M}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}'_0)\otimes_{\mathrm{A}'}\mathrm{A}'$ identifies with $\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}'}(\mathrm{M}')$ \sref{IV.11.2.9.2}, one sees that
\begin{equation}
  \mathrm{Q}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Q}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}'.
  \tag{11.2.9.8}
\end{equation}
\end{proof}

\oldpage[IV-3]{131}

whence, by comparison with \sref{IV.11.2.9}[IV.11.2.9.7], an isomorphism of $\mathrm{C}'$-graded modules $\mathrm{Q}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Q}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}'$.

As $\mathrm{C}'_0$ is Noetherian, $\mathrm{Q}_0$ is a $\mathrm{C}'_0$-module of finite type, hence $\mathrm{Q}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}'_0}\mathrm{A}'$ is a $\mathrm{C}'$-graded module of finite type, and so it is likewise a module of the same type of $\mathrm{Q}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'$; let $q_h$ ($1\leqslant h\leqslant s$) be homogeneous elements of $\mathrm{Q}$ whose images in $\mathrm{Q}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'$ engender this $\mathrm{C}'$-module.

Consider now any prime ideal $\mathfrak{p}\supset\mathfrak{R}\mathrm{B}$ of $\mathrm{B}$; one has $\mathrm{C}_\mathfrak{p}=\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}_\mathfrak{p}}(\mathrm{B}_\mathfrak{p})$ by flatness, and $\operatorname{gr}^0_{\mathfrak{J}_\mathfrak{p}}(\mathrm{B}_\mathfrak{p})=\mathrm{B}_\mathfrak{p}/\mathfrak{J}_\mathfrak{p}$ is reduced to $0$ or is a local ring; to prove the lemma, one can evidently restrict to the second case. It is clear that for every $i$, $\mathrm{M}/\mathfrak{J}^i\mathrm{M}$ identifies with $(\mathrm{M}_0/\mathfrak{J}^k_0\mathrm{M}_0)\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}$, and is therefore a B-module of finite presentation. On the other hand, for every $i$, $\mathrm{M}_\mu/\mathfrak{J}^i_\mu\mathrm{M}_\mu$ for $k\leqslant i$ is the same as $\mathrm{M}_\mu/\mathfrak{J}^{k+1}_\mu\mathrm{M}_\mu$ is an $\mathrm{A}_\mu$-module flat, and also $(\mathbf{0}_\mathrm{I},~6.1.2)$ the quotient $\operatorname{gr}^k_{\mathfrak{J}_\mu}(\mathrm{M}_\mu)$ is an $\mathrm{A}_\mu$-module flat for $k\leqslant i$. One deduces from \sref{IV.11.2.9.2} that each of the $(\mathrm{B}/\mathfrak{J})$-modules $\operatorname{gr}^k_\mathfrak{J}(\mathrm{M})$ is of finite presentation. Moreover, the images of the $q_h$ in $\mathrm{Q}_\mathfrak{p}\otimes_{\mathrm{B}_\mathfrak{p}}\boldsymbol{k}(\mathfrak{p})$ (where $\mathrm{C}_\mathfrak{p}\otimes_{\mathrm{B}_\mathfrak{p}}\boldsymbol{k}(\mathfrak{p})=\mathrm{C}_\mathfrak{p}/\mathfrak{p}\mathrm{C}_\mathfrak{p}$) engender this $(\mathrm{C}_\mathfrak{p}\otimes_{\mathrm{B}_\mathfrak{p}}\boldsymbol{k}(\mathfrak{p}))$-module (since $\mathrm{C}_\mathfrak{p}\otimes_{\mathrm{B}_\mathfrak{p}}\boldsymbol{k}(\mathfrak{p})$); as on the exact sequence
\[
  \mathrm{Q}_\mathfrak{p}\otimes_{\mathrm{B}_\mathfrak{p}}\boldsymbol{k}(\mathfrak{p})\to\mathrm{C}'_\mathfrak{p}\otimes_{\mathrm{B}_\mathfrak{p}}\boldsymbol{k}(\mathfrak{p})\to\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}_\mathfrak{p}}(\mathrm{M}_\mathfrak{p})\otimes_{\mathrm{B}_\mathfrak{p}}\boldsymbol{k}(\mathfrak{p})\to 0
\]
one concludes that $\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}_\mathfrak{p}}(\mathrm{M}_\mathfrak{p})$ is a $(\mathrm{C}_\mathfrak{p}\otimes_{\mathrm{B}_\mathfrak{p}}\boldsymbol{k}(\mathfrak{p}))$-module of finite presentation. Applying the lemma \sref{IV.11.2.9.4} where $\mathrm{A}$, $\mathrm{B}$, $\mathrm{M}$ are replaced respectively by $\mathrm{B}_\mathfrak{p}$, $\mathrm{C}_\mathfrak{p}$ and $\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}_\mathfrak{p}}(\mathrm{M}_\mathfrak{p})$, one concludes that $\mathrm{Q}_\mathfrak{p}$ is a $\mathrm{C}_\mathfrak{p}$-module \emph{of finite type}. Using now the Nakayama lemma (and the fact that $\mathrm{C}=\mathrm{B}$) one deduces that the images of the $q_h$ in $\mathrm{Q}_\mathfrak{p}$ engender this $\mathrm{C}_\mathfrak{p}$-module. This completes the proof of the lemma \sref{IV.11.2.9.6} and the fact that $\operatorname{gr}^*_\mathfrak{J}(\mathrm{M})$ is a C-module of finite presentation.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{VI)} \emph{End of the proof.} --- Set, to abbreviate, $\mathrm{C}_\lambda=\mathrm{C}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}_\lambda$ (equal in fact to $\mathrm{B}_\lambda$), $\mathrm{N}_\lambda=\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathfrak{J}_\lambda}(\mathrm{M}_\lambda)$ and $\mathrm{N}=\operatorname{gr}^*_\mathfrak{J}(\mathrm{M})$. Note first that for each $k$, $\mathrm{N}$ identifies with $\varinjlim\mathfrak{J}^k_\lambda\mathrm{M}_\lambda$, the image of $\mathfrak{J}^k_\lambda\mathrm{M}_\lambda$ in $\mathrm{M}_\mu$ engendering $\mathfrak{J}^k_\mu\mathrm{M}_\mu$ for $\lambda\leqslant\mu$ (resp.\ in $\mathrm{M}$) engendering $\mathfrak{J}^k\mathrm{M}$; using again the exactness of $\varinjlim$, one concludes that $\mathrm{N}$ identifies canonically with $\varinjlim\mathrm{N}_\lambda$. Making this identification, we will first prove that:
\begin{equation}
\textbf{(11.2.9.9)}\quad
\text{\emph{For $\lambda$ sufficiently large, the canonical homomorphism $v_\lambda:\mathrm{N}_\lambda\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_\lambda}\mathrm{A}\to\mathrm{N}$ is bijective.}}
\end{equation}
As the $\mathrm{C}_\lambda$ are Noetherian and $\mathrm{N}_\lambda$ a $\mathrm{C}_\lambda$-module of finite type, the $\mathrm{N}_\lambda\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_\lambda}\mathrm{A}$ are C-modules of finite presentation and form an inductive filtrant system, whose inductive limit identifies canonically with $\mathrm{N}$ by virtue of the fact that $\varinjlim$ commutes with tensor products. Moreover, the homomorphisms of transition $v_{\mu\lambda}:\mathrm{N}_\lambda\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_\lambda}\mathrm{A}\to\mathrm{N}_\mu\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_\mu}\mathrm{A}$

\oldpage[IV-3]{132}

for $\lambda\leqslant\mu$ and the homomorphisms $v_\lambda$ are \emph{surjective}. For a fixed $\lambda$, consider the sub-C-modules $\operatorname{Ker}(v_{\mu\lambda})=\mathrm{N}'_\mu$ of $\mathrm{N}_\lambda\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_\lambda}\mathrm{A}$; by definition of the inductive limit, they form an increasing filtrant family of sub-C-modules, whose union is $\operatorname{Ker}(v_\lambda)$; but as one has seen in V) that $\mathrm{N}$ is a C-module of finite presentation, hence $\operatorname{Ker}(v_\lambda)$ is a C-module \emph{of finite type}; there exists therefore an index $\mu\geqslant\lambda$ such that $\mathrm{N}'_\mu=\operatorname{Ker}(v_\lambda)$, which means (seeing that $v_\mu$ is surjective) that $v_\mu$ is \emph{injective}; as it is surjective, this proves \sref{IV.11.2.9}[IV.11.2.9.9].

It suffices to replace $\mathrm{A}_0$ and $\mathrm{M}_0$ and $\mathrm{M}_\lambda$ for $\lambda$ large enough, one may suppose that the canonical homomorphism $v_\lambda$ is bijective for \emph{every} $\lambda$. Set then $\mathrm{P}_\lambda=\mathrm{N}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{C}_0}\mathrm{C}_\lambda=\mathrm{N}_0\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_0}\mathrm{A}_\lambda$, so that $\mathrm{N}=\varinjlim\mathrm{P}_\lambda$. As $\mathrm{C}_0$ is an $\mathrm{A}_0$-algebra of finite presentation and $\mathrm{P}_0$ a $\mathrm{C}_0$-module of finite presentation, one can apply $\mathrm{C}$ and $\mathrm{N}$ the result of \sref{IV.11.2.6.1} and one sees therefore that there exists an index $\lambda$ such that, for $\mu\geqslant\lambda$, $\mathrm{P}_\mu$ be a flat $\mathrm{A}_\mu$-module. Or, for $\mu\geqslant\lambda$, one has a commutative diagram
\[
\xymatrix{
  \mathrm{P}_\mu \ar[r] \ar[d]^{w_\mu} & \mathrm{N} \ar@{=}[d] \\
  \mathrm{N}_\mu \ar[r] & \mathrm{N}
}
\]
whence it follows from the definitions that $w_\mu$ is \emph{surjective}. As, in virtue of III), the homomorphisms $\mathrm{A}_\mu\to\mathrm{A}$ are injective and that $\mathrm{P}_\mu$ is a flat $\mathrm{A}_\mu$-module, the canonical homomorphism $\mathrm{P}_\mu\to\mathrm{N}=\mathrm{P}_\mu\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_\mu}\mathrm{A}$ is \emph{injective}; one concludes therefore from the preceding commutative diagram that $w_\mu$ is also \emph{injective}, hence bijective, and hence $\mathrm{N}_\mu$ is an $\mathrm{A}_\mu$-module \emph{flat} for $\mu\geqslant\lambda$, which completes the proof of \sref{IV.11.2.9}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[11.2.10]
\label{IV.11.2.10}
We do not know if the generalisation of \sref{IV.11.2.6},~(i)) analogous to the th.~de Raynaud, is valid.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Application to the elimination of Noetherian hypotheses}
\label{subsection:IV.11.3}

\oldpage[IV-3]{132}

\begin{theorem}[11.3.1]
\label{IV.11.3.1}
Let $f:\mathrm{X}\to\mathrm{Y}$ be a morphism locally of finite presentation, $\sh{F}$ an $\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}$-Module quasi-coherent of finite presentation. Then the set $\mathrm{U}$ of points $x\in\mathrm{X}$ such that $\sh{F}$ is $f$-flat at the point $x$ is open in $\mathrm{X}$. If in addition $\operatorname{Supp}(\sh{F})=\mathrm{X}$, $f|\mathrm{U}$ is an open morphism.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The second assertion has already been proved $(2.4.6)$ and is inserted here only for convenience.

The question being local on $\mathrm{X}$ and $\mathrm{Y}$, one can suppose that $\mathrm{X}$ and $\mathrm{Y}$ are affine, and that $f$ is a morphism of finite presentation. Let $x\in\mathrm{X}$ be a point such that $\sh{F}$ is $f$-flat at the point $x$. Applying \sref{IV.11.2.7}, one can suppose that $\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{X}_0\times_{\mathrm{Y}_0}\mathrm{Y}$, $f=f_0\times 1$, $\sh{F}=\sh{F}_0\otimes_{\sh{O}_{\mathrm{Y}_0}}\sh{O}_\mathrm{Y}$, where $\mathrm{Y}_0$ is Noetherian, $\sh{F}_0$ a coherent $\sh{O}_{\mathrm{X}_0}$-Module; moreover, if $x_0$ is the projection of $x$ in $\mathrm{X}_0$, one can suppose that $\sh{F}_0$ is $f_0$-flat at the point $x_0$. Then, by virtue of \sref{IV.11.1.1}, the set $\mathrm{U}_0$ of points of $\mathrm{X}_0$ where $\sh{F}_0$ is $f_0$-flat is a neighbourhood of $x_0$; hence $\sh{F}$ is $f$-flat at the points of the inverse image of $\mathrm{U}_0$ in $\mathrm{X}$ $(2.1.4)$, and this proves that $\mathrm{U}$ is a neighbourhood of $x$.
\end{proof}

\oldpage[IV-3]{133}

\begin{corollary}[11.3.2]
\label{IV.11.3.2}
Let $f:\mathrm{X}\to\mathrm{Y}$, $g:\mathrm{Y}\to\mathrm{Z}$ be two morphisms of finite presentation, $\sh{F}$ an $\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}$-Module quasi-coherent of finite presentation. Then the set $\mathrm{U}$ of $y\in\mathrm{Y}$ such that, for every generization $y'$ of $y$, $\sh{F}$ is $(g\circ f)$-flat at all the points of $f^{-1}(y')$ (i.e.\ such that $\sh{F}\otimes_\mathrm{Y}\Spec(\sh{O}_{\mathrm{Y},y})$ is flat relative to the morphism $\mathrm{X}\times_\mathrm{Y}\Spec(\sh{O}_{\mathrm{Y},y})\to\mathrm{Z}$) is open in $\mathrm{Y}$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The same reasoning as in \sref{IV.11.1.5} shows that if $\mathrm{V}$ is the set of $x\in\mathrm{X}$ where $\sh{F}$ is $(g\circ f)$-flat, $\mathrm{U}$ is the set of $y\in\mathrm{Y}$ whose every generization belongs to $\mathrm{E}=\mathrm{Y}-f(\mathrm{X}-\mathrm{V})$. As $g\circ f$ is of finite presentation, $\mathrm{V}$ is open in $\mathrm{X}$ by virtue of \sref{IV.11.3.1}, and hence $\mathrm{V}$ is ind-constructible $(1.9.6)$, and hence $\mathrm{X}-\mathrm{V}$ is pro-constructible; but as $f$ is quasi-compact, $f(\mathrm{X}-\mathrm{V})$ is pro-constructible in $\mathrm{Y}$ $(1.9.5,~(\mathrm{vii}))$, and hence $\mathrm{E}$ is ind-constructible in $\mathrm{Y}$. But it follows then from $(1.10.1)$ that $\mathrm{U}$ is open in $\mathrm{Y}$, hence the conclusion.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[11.3.3]
\label{IV.11.3.3}
Let $\mathrm{A}$ be a ring, $\mathrm{B}$ an $\mathrm{A}$-algebra of finite presentation, $\mathrm{C}$ a $\mathrm{B}$-algebra of finite presentation, $\mathrm{M}$ a $\mathrm{C}$-module of finite presentation. Then the set of $\mathfrak{q}\in\Spec(\mathrm{B})$ such that $\mathrm{M}_\mathfrak{q}$ is a flat $\mathrm{A}$-module is open in $\Spec(\mathrm{B})$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Taking into account $(2.1.2)$, this is a consequence of \sref{IV.11.1.5} applied to $\mathrm{Z}=\Spec(\mathrm{A})$, $\mathrm{Y}=\Spec(\mathrm{B})$, $\mathrm{X}=\Spec(\mathrm{C})$, $\sh{F}=\widetilde{\mathrm{M}}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[11.3.4]
\label{IV.11.3.4}
Let $f:\mathrm{X}\to\mathrm{S}$ be a morphism locally of finite presentation, $\mathscr{J}$ a quasi-coherent Ideal of finite type of $\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}$, $\mathrm{Y}$ the closed sub-prescheme of $\mathrm{X}$ defined by $\mathscr{J}$, $\sh{F}$ an $\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}$-Module of finite presentation. Suppose that $\operatorname{gr}^*_\mathscr{J}(\sh{F})$ is $f$-flat. Under these conditions:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\emph{(\roman*)}]
  \item $\sh{F}$ is $f$-flat at the points of $\mathrm{Y}$.
  \item If $\operatorname{gr}^*_\mathscr{J}(\sh{O}_\mathrm{X})$ is $f$-flat, $\operatorname{gr}^*_\mathscr{J}(\sh{O}_\mathrm{X})$ is an $(\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}/\mathscr{J})$-Algebra of finite presentation and $\operatorname{gr}^*_\mathscr{J}(\sh{F})$ is an $(\operatorname{gr}^*_\mathscr{J}(\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}))$-Module of finite presentation.
  \item Suppose $\operatorname{gr}^*_\mathscr{J}(\sh{O}_\mathrm{X})$ is $f$-flat (which implies that $\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}/\mathscr{J}$ is $f$-flat). Then the set $\mathrm{W}$ of $y\in\mathrm{Y}$ such that $(\operatorname{gr}^*_\mathscr{J}(\sh{F}))_y$ is a flat $(\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}/\mathscr{J})_y$-module is open in $\mathrm{Y}$.
  \item Suppose $\operatorname{gr}^*_\mathscr{J}(\sh{O}_\mathrm{X})$ is $f$-flat. Let $\mathrm{S}'\to\mathrm{S}$ be any morphism; let $\mathrm{X}'=\mathrm{X}\times_\mathrm{S}\mathrm{S}'$ the closed sub-prescheme of $\mathrm{X}'$, $\mathscr{J}'=p^*(\mathscr{J})\sh{O}_{\mathrm{X}'}$, $\sh{F}'=p^*(\sh{F})=\sh{F}\otimes_{\sh{O}_\mathrm{S}}\sh{O}_{\mathrm{S}'}$; then, if $\mathrm{W}'=p^{-1}(\mathrm{W})$, one has $\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathscr{J}'}(\sh{F}')|_{\mathrm{W}'}\cong p^*(\operatorname{gr}^*_\mathscr{J}(\sh{F})|_\mathrm{W})$, and $\operatorname{gr}^*_{\mathscr{J}'}(\sh{F}')$ is a flat $(\sh{O}_{\mathrm{X}'}/\mathscr{J}')$-Module at the points of $\mathrm{W}'$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The questions being local on $\mathrm{X}$ and $\mathrm{S}$, one may suppose that $\mathrm{X}=\Spec(\mathrm{B})$ and $\mathrm{S}=\Spec(\mathrm{A})$, $\sh{F}=\widetilde{\mathrm{M}}$, where $\mathscr{J}=\widetilde{\mathfrak{J}}$, where $\mathfrak{J}$ is an ideal of finite type of $\mathrm{B}$; by virtue of $(8.9.1)$ and $(8.5.11)$, there exists a sub-ring Noetherian $\mathrm{A}_0$ of $\mathrm{A}$, an $\mathrm{A}_0$-algebra of finite type $\mathrm{B}_0$, an ideal $\mathfrak{J}_0$ of $\mathrm{B}_0$ such that $\mathfrak{J}=\mathfrak{J}_0\mathrm{B}$, a $\mathrm{B}_0$-module of type fini $\mathrm{M}_0$.
