\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
  \item For all $\alpha$, $\sh{F}'_\alpha$ is $f'_\alpha$-flat at every point whose projection in $\mathrm{X}$ is equal
  to $x$ and whose projection in $\mathrm{Y}'_\alpha$ is equal to $y'_\alpha$.
  \item For all $\alpha$, there exists a point $x'_\alpha\in\mathrm{X}'_\alpha$ whose projection on $\mathrm{X}$ is $x$ and whose projection in $\mathrm{Y}'_\alpha$
  is equal to $y'_\alpha$, such that $\sh{F}'_\alpha$ is $f'_\alpha$-flat at the point $x'_\alpha$, and $k(x)$ is a primary extension of $k(y)$.
\end{enumerate}

Under these conditions, $\sh{F}$ is $f$-flat at the point $x$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Let $\mathfrak{m}_y$ be the maximal ideal of $\sh{O}_y$; since $\sh{O}_y$ and $\sh{O}_x$ are Noetherian and $\sh{O}_y\to\sh{O}_x$ a
homomorphism of local rings, it suffices, by virtue of $(\mathbf{0}_{\mathrm{III}},~10.2.2)$, to prove that for
every $n>0$, $\sh{F}_x/\mathfrak{m}_y^n\sh{F}_x$ is an $(\sh{O}_y/\mathfrak{m}_y^n)$-module flat. Let us denote by $(\mathfrak{J}_\lambda)$ the family of
intersections of the $u_\alpha^{-1}(\mathfrak{m}'^{n\alpha}_\alpha)$; by hypothesis, there exists $\lambda$ such that $\mathfrak{J}_\lambda\subset\mathfrak{m}_y^n$, and since
$\sh{F}_x\otimes_{\sh{O}_y}(\sh{O}_y/\mathfrak{J}_\lambda)=(\sh{F}_x\otimes_{\sh{O}_y}(\sh{O}_y/\mathfrak{J}_\lambda))\otimes_{\sh{O}_y/\mathfrak{J}_\lambda}(\sh{O}_y/\mathfrak{m}_y^n)$, it will suffice to prove that $\sh{F}_x/\mathfrak{J}_\lambda\sh{F}_x$ is
an $(\sh{O}_y/\mathfrak{J}_\lambda)$-module flat. Now, for each $\alpha$, such that $\mathfrak{J}_\lambda\subset u_\alpha^{-1}(\mathfrak{m}'^{n\alpha}_\alpha)$, one has, by passage
to quotients, a homomorphism $u'_\alpha:\sh{O}_y/\mathfrak{J}_\lambda\to\mathrm{A}'_\alpha/\mathfrak{m}'^{n\alpha}_\alpha$, and the intersection of the kernels
of the $u'_\alpha$ is reduced to $0$. Taking into account (\textbf{I},~$3.6.1$), one sees that one is brought back to \sref{IV.11.4.11}
in case (ii), and to \sref{IV.11.4.12} in case (i).
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[11.5.2]
\label{IV.11.5.2}
Let $\mathrm{Y}$ be a locally Noetherian prescheme, $f:\mathrm{X}\to\mathrm{Y}$ a
morphism locally of finite type, $\sh{F}$ a coherent $\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}$-Module, $x$ a point of $\mathrm{X}$, $y=f(x)$; set
$\mathrm{A}=\sh{O}_y$. Let $u:\mathrm{A}\to\mathrm{A}'$ be a homomorphism of $\mathrm{A}$ into a Zariski ring $\mathrm{A}'$, such that the image
of the inverse image $u^{-1}(\mathfrak{r}')$ of the radical $\mathfrak{r}'$ of $\mathrm{A}'$ is the maximal ideal $\mathfrak{m}$ of $\mathrm{A}$; suppose moreover
that the homomorphism $\hat{u}:\hat{\mathrm{A}}\to\hat{\mathrm{A}}'$ is injective. Set $\mathrm{Y}'=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A}')$, $\mathrm{X}'=\mathrm{X}\times_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{Y}'$, $f'=f_{|\mathrm{Y}'}$,
$\sh{F}'=\sh{F}\otimes_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{Y}'$. For $\sh{F}$ to be $f$-flat at the point $x$, it is necessary and sufficient that $\sh{F}'$ be $f'$-flat at every
point whose projection in $\mathrm{X}$ is equal to $x$ and whose projection in $\mathrm{Y}'$ is equal to a closed point
of $\mathrm{Y}'$.

If moreover $\mathrm{A}'$ is a finite $\mathrm{A}$-algebra, one can in what precedes replace the hypothesis that $\hat{u}$
is injective by the hypothesis that $u$ is injective.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Since $\mathrm{A}$ (resp.\ $\mathrm{A}'$) is identified with a sub-ring of $\hat{\mathrm{A}}$ (resp.\ $\hat{\mathrm{A}}'$) (Bourbaki,
\emph{Alg.\ comm.}, chap.~III, \S~3, n\textsuperscript{o}~3, prop.~6), one sees first that $u$ is itself injective
and that $\hat{u}$ is its extension by continuity to $\hat{\mathrm{A}}$.

Let $(\mathfrak{m}'_\alpha)$ be the family of maximal ideals of $\mathrm{A}'$; since one has
\[
\mathfrak{m}'^n_\alpha\hat{\mathrm{A}}'=\hat{\mathfrak{m}}'^n_\alpha, \qquad \text{and} \qquad \hat{\mathfrak{m}}'^n_\alpha\cap\mathrm{A}'=\mathfrak{m}'^n_\alpha,
\]
and that the $\mathfrak{m}'^n_\alpha$ are open in $\mathrm{A}'$, the intersections of finitely many of the $u^{-1}(\mathfrak{m}'^n_\alpha)=\mathrm{A}\cap\hat{u}^{-1}(\hat{\mathfrak{m}}'^n_\alpha)$, and it will suffice to
\oldpage[IV-3]{152}
show that in $\hat{\mathrm{A}}$, the intersections of the $\hat{\mathfrak{m}}'^n_\alpha$ for $n>0$ are reduced to $0$ in the local Noetherian ring $\hat{\mathrm{A}}_{\hat{\mathfrak{m}}'^n_\alpha}$.
On the other hand the $\hat{\mathfrak{m}}'_\alpha$ are the maximal ideals of $\hat{\mathrm{A}}'$, hence the canonical homomorphism
$\hat{\mathrm{A}}'\to\prod_\alpha\hat{\mathrm{A}}'_{\hat{\mathfrak{m}}'_\alpha}$ is injective (Bourbaki, \emph{Alg.\ comm.}, chap.~II, \S~3, n\textsuperscript{o}~3, cor.~2 of th.~1), and
since by hypothesis $\hat{u}:\hat{\mathrm{A}}\to\hat{\mathrm{A}}'$ is also injective, this achieves the proof in
the general case. The last assertion follows from the fact that $\hat{\mathrm{A}}$ is an $\mathrm{A}$-module faithfully
flat $(\mathbf{0}_\mathrm{I},~7.3.5)$ and $\hat{\mathrm{A}}'=\mathrm{A}'\otimes_\mathrm{A}\hat{\mathrm{A}}$ since $\mathrm{A}'$ is an $\mathrm{A}$-module of finite type
(Bourbaki, \emph{Alg.\ comm.}, chap.~III, \S~3, n\textsuperscript{o}~4, th.~3 and chap.~IV, \S~2, n\textsuperscript{o}~5, cor.~3 of prop.~9).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[11.5.3]
\label{IV.11.5.3}
Let $f:\mathrm{X}\to\mathrm{Y}$ be a morphism locally of finite type,
$\sh{F}$ a quasi-coherent $\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}$-Module of finite presentation, $x$ a point of $\mathrm{X}$, $y=f(x)$, $g=(\psi,\theta):\mathrm{Y}'\to\mathrm{Y}$
a proper morphism of finite presentation. Suppose that:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
  \item The homomorphism $\theta_y:\sh{O}_y\to(g_*(\sh{O}_{\mathrm{Y}'}))_y$ is injective.
  \item For every $x'\in\mathrm{X}'=\mathrm{X}\times_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{Y}'$ whose projection in $\mathrm{X}$ is $x$, $\sh{F}'=\sh{F}\otimes_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{Y}'$ is
  $\mathrm{Y}'$-flat at the point $x'$.
\end{enumerate}

Then $\sh{F}$ is $f$-flat at the point $x$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The question being local on $\mathrm{X}$, one can suppose $f$ of finite presentation. Since
$f'=f_{|\mathrm{Y}'}:\mathrm{X}'\to\mathrm{Y}'$ is of finite presentation
$(1.6.2)$ and $\sh{F}'$ is an $\sh{O}_{\mathrm{X}'}$-Module of finite presentation $(\mathbf{0}_\mathrm{I},~5.2.5)$, it follows
from \sref{IV.11.3.1} that the set $\mathrm{U}'$ of points of $\mathrm{X}'$ where $\sh{F}'$ is $f'$-flat is open. Since $\mathrm{U}'$
contains $p^{-1}(x)$ by hypothesis, and that $p$ is proper, hence \emph{closed}, $\mathrm{U}'$ contains a closed set, hence closed, $\mathrm{U}'$ contains an
open set of the form $p^{-1}(\mathrm{U})$, where $\mathrm{U}$ is a neighbourhood of $x$. Replacing $\mathrm{X}$ by $\mathrm{U}$, one can therefore
suppose that $\sh{F}'$ is $f'$-flat. On the other hand, taking into account (\textbf{I},~$3.6.5$), (\textbf{II},~$5.4.2$)
and $(2.1.4)$, one can also replace $\mathrm{Y}$ by $\operatorname{Spec}(\sh{O}_y)$, i.e.\ suppose that $\mathrm{Y}=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A})$,
where $\mathrm{A}$ is a local ring. Under these conditions, we want to prove that $\sh{F}$ is $f$-flat. By virtue
of $(5.13.3)$, $\mathrm{A}$ is the inductive limit of local Noetherian sub-rings $\mathrm{A}_\lambda$ such that
the canonical injection $\mathrm{A}_\lambda\to\mathrm{A}$ is a \emph{local} homomorphism. By virtue of $(8.9.1)$, one
can suppose that $\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{X}_\lambda\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_\lambda}\mathrm{A}$, $f=f_\lambda\otimes 1_\mathrm{A}$, $\sh{F}=\sh{F}_\lambda\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_\lambda}\mathrm{A}$, for a suitable $\lambda$ and an $\sh{O}_{\mathrm{X}_\lambda}$-Module coherent.
Similarly, one can suppose that $\mathrm{Y}'=\mathrm{Y}'_\lambda\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_\lambda}\mathrm{A}$, $g=g_\lambda\otimes 1_\mathrm{A}$, where $g_\lambda:\mathrm{Y}'_\lambda\to\mathrm{Y}_\lambda$ is a
morphism of finite presentation; moreover $(8.10.5,~(\text{xii}))$, one can suppose $g_\lambda$
\emph{proper}. Since by hypothesis the homomorphism $\mathrm{A}\to\Gamma(\mathrm{Y}',\sh{O}_{\mathrm{Y}'})$ is injective and that $\mathrm{A}_\lambda$
is a sub-ring of $\mathrm{A}$, the homomorphism $\mathrm{A}_\lambda\to\Gamma(\mathrm{Y}',\sh{O}_{\mathrm{Y}'})$ is also injective. Finally,
by virtue of \sref{IV.11.2.6}, one can suppose $\lambda$ taken such that $\sh{F}'_\lambda=\sh{F}_\lambda\otimes_{\mathrm{Y}_\lambda}\mathrm{Y}'_\lambda$ is $f'_\lambda$-flat,
since $\sh{F}'=\sh{F}'_\lambda\otimes_{\mathrm{Y}'_\lambda}\mathrm{Y}'$. These remarks show that one can henceforth suppose that
the ring $\mathrm{A}$ is \emph{Noetherian}, the other hypotheses of \sref{IV.11.5.3} being verified. Let us then set
$\mathrm{B}=\hat{\mathrm{A}}$, $\mathrm{Z}=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{B})$; since $\mathrm{B}$ is an $\mathrm{A}$-module faithfully flat $(\mathbf{0}_\mathrm{I},~7.3.5)$, it amounts
to the same to say that $\sh{F}$ is $f$-flat or that $\sh{F}\otimes_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{Z}$ is $\mathrm{Z}$-flat $(2.1.4)$; similarly, if one
sets $\mathrm{Z}'=\mathrm{Y}'\times_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{Z}$, the morphism $\mathrm{Z}'\to\mathrm{Y}'$ is faithfully flat $(2.2.13)$, hence if
it amounts to the same to say that $\sh{F}'$ is $f'$-flat or that $\sh{F}'\otimes_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{Z}'$ is $\mathrm{Z}'$-flat; finally $h=g_{(\mathfrak{m})}:\mathrm{Z}'\to\mathrm{Z}$
is proper (\textbf{II},~$5.4.2$) and of finite type $(1.5.2)$, $\hat{\mathrm{A}}$ is Noetherian, and if $z$ is its
\oldpage[IV-3]{153}
closed point, the homomorphism $\sh{O}_z\to(h_*(\sh{O}_{\mathrm{Z}'}))_z$ is \emph{injective}, since it follows from $(2.3.1)$ that
$h_*(\sh{O}_{\mathrm{Z}'})=g_*(\sh{O}_{\mathrm{Y}'})\otimes_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{Z}$, and our assertion follows from hypothesis (i) and from the definition of
flat modules $(\mathbf{0}_\mathrm{I},~6.1.1)$.

One can therefore now suppose the local Noetherian ring $\mathrm{A}$ \emph{complete}; the proof
will be finished if one proves that the intersection of the kernels of the homomorphisms
$\mathrm{A}=\sh{O}_y\to\sh{O}_{y'}$ (where $y'$ ranges over $g^{-1}(y)$) is reduced to $0$. Indeed, the $\sh{O}_{y'}$ are local
Noetherian rings, hence for each $y'$ the intersection of the $\mathfrak{m}_y^n$ $(n\geq 0)$ is reduced to $0$;
if $\mathfrak{a}_{n,y'}$ is the inverse image of $\mathfrak{m}_y^n$ in $\mathrm{A}$, the intersections of finitely many of the $\mathfrak{a}_{n,y'}$ are
neighbourhoods of $0$ in $\mathrm{A}$ and the intersection of all the $\mathfrak{a}_{n,y'}$ is reduced to $0$; the intersections
$\mathfrak{a}_{n,y'}$ will therefore form a fundamental system of neighbourhoods of $0$ in $\mathrm{A}$
(Bourbaki, \emph{Alg.\ comm.}, chap.~III, \S~2, n\textsuperscript{o}~7, prop.~8, where one can in the proof
replace the decreasing sequence by an arbitrary filtered set); one will then be able to
apply \sref{IV.11.5.1}. Now, let $s\in\mathrm{A}$ be an element belonging to the kernel of each of
the homomorphisms $\mathrm{A}\to\sh{O}_{y'}$; the image $s'$ of $s$ in $\Gamma(\mathrm{Y}',\sh{O}_{\mathrm{Y}'})$ is therefore a section
of $\sh{O}_{\mathrm{Y}'}$ above $\mathrm{Y}'$ such that $s'_{y'}=0$ for every $y'\in g^{-1}(y)$; then there is a neighbourhood $\mathrm{V}'$
of $g^{-1}(y)$ in $\mathrm{Y}'$ such that $s'|\mathrm{V}'=0$. But since $g$ is \emph{closed}, $\mathrm{V}'$ contains a set
of the form $g^{-1}(\mathrm{V})$, where $\mathrm{V}$ is a neighbourhood of the only closed point $y$ of $\mathrm{Y}$, i.e.\ $\mathrm{V}'=\mathrm{Y}'$,
$s'=0$, and since $\mathrm{A}\to\Gamma(\mathrm{Y}',\sh{O}_{\mathrm{Y}'})$ is injective by hypothesis, $s=0$.
\end{proof}

The following particular case of \sref{IV.11.5.3} will be useful in chap.~V:

\begin{corollary}[11.5.4]
\label{IV.11.5.4}
Let $f=(\psi,\theta):\mathrm{X}\to\mathrm{Y}$ be a proper morphism of finite presentation,
and let $p:\mathrm{X}\times_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{X}\to\mathrm{X}$ be the first projection. Suppose that $\theta:\sh{O}_\mathrm{Y}\to f_*(\sh{O}_\mathrm{X})$ is injective.
Then, for $f$ to be flat, it is necessary and sufficient that $p$ be flat.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proposition}[11.5.5]
\label{IV.11.5.5}
Let $\mathrm{A}$ be a ring, $\mathrm{Y}=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A})$, $f:\mathrm{X}\to\mathrm{Y}$ a morphism
locally of finite presentation, $\sh{F}$ a quasi-coherent $\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}$-Module of finite presentation, $x$ a point
of $\mathrm{X}$. Let $\mathrm{A}\to\mathrm{A}'$ be an injective homomorphism making $\mathrm{A}'$ an entire algebra over $\mathrm{A}$. Set
$\mathrm{Y}'=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A}')$, $\mathrm{X}'=\mathrm{X}\times_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{Y}'$, $f'=f\times 1$, $\sh{F}'=\sh{F}\otimes_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{Y}'$. Then, if $\sh{F}'$ is $f'$-flat at every point
of $\mathrm{X}'$ whose projection in $\mathrm{X}$ is $x$, $\sh{F}$ is $f$-flat at the point $x$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Suppose first that $\mathrm{A}'$ is an $\mathrm{A}$-algebra \emph{finite} and of \emph{finite presentation}; then the
morphism $\mathrm{Y}'\to\mathrm{Y}$ is proper (\textbf{II},~$6.1.11$) and of finite presentation, hence the hypotheses
of \sref{IV.11.5.3} are satisfied, whence the conclusion. In the general case, the proposition results
from this particular case, from the fact that $\mathrm{A}'$ is the inductive limit of its sub-$\mathrm{A}$-algebras \emph{finite}
$\mathrm{A}'_\lambda$ and of the two following lemmas:
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[11.5.5.1]
\label{IV.11.5.5.1}
Every finite $\mathrm{A}$-algebra $\mathrm{A}'$ is the inductive limit of $\mathrm{A}$-algebras $\mathrm{A}'_\lambda$
which are finite and of finite presentation.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
One reasons as in $(1.9.3.1)$. Indeed $\mathrm{A}'=\mathrm{B}/\mathfrak{J}$, where $\mathrm{B}$ is a finite $\mathrm{A}$-algebra
free which is an $\mathrm{A}$-module free, and $\mathfrak{J}$ an ideal of $\mathrm{B}$ $(1.4.7.1)$. Now, $\mathfrak{J}$ is the inductive
limit of ideals $\mathfrak{J}_\lambda\subset\mathfrak{J}$ of $\mathrm{B}$ which are of finite type (and \emph{a fortiori} of $\mathrm{A}$-modules \emph{of finite type}), hence,
by the exactness of the functor $\varinjlim$, $\mathrm{A}'$ is the inductive limit of the $\mathrm{A}$-algebras $\mathrm{B}/\mathfrak{J}_\lambda$; now, $\mathrm{B}/\mathfrak{J}_\lambda$
is by definition an $\mathrm{A}$-module of finite presentation, hence also $(1.4.7)$ a finite $\mathrm{A}$-algebra
of finite presentation.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[11.5.5.2]
\label{IV.11.5.5.2}
Let $\mathrm{A}$ be a ring, $\mathrm{A}'$ an $\mathrm{A}$-algebra, $(\mathrm{A}'_\lambda)$ an inductive system
of $\mathrm{A}$-algebras such that $\mathrm{A}'=\varinjlim\mathrm{A}'_\lambda$; set $\mathrm{Y}=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A})$, $\mathrm{Y}'=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A}')$, $\mathrm{Y}'_\lambda=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A}'_\lambda)$.
Let $f:\mathrm{X}\to\mathrm{Y}$ be a morphism of finite presentation, $\sh{F}$ a quasi-coherent $\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}$-Module of finite
presentation; set $\mathrm{X}'=\mathrm{X}\times_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{Y}'$, $f'=f_{|\mathrm{Y}'}$, $\sh{F}'=\sh{F}\otimes_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{Y}'$, $\mathrm{X}'_\lambda=\mathrm{X}\times_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{Y}'_\lambda$, $f'_\lambda=f_{|\mathrm{Y}'_\lambda}$,
$\sh{F}'_\lambda=\sh{F}\otimes_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{Y}'_\lambda$. Let $x$ be a point of $\mathrm{X}$, such that $\sh{F}'$ is $f'$-flat at every point $x'\in\mathrm{X}'$ above $x$;
then there exists $\lambda$ such that $\sh{F}'_\lambda$ is $f'_\lambda$-flat at every point $x'\in\mathrm{X}'_\lambda$ above $x$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\oldpage[IV-3]{154}
Let $\mathrm{U}'$ be the set of $x'\in\mathrm{X}'$ such that $\sh{F}'$ is $f'$-flat at the point $x'$; one knows \sref{IV.11.3.1}
that $\mathrm{U}'$ is open in $\mathrm{X}'$ since $f'$ is of finite type $(1.6.2)$; similarly the
set $\mathrm{U}'_\lambda$ of points of $\mathrm{X}'_\lambda$ where $\sh{F}'_\lambda$ is $f'_\lambda$-flat is open in $\mathrm{X}'_\lambda$, and one knows moreover
\sref{IV.11.2.6} that $\mathrm{U}'$ is the union of the $v_\lambda^{-1}(\mathrm{U}'_\lambda)$, where $v_\lambda:\mathrm{X}'\to\mathrm{X}'_\lambda$ is the canonical projection. Let us set $\mathrm{V}'_\lambda=g_\lambda^{-1}(\mathrm{U}'_\lambda)$,
$\mathrm{V}'=g'^{-1}(\mathrm{U}')=\bigsqcup w_\lambda^{-1}(\mathrm{U}'_\lambda)$.
Let us consider the scheme $\mathrm{T}=\operatorname{Spec}(k(x))$; set $\mathrm{T}'=\mathrm{T}\times_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{Y}'$, $\mathrm{T}'_\lambda=\mathrm{T}\times_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{Y}'_\lambda$, and let
$w_\lambda:\mathrm{T}'\to\mathrm{T}'_\lambda$, $g':\mathrm{T}'\to\mathrm{X}'$, $g'_\lambda:\mathrm{T}'_\lambda\to\mathrm{X}'_\lambda$ be the canonical projections. Let us set $\mathrm{V}'_\lambda=g_\lambda'^{-1}(\mathrm{U}'_\lambda)$,
$\mathrm{V}'=g'^{-1}(\mathrm{U}')=\bigcup w_\lambda^{-1}(\mathrm{V}'_\lambda)$. By hypothesis on $\mathrm{U}'$ (and taking into account (\textbf{I},~$3.6.1$)) $\mathrm{V}'=\mathrm{T}'$;
as $\mathrm{T}'$ is quasi-compact, there exists $\lambda$ such that $w_\lambda^{-1}(\mathrm{V}'_\lambda)=\mathrm{T}'$. One deduces then from $(8.3.3)$
applied to the closed parts $\mathrm{T}'_\lambda-\mathrm{V}'_\lambda$ of $\mathrm{T}'_\lambda$, that there exists $\mu\geq\lambda$ such
that $\mathrm{T}'_\mu=\mathrm{V}'_\mu$; this means that $\sh{F}'_\mu$ is $f'_\mu$-flat at every point of $\mathrm{X}'_\lambda$ whose the projection
in $\mathrm{X}$ is $x$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Descent of flatness by arbitrary morphisms: the case of a unibranch base}
\label{subsection:IV.11.6}

\begin{theorem}[11.6.1]
\label{IV.11.6.1}
Let $\mathrm{A}$ be a local ring which is integral and geometrically unibranch
$(\mathbf{0},~23.2.1)$, $\mathrm{Y}=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A})$, $f:\mathrm{X}\to\mathrm{Y}$ a morphism locally of finite presentation, $\sh{F}$ a
coherent $\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}$-Module of finite presentation. Let $\mathrm{A}'$ be a local ring, $u:\mathrm{A}\to\mathrm{A}'$ a local
injective homomorphism; set $\mathrm{Y}'=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A}')$, $\mathrm{X}'=\mathrm{X}\times_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{Y}'$, $f'=f_{|\mathrm{Y}'}$, $\sh{F}'=\sh{F}\otimes_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{Y}'$. Let $x$ be
a point of $\mathrm{X}$ whose projection $f(x)=y$ is the closed point of $\mathrm{Y}$, $x'$ a point of $\mathrm{X}'$ whose projections
in $\mathrm{X}$ and $\mathrm{Y}'$ are respectively $x$ and the closed point $y'$ of $\mathrm{Y}'$. Then, if $\sh{F}'$ is $f'$-flat at the point $x'$,
$\sh{F}$ is $f$-flat at the point $x$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
One can restrict to the case where $f$ is of finite presentation, the question being local
on $\mathrm{X}$. We will proceed in several steps.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{I)} \emph{Reduction to the case where $\mathrm{A}$ and $\mathrm{A}'$ are local rings integrally closed.}

\medskip
Since $u$ is injective and $\mathrm{A}$ integral, on can therefore first suppose $\mathrm{A}'$
integrally closed $(\mathbf{0}_\mathrm{I},~1.5.8)$; the composite homomorphism $\mathrm{A}\to\mathrm{A}'\to\mathrm{A}''=\mathrm{A}'/\mathfrak{p}'$ is therefore injective
and local, and if $\mathrm{Y}''=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A}'')$, $\mathrm{X}''=\mathrm{X}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}''=\mathrm{X}\times_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{Y}''$, $\sh{F}''=\sh{F}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}''$, $\sh{F}''$ is $\mathrm{Y}''$-flat
at the points of $\mathrm{X}''$ above $x'$ $(2.1.4)$; replacing $\mathrm{A}'$ by $\mathrm{A}''$ as needed and taking
into account (\textbf{I},~$3.4.7$), one can therefore first suppose $\mathrm{A}'$ integral. If $\mathrm{K}'$ is the field of
fractions of $\mathrm{A}'$, there is a valuation ring $\mathrm{B}'$ in $\mathrm{K}'$ that dominates $\mathrm{A}'$; the composite
morphism $\mathrm{A}\xrightarrow{u}\mathrm{A}'\to\mathrm{B}'$ being injective and local, the same reasoning as
\oldpage[IV-3]{155}
previously allows one to replace $\mathrm{A}'$ by $\mathrm{B}'$; $\mathrm{A}'$ being a sub-local ring of $\mathrm{A}'$ dominated by $\mathrm{A}'$. Let $\mathrm{A}_1$ be the integral
closure of $\mathrm{A}$; it is clear that $\mathrm{A}\subset\mathrm{A}_1\subset\mathrm{A}'$, and by hypothesis $\mathrm{A}_1$ is a \emph{local} ring;
if $\mathfrak{m}$, $\mathfrak{m}_1$, $\mathfrak{m}'$ are the maximal ideals of $\mathrm{A}$, $\mathrm{A}_1$, $\mathrm{A}'$, one has $\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathfrak{m}_1\subset\mathfrak{m}'$; indeed, $\mathfrak{m}_1$ is
the \emph{sole} prime ideal of $\mathrm{A}_1$ above $\mathfrak{m}$, since $\mathrm{A}_1$ is a local ring (Bourbaki,
\emph{Alg.\ comm.}, chap.~V, \S~2, n\textsuperscript{o}~1, prop.~1); since $\mathfrak{m}'\cap\mathrm{A}=\mathfrak{m}$, one has $\mathfrak{m}'\cap\mathrm{A}_1\cap\mathrm{A}=\mathfrak{m}$,
hence $\mathfrak{m}'\cap\mathrm{A}_1=\mathfrak{m}_1$. Set $\mathrm{Y}_1=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A}_1)$, $\mathrm{X}_1=\mathrm{X}\times_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{Y}_1$, $f_1=f_{|\mathrm{Y}_1}$, $\sh{F}_1=\sh{F}\otimes_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{Y}_1$, and
let $x_1$ be the projection of $x'$ in $\mathrm{X}_1$; let us denote furthermore by $y_1$ the unique closed point
of $\mathrm{Y}_1$, so that $y_1=f_1(x_1)$. By hypothesis the morphism $\operatorname{Spec}(k(y_1))\to\operatorname{Spec}(k(y))$
is \emph{radicial}, whence one concludes, by the transitivity of fibres (\textbf{I},~$3.6.4$) and (\textbf{I},~$3.5.7$), that
the morphism $f_1^{-1}(y_1)\to f^{-1}(y)$ is radicial, and in particular $x_1$ is the \emph{sole} point of $\mathrm{X}_1$
whose projections in $\mathrm{X}$ and $\mathrm{Y}_1$ are $x$ and $y_1$ respectively; moreover, as one has seen, $y_1$
is the \emph{sole} point of $\mathrm{Y}_1$ above the projection in $\mathrm{Y}$, hence $x_1$ is the \emph{sole} point of $\mathrm{X}_1$
whose projection in $\mathrm{X}$ is $x$. If one proves that $\sh{F}_1$ is $f_1$-flat at the point $x_1$, one will be able to
apply \sref{IV.11.5.5}, whence the conclusion. One is therefore brought back to the case where $\mathrm{A}$
is also \emph{integrally closed}.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{II)} \emph{Reduction to the case where $\mathrm{A}$ and $\mathrm{A}'$ are local rings of $\mathbf{Z}$-algebras of finite type integrally
closed.}

\medskip
One can consider $\mathrm{A}'$ as the inductive limit of the filtrant family of its sub-$\mathbf{Z}$-algebras
(integral) of finite type $\mathrm{B}'_\lambda$; moreover, since $\mathrm{A}'$ is integrally closed and the integral
closure of a $\mathbf{Z}$-algebra of finite type is also a $\mathbf{Z}$-algebra of finite type $(7.8.3)$, one
sees that $\mathrm{A}'$ is the inductive limit of its sub-$\mathbf{Z}$-algebras of finite type $\mathrm{B}'_\lambda$ \emph{integrally closed};
if $\mathfrak{m}'_\lambda=\mathfrak{m}'\cap\mathrm{B}'_\lambda$, $\mathrm{A}'$ is also the inductive limit of the local sub-rings $(\mathrm{B}'_\lambda)_{\mathfrak{m}'_\lambda}=\mathrm{A}'_\lambda$
dominated by $\mathrm{A}'$ $(5.13.3)$. For every $\lambda$, $\mathrm{B}'_\lambda\cap\mathrm{A}$ is also the inductive limit of its sub-$\mathbf{Z}$-algebras
of finite type $\mathrm{B}_{\alpha\lambda}$, hence $\mathrm{A}=\varinjlim_{\alpha,\lambda}\mathrm{B}_{\alpha\lambda}$, and one can moreover replace $\mathrm{B}_{\alpha\lambda}$
by the local ring $\mathrm{A}_{\alpha\lambda}=(\mathrm{B}_{\alpha\lambda})_{\mathfrak{m}_{\alpha\lambda}}$, where $\mathfrak{m}_{\alpha\lambda}=\mathfrak{m}\cap\mathrm{B}_{\alpha\lambda}$, so that $\mathrm{A}_{\alpha\lambda}$ is
dominated by $\mathrm{A}'_\lambda$ and by $\mathrm{A}$. Set $\mathrm{Y}_{\alpha\lambda}=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A}_{\alpha\lambda})$; it follows from $(8.9.1)$ that there exist
a pair $(\alpha,\lambda)$ large enough, a morphism $f_{\alpha\lambda}:\mathrm{X}_{\alpha\lambda}\to\mathrm{Y}_{\alpha\lambda}$ of finite type and a
coherent $\sh{O}_{\mathrm{X}_{\alpha\lambda}}$-Module $\sh{F}_{\alpha\lambda}$ such that $\mathrm{X}=\mathrm{X}_{\alpha\lambda}\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_{\alpha\lambda}}\mathrm{A}$, $f=f_{\alpha\lambda}\otimes 1_\mathrm{A}$, $\sh{F}=\sh{F}_{\alpha\lambda}\otimes_{\mathrm{A}_{\alpha\lambda}}\mathrm{A}$; if $x_{\alpha\lambda}$
is the projection of $x$ in $\mathrm{X}_{\alpha\lambda}$, it will suffice to show that $\sh{F}_{\alpha\lambda}$ is $f_{\alpha\lambda}$-flat at the point $x_{\alpha\lambda}$.
Since $\mathrm{A}'$ is the inductive limit of the $\mathrm{A}'_\mu$ for $\mu\geq\lambda$, $\mathrm{X}'$ is the projective limit of the
$\mathrm{X}_{\alpha\lambda}\times_{\mathrm{Y}_{\alpha\lambda}}\mathrm{Y}'_\mu=\mathrm{X}'_\mu$, and one also has $\sh{F}'=\sh{F}'_{\alpha\mu}\otimes_{\mathrm{Y}_{\alpha\lambda}}\mathrm{Y}'_\mu$, where $\sh{F}'_{\alpha\mu}=\sh{F}_{\alpha\lambda}\otimes_{\mathrm{Y}_{\alpha\lambda}}\mathrm{Y}'_\mu$. Applying
\sref{IV.11.2.6}, one sees that one can take $\mu$ large enough for $\sh{F}'_\mu$ to be $\mathrm{Y}'_\mu$-flat at the
point $x'_\mu$, projection of $x'$ in $\mathrm{X}'_\mu$, and moreover, by construction of the $\mathrm{A}'_\mu$, the projection
of $x'_\mu$ in $\mathrm{Y}'_\mu$ is the closed point of $\mathrm{Y}'_\mu$.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{III)} \emph{Reduction to the case where the residue field $k'$ of $\mathrm{A}'$ is a finite extension and radicial of the
residue field $k$ of $\mathrm{A}$.}

\medskip
One can first of all repeat the reasoning of part I) of the
proof of \sref{IV.11.4.11}, taking into account the fact that $\mathbf{Z}$ is a ring of
\end{proof}
\subsection{Counter-examples}
\label{subsection:IV.11.7}

\begin{env}[11.7.1]
\label{IV.11.7.1}
Let us first consider the case where $\mathrm{A}$ is an \emph{Artinian} local ring, and where the hypotheses of \sref{IV.11.4.11} are satisfied \emph{except} for condition~(ii) concerning the residue field $k$ of $\mathrm{A}$.
We will see that the conclusion of \sref{IV.11.4.11} can then fail to hold.
Let $k$ be a field admitting a \emph{Galois} extension $k'$ of degree $[k':k]>1$, and let $\Gamma$ denote the Galois group of $k'$.
Let $\mathrm{A}$ be a $k$-algebra having a base of $3$ elements $1,a,b$ with the multiplication table $a^2=ab=ba=b^2=0$, so that $\mathrm{A}$ is an Artinian local ring whose maximal ideal $\mathfrak{m}=ka+kb$ is of square zero.
Let $\mathrm{A}'=\mathrm{A}\otimes_k k'$, which is a $k'$-algebra of base $1,a,b$, an Artinian local ring of maximal ideal $\mathfrak{m}'=k'a+k'b=\mathfrak{m}\mathrm{A}'$, of square zero; $\mathrm{A}$ identifies canonically with a subring of $\mathrm{A}'$.
Let $\mathfrak{Z}$ be the sub-$k'$-vector space of $\mathfrak{m}'$ generated by $a+\gamma b$, where $\gamma\in k'$ does not belong to $k$; it is clear that $\mathfrak{Z}$ is an ideal of $\mathrm{A}'$.
Let $\mathrm{B}=\mathrm{A}'/\mathfrak{Z}$; this is an Artinian ring which is an $\mathrm{A}$-module \emph{not flat}; indeed (Bourbaki, \emph{Alg.\ comm.}, chap.~II, \S~3, no.~2, cor.~2 of prop.~5), $\mathrm{B}$ would be a free $\mathrm{A}$-module, and since the canonical homomorphism $\mathrm{A}'/\mathfrak{m}\mathrm{A}'\to\mathrm{B}/\mathfrak{m}\mathrm{B}$ is bijective, the canonical homomorphism $\mathrm{A}'\to\mathrm{B}=\mathrm{A}'/\mathfrak{Z}$ would itself
\oldpage[IV-3]{158}
be bijective, which is absurd.
Alternatively, put $\mathrm{A}_1=\mathrm{B}$, and $\mathrm{Y}_1=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A}_1)$, and consider the canonical homomorphism $\mathrm{A}\to\mathrm{A}_1$, which is \emph{local} and \emph{injective} since $\mathfrak{Z}\cap\mathrm{A}=0$; let $\mathrm{B}_1=\mathrm{B}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}_1=\mathrm{B}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{B}$, and let us see that there is a point of $\mathrm{X}_1=\mathrm{X}\times_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{Y}_1=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{B}_1)$ where $\mathscr{O}_{x_1}$ is $\mathrm{Y}_1$-\emph{flat}.
For this, let us remark that we have $\mathrm{B}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{B}=(\mathrm{A}'\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}')/(\operatorname{Im}(\mathfrak{Z}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}')+\operatorname{Im}(\mathrm{A}'\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathfrak{Z}))$.
Now, the structure of $\mathrm{C}'=\mathrm{A}'\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'$ is easily obtained; one considers the product $\mathrm{C}=\prod\mathrm{A}'_\sigma$, where all the $\mathrm{A}'_\sigma$ are equal to $\mathrm{A}'$, and the map $\varphi:\mathrm{A}'\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'\to\mathrm{C}$ such that $\varphi(x\otimes y)=(\sigma(x)y)_{\sigma\in\Gamma}$ (the group $\Gamma$ operating canonically on $\mathrm{A}'$); passing to quotients, one deduces a homomorphism $\mathrm{C}'/\mathfrak{m}\mathrm{C}'\to\mathrm{C}/\mathfrak{m}\mathrm{C}$ which is none other than the canonical homomorphism $k'\otimes_k k'\to\prod k'_\sigma$ (with $k'_\sigma=k'$ for all $\sigma\in\Gamma$); it is known that this last one is bijective (Bourbaki, \emph{Alg.}, chap.~VIII, \S~8, prop.~4), whence it is the same for $\varphi$, since $\mathrm{C}'$ and $\mathrm{C}$ are free $\mathrm{A}$-modules \emph{libres} (Bourbaki, \emph{Alg.\ comm.}, chap.~II, \S~3, no.~2, cor.\ of prop.~6).
From what precedes, it follows that $\mathrm{B}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{B}$ is a semi-local $\mathrm{A}$-algebra, composed as a direct product of local $\mathrm{A}$-algebras $\mathrm{A}'/((\sigma(\mathfrak{Z}))+\mathfrak{Z})$.
That of these algebras corresponding to the identity of $\Gamma$ is isomorphic to $\mathrm{A}'/\mathfrak{Z}$, hence is an $\mathrm{A}_1$-module \emph{flat}, but for $\gamma\notin k$, it is clear that $\mathrm{R}$ is an irreductible and separable polynomial of $\mathrm{K}[\mathrm{T}]$; one deduces from this that $\mathrm{A}_1$ is first of all an integral ring whence the field of fractions $\mathrm{K}_1=\mathrm{K}[\mathrm{T}]/(\mathrm{R})$ is a \emph{separable} extension of $\mathrm{K}$.
In addition, if $t$ is the canonical image of $\mathrm{T}$ in $\mathrm{A}_1$, the $t^j$ ($0\leq j<n$) form a base of the $\mathrm{A}$-module $\mathrm{A}_1$, and their images in $k_1$ a base over $k$; one deduces from this that $d=\det(\operatorname{Tr}_{\mathrm{A}_1/\mathrm{A}}(t^{i+j}))$ is an element of $\mathrm{A}$ whose class in $k$ is $\neq 0$, and which is therefore invertible.
The same reasoning as in \sref{IV.6.12.4.1},~I) then proves that the morphism $\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A}_1)\to\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A})$ is flat and has regular fibres; one concludes by \sref{IV.6.5.4},~(ii)) that $\mathrm{A}_1$ is \emph{integrally closed}.
Let us then consider the ring $\mathrm{A}'_1=\mathrm{A}'\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}_1$; this is a free $\mathrm{A}'$-module of finite type, whence a semi-local ring (Bourbaki, \emph{Alg.\ comm.}, chap.~IV, \S~2, no.~5, cor.~3 of prop.~9); moreover, the maximal ideals of this finite $\mathrm{A}'$-algebra are all above the maximal ideal $\mathfrak{m}'$ of $\mathrm{A}'$, and \emph{a fortiori} contain $\mathfrak{m}\mathrm{A}'_1$.
But $\mathrm{A}'_1/\mathfrak{m}\mathrm{A}'_1=(\mathrm{A}'/\mathfrak{m}\mathrm{A}')\otimes_k k_1$, and since $k_1$ is a separable and finite extension of $k$, the radical of $\mathrm{A}'_1/\mathfrak{m}\mathrm{A}'_1$ is equal to $(\mathfrak{m}'/\mathfrak{m}\mathrm{A}')\otimes_k k_1$ (Bourbaki, \emph{Alg.}, chap.~VIII, \S~7, no.~2, cor.~2 of prop.~3); if $\mathfrak{n}_i$ ($1\leq i\leq r$) are the maximal ideals of $\mathrm{A}'_1$, the fields composing the algebra $k'\otimes_k k_1$, in other words the said fields are \emph{radicial} extensions of $k_1$.
Moreover, since $\mathrm{A}\to\mathrm{A}'$ is an injective homomorphism, so is $\mathrm{A}_1\to\mathrm{A}'_1$, and $\mathrm{A}_1$ being a flat $\mathrm{A}$-module; the canonical homomorphism $\mathrm{A}'_1\to\prod_{i=1}^r (\mathrm{A}'_1)_{\mathfrak{n}_i}$ being also injective (Bourbaki, \emph{Alg.\ comm.}, chap.~II, \S~3, no.~3, th.~1), the same holds for the composite $\mathrm{A}_1\to\prod_{i=1}^r(\mathrm{A}'_1)_{\mathfrak{n}_i}$.
But $\mathrm{A}_1$ is integral, and the kernels of the homomorphisms $\mathrm{A}_1\to(\mathrm{A}'_1)_{\mathfrak{n}_i}$ are finite in number; since their intersection is zero, one of them is already zero.
In other terms, there is a $\mathrm{B}_1=(\mathrm{A}'_1)_{\mathfrak{n}_i}$ such that the homomorphism $\mathrm{A}_1\to\mathrm{B}_1$ is \emph{injective} and \emph{local}.
Set $\mathrm{Y}'_1=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{B}_1)$, $\mathrm{X}'_1=\mathrm{X}'\times_{\mathrm{Y}'}\mathrm{Y}'_1$, $\mathscr{F}'_1=\mathscr{F}_1\otimes_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{Y}'_1$; $\mathrm{Y}'_1$-flat in \emph{all} the points of $\mathrm{X}'_1$ above $x'$; moreover the maximal ideal of $\mathrm{B}_1$ is the only one above $\mathfrak{m}'$, and hence all these points have for projection in $\mathrm{Y}'_1$ the closed point $y'_1$.
On the other hand, set $\mathrm{Y}_1=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A}_1)$, $\mathrm{X}_1=\mathrm{X}\times_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{Y}_1$, $\mathscr{F}_1=\mathscr{F}\otimes_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{Y}_1$; if $x_1$ is the projection of $x'_1$ in $\mathrm{X}_1$, and $x$ its projection in $\mathrm{X}$ and $y_1$ is the closed point $y_1$.
If one proves that $(\mathscr{F}_1)_{x_1}$ is a $\sh{O}_{y_1}$-module flat, it will follow that $\mathscr{F}_x$ is an $\sh{O}_y$-module flat; indeed $\sh{O}_{y_1}$ is a faithfully flat $\sh{O}_y$-module; whence \sref{IV.2.2.11},~(iii)) $\mathscr{F}_x$ is an $\sh{O}_y$-module flat.
As $\mathrm{X}'_1=\mathrm{X}_1\times_{\mathrm{Y}_1}\mathrm{Y}'_1$, $\mathscr{F}'_1=\mathscr{F}_1\otimes_{\mathrm{Y}_1}\mathrm{Y}'_1$, we have brought ourselves back to the situation of \sref{IV.11.6.1}, with $\mathrm{A}$ and $\mathrm{A}'$ replaced by $\mathrm{A}_1$ and $\mathrm{B}_1$ respectively.

IV) \emph{End of the proof.} --- We are finally reduced to proving \sref{IV.11.6.1} under the following supplementary hypotheses:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
  \item $\mathrm{A}$ and $\mathrm{A}'$ are local rings of $\mathbf{Z}$-algebras of finite type (hence \emph{excellent} rings \sref{IV.7.8.3}));
  \item $\mathrm{A}$ is integrally closed;
  \item the residue field $k'$ of $\mathrm{A}'$ is a finite and radicial extension of the residue field $k$ of $\mathrm{A}$.
\end{enumerate}

It is known then (\sref{IV.7.8.3} and \sref{IV.2.3.8}) that under conditions~(i) and~(ii), if $\mathfrak{m}$ and $\mathfrak{m}'$ are the maximal ideals of $\mathrm{A}$ and $\mathrm{A}'$ respectively, the topology on $\mathrm{A}$ is \emph{induced} by the $\mathfrak{m}'$-adic topology on $\mathrm{A}'$.
The completion $\hat{u}:\hat{\mathrm{A}}\to\hat{\mathrm{A}}'$ is therefore an injective homomorphism.
On the other hand, since the morphism $\operatorname{Spec}(k')\to\operatorname{Spec}(k)$ is radicial, it is the same for the morphism $f'^{-1}(y')\to f^{-1}(y)$ \sref[I]{I.3.5.7}, and there is therefore only \emph{one} point $x'$ of $\mathrm{X}'$ whose projections in $\mathrm{X}$ and $\mathrm{Y}'$ are $x$ and $y'$ respectively.
One can therefore apply the result of \sref{IV.11.5.2}.

\begin{corollary}[11.6.2]
\label{IV.11.6.2}
Let $\mathrm{A}$ be an integral local ring, unibranch, $\mathrm{Y}=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A})$, $f:\mathrm{X}\to\mathrm{Y}$ a locally of finite presentation morphism, $\sh{F}$ a quasi-coherent $\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}$-Module of finite presentation.
Let $\mathrm{A}'$ be a local ring, $\mathrm{A}\to\mathrm{A}'$ a local injective homomorphism; set $\mathrm{Y}'=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A}')$, $\mathrm{X}'=\mathrm{X}\times_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{Y}'$, $\mathscr{F}'=\mathscr{F}\otimes_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{Y}'$.
Let $x$ be a point of $\mathrm{X}$ whose projection $f(x)=y$ is the closed point of $\mathrm{Y}$; we suppose that $\mathscr{F}'$ is $f'$-flat in all the points $x'$ of $\mathrm{X}'$ whose projections in $\mathrm{X}$ and $\mathrm{Y}'$ are respectively $x$ and the closed point $y'$ of $\mathrm{Y}'$.
Then $\mathscr{F}$ is $f$-flat at the point $x$.
\end{corollary}

One can indeed take up again part~I) of the proof of \sref{IV.11.6.1}, which proves (with the same notations) that if $\mathscr{F}_1$ is $f_1$-flat in \emph{all} the points $x_1$ of $\mathrm{X}_1$ whose projections in $\mathrm{X}$ and $\mathrm{Y}_1$ are $x$ and $y_1$, then $\mathscr{F}$ is $f$-flat at the point $x$; one is thus brought back to the case where $\mathrm{A}$ is integrally closed and not geometrically unibranch.
On then applies the result of \sref{IV.11.5.2}.
\end{env}

\begin{env}[11.7.2]
\label{IV.11.7.2}
We will now show that the result of \sref{IV.11.5.4} loses its validity when one does not assume the morphism to be \emph{proper} (and \emph{a fortiori} \sref{IV.11.5.3} ceases to be exact when one does not assume $g$ to be \emph{proper}).
Let $k$ be a field, $\mathrm{A}_0$ the ring of polynomials $k[\mathrm{S},\mathrm{T}]$, $\mathrm{A}$ the quotient ring $\mathrm{A}_0/\mathrm{A}_0\mathrm{S}\mathrm{T}$; $\mathrm{Y}=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A})$ is thus the reducible curve formed by the two ``coordinate axes'' in the affine plane $\mathrm{V}_k^2=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A}_0)$.
$\mathrm{A}$ admits two minimal prime ideals $\mathfrak{p}_1=\mathrm{A}_0\mathrm{S}/\mathrm{A}_0\mathrm{S}\mathrm{T}$, $\mathfrak{p}_2=\mathrm{A}_0\mathrm{T}/\mathrm{A}_0\mathrm{S}\mathrm{T}$, and since $\mathrm{A}$ is reduced, it embeds in a canonical way; moreover $\mathrm{B}_1$ identifies with $k[\mathrm{T}]$ and $\mathrm{B}_2$ with $k[\mathrm{S}]$, whence these are integral rings, integrally closed, and thus $\mathrm{Z}=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{B})$ is none other than the \emph{normalised} of the prescheme $\mathrm{Y}$ with respect to $\mathrm{R}(\mathrm{Y})$ \sref[II]{II.6.3.8}, the \emph{sum} of the two schemes $\mathrm{Z}_1=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{B}_1)$, $\mathrm{Z}_2=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{B}_2)$.
Let us denote by $y$ the ``double point'' of $\mathrm{Y}$, corresponding to the maximal ideal $\mathfrak{p}_1+\mathfrak{p}_2=(\mathrm{S})$ of $\mathrm{A}$, by $z_1$ and $z_2$ the points of $\mathrm{Z}$ that project onto $y$, corresponding respectively to the maximal ideals $\mathfrak{m}_1=(\mathrm{T})$ and $\mathfrak{m}_2=(\mathrm{S})$ of $\mathrm{B}_1$ and $\mathrm{B}_2$.
We will denote by $\mathrm{X}$ the complement of $z_2$ in $\mathrm{Z}$, and thus $\mathrm{X}=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{B}_1\oplus(\mathrm{B}_2)_\mathfrak{s})$; it is immediate that the homomorphism $\mathrm{A}\to\mathrm{A}'=\mathrm{B}_1\oplus(\mathrm{B}_2)_\mathfrak{s}$ is \emph{injective}, and hence also the corresponding morphism $f:\mathrm{X}\to\mathrm{Y}$ is not a closed map (since $f(\mathrm{Z}_2-(z_2))$ is not closed in $\mathrm{Y}$); \emph{a fortiori} it is not proper.
We will now show that $f$ \emph{is not flat} at the point $z_1$; it will suffice to show \sref{IV.0.6.6.2} that $\mathscr{O}_{z_1}$ is not an $\sh{O}_y$-module flat, but the canonical homomorphism $\sh{O}_y\to\mathscr{O}_{z_1}$ is not \emph{injective}; however, the first projection $p:\mathrm{X}\times_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{X}\to\mathrm{X}$ is an \emph{isomorphism}: indeed, on the one hand, $\mathrm{B}_1\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{B}_1=(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{p}_1)\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{p}_1)=\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{p}_1$, and $(\mathrm{B}_2)_\mathfrak{s}\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{B}_2)_\mathfrak{s}=(\mathrm{B}_2)_\mathfrak{s}$, and finally $\mathrm{B}_1\otimes_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{B}_2)_\mathfrak{s}=0$, since the image of the generic element of $\mathrm{S}$ in $\mathrm{B}_1$ is zero.
\end{env}

\begin{env}[11.7.3]
\label{IV.11.7.3}
The preceding example can be generalised: one considers on a field $k$ an algebraic curve which is reduced and admits a single ``ordinary double point'' $y$ (a notion that will be defined more generally later on), and its \emph{normalised} $\mathrm{Z}$, so that the morphism $g:\mathrm{Z}\to\mathrm{Y}$ is finite, and that the restriction of $g$ to $\mathrm{Z}-g^{-1}(y)$ reduces to two ``simple'' points $z_1$, $z_2$; moreover the prescheme $g^{-1}(y)$ is the sum of the two preschemes $\operatorname{Spec}(k(z_1))$, $\operatorname{Spec}(k(z_2))$, canonically isomorphic to $\operatorname{Spec}(k(y))$.
Let $\mathrm{X}$ be the sub-prescheme of $\mathrm{Z}$ induced on the open $\mathrm{Z}-\{z_2\}$; the morphism $f:\mathrm{X}\to\mathrm{Y}$, restriction of $g$ to $\mathrm{X}$, is not closed.
The morphism $f$ is \emph{radicial}, for every $y'\in\mathrm{Y}$, the fibre $f^{-1}(y')$ is reduced to a point $x'$, $k(y')\to k(x')$ being an isomorphism.
Without the injection $j:\mathrm{X}\to\mathrm{Z}$, which is not closed, the morphism $f:\mathrm{X}\to\mathrm{Y}$ would not be proper, and without \sref[II]{II.5.4.3} it would also follow that it is not a finite morphism.
The morphism $f$ is \emph{radicial}, for every $y'\in\mathrm{Y}$, the fibre $g^{-1}(y')$ ne comprendrait qu'un seul point $x'$, $k(y')\to k(x')$ being an isomorphism.
Consider in particular the case where $\mathrm{Z}_\lambda$ is an open sub-prescheme induced on the open $\mathrm{Z}-\{z_2\}$; the morphism $f:\mathrm{X}\to\mathrm{Y}$ is the restriction of $g$ to $\mathrm{X}$.

One may also interpret $u'_\lambda$ as follows: it suffices to know the value of $u'_\lambda(t')$, where $t'$ is a section of $\mathscr{F}'$ above an open subset $\mathrm{U}'$ of $\mathrm{X}'$, of the following particular type: $t'$ is the restriction to $\mathrm{U}'$ of the canonical image by $\Gamma(\mathrm{U},\mathscr{F})\to\Gamma(g^{-1}(\mathrm{U}),\mathscr{F}')$ of a section $t$ of $\mathscr{F}$ above an open $\mathrm{U}$ of $\mathrm{X}$ containing $g(\mathrm{U}')$ (these sections generate the $\sh{O}_{\mathrm{X}'}$-Module $\mathscr{F}'$ \sref[0]{0.3.7.1}).
Consider the section $u_\lambda(t)$ of $\sh{G}_\lambda$ above $f_\lambda^{-1}(\mathrm{U})$, and its canonical image $t''$ by $\Gamma(f_\lambda^{-1}(\mathrm{U}),\sh{G}_\lambda)\to\Gamma(g_\lambda'^{-1}(f_\lambda^{-1}(\mathrm{U})),\sh{G}'_\lambda)$; then $u'_\lambda(t')$ is the restriction of $t''$ to $f_\lambda'^{-1}(\mathrm{U}')$.
Consider in particular the case where $\mathrm{Z}_\lambda$ is an open sub-prescheme of $\mathrm{X}$, where $j_\lambda:\mathrm{Z}_\lambda\to\mathrm{X}$ is the canonical injection, and where $u_\lambda$ is the canonical homomorphism $\rho_\mathscr{F}:\mathscr{F}\to(j_\lambda)_*(j_\lambda^*(\mathscr{F}))=(j_\lambda)_*(\sh{G}_\lambda)$ \sref[0]{0.4.4.3.2} corresponding to $j_\lambda$.
Then $\mathrm{Z}'_\lambda$ is induced on an open of $\mathrm{X}'$, and the preceding interpretation shows that $u'_\lambda$ corresponds to the canonical injection $j'_\lambda:\mathrm{Z}'_\lambda\to\mathrm{X}'$.
\end{env}

\begin{env}[11.7.4]
\label{IV.11.7.4}
One will note that in the preceding example the homomorphism $u$ is \emph{injective} when $\mathrm{Y}$ is \emph{irreducible} (one can for example take $\mathrm{Y}=\operatorname{Spec}(k[\mathrm{S},\mathrm{T}]/(\mathrm{S}(\mathrm{S}^2+\mathrm{T}^2)-(\mathrm{S}^2-\mathrm{T}^2)))$, ``cubique \`a point double''); one can indeed, replacing $\mathrm{Y}$ by an affine neighbourhood of $y$, suppose that $\mathrm{Y}=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A})$, where $\mathrm{A}$ is integral, whence $\mathrm{Z}=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{B})$, where $\mathrm{B}$ is the integral closure of $\mathrm{A}$; as $\mathrm{B}$, $\sh{O}_y$ and $\sh{O}_{z_1}$ identify then with subrings of the field of fractions of $\mathrm{A}$.
\end{env}

\begin{env}[11.7.5]
\label{IV.11.7.5}
The examples of \sref{IV.11.7.2} and \sref{IV.11.7.3} explain the restriction to \emph{unibranch} local rings in \sref{IV.11.6.1}.
We now wish to see that in \sref{IV.11.6.1}, one cannot weaken the hypothesis on $\mathrm{A}$ by supposing only that $\mathrm{A}$ is \emph{unibranch}.
Consider in fact the integral complete local ring $\mathrm{A}=\mathbf{R}[[\mathrm{U},\mathrm{V}]]/(\mathrm{U}^2+\mathrm{V}^2)$ which is unibranch but not geometrically unibranch \sref{IV.6.5.11}.
One knows (\emph{loc.\ cit.}) that if $u$, $v$ are the images of $\mathrm{U}$ and $\mathrm{V}$ in $\mathrm{A}$, the integral closure of $\mathrm{A}$ is $\bar{\mathrm{A}}=\mathrm{A}[t]$ with $t=v/u$, such that $t^2=-1$, so that $\bar{\mathrm{A}}$ is isomorphic to $\mathbf{C}[[\mathrm{U}]]$.
Set $\mathrm{Y}=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A})$, $\mathrm{X}=\operatorname{Spec}(\bar{\mathrm{A}})$ (normalised of $\mathrm{Y}$ \sref[II]{II.6.3.8}), and let $y$ and $x$ be the closed points of $\mathrm{Y}$ and $\mathrm{X}$ respectively; we will show that for a convenient $\mathrm{A}'$-algebra, if one sets $\mathrm{Y}'=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A}')$, $\mathscr{O}_{x'}$ is $\mathrm{Y}'$-flat in an arbitrary point of $\mathrm{X}'$ having these projections; it will then follow evidently that $\sh{O}_x$ is not $\mathrm{Y}$-flat at the point $x$ (which is moreover trivially \emph{a priori}, $\bar{\mathrm{A}}$ not being a free $\mathrm{A}$-module).

Let $\mathrm{B}=\mathrm{A}\otimes_\mathbf{R}\mathbf{C}$, isomorphic to $\mathbf{C}[[\mathrm{U},\mathrm{V}]]/(\mathrm{U}+i\mathrm{V})(\mathrm{U}-i\mathrm{V})$; $\mathrm{B}$ has two minimal prime ideals $\mathfrak{p}'$, $\mathfrak{p}''$ generated respectively by $u+iv$ and $u-iv$, and $\mathfrak{n}=\mathfrak{p}'+\mathfrak{p}''$ is the maximal ideal of the complete local ring $\mathrm{B}$.
Let $\bar{\mathrm{B}}=\bar{\mathrm{A}}\otimes_\mathbf{R}\mathbf{C}$; $\bar{\mathrm{B}}$ is a composite direct product of two algebras isomorphic to $\mathbf{C}[[\mathrm{U}]]$, generated by the idempotents $e'=(1\otimes 1+i\otimes i)/2$ and $e''=(1\otimes 1-i\otimes i)/2$; as the homomorphism $\mathrm{A}\to\bar{\mathrm{A}}$ is injective, so is $\mathrm{B}\to\bar{\mathrm{B}}$; one concludes immediately that $\bar{\mathrm{B}}$ identifies with $\bar{\mathrm{B}}\otimes_\mathrm{B}\mathrm{A}'$.
Taking $\mathrm{A}'$ to be the local ring $\mathrm{B}/\mathfrak{p}'$; then $\bar{\mathrm{A}}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'$ is isomorphic to $\mathrm{A}'\oplus(\mathrm{B}/\mathfrak{n})$.
This establishes our assertion, since $\mathrm{B}/\mathfrak{n}=\mathrm{A}'/(n/\mathfrak{p}')$ is not a flat $\mathrm{A}'$-module (without which it would be a free $\mathrm{A}'$-module (Bourbaki, \emph{Alg.\ comm.}, chap.~II, \S~3, no.~2, cor.~2 of prop.~5), which is absurd).
\end{env}

\subsection{A valuative criterion for flatness}
\label{subsection:IV.11.8}

\begin{theorem}[11.8.1]
\label{IV.11.8.1}
Let $f:\mathrm{X}\to\mathrm{Y}$ be a locally of finite presentation morphism, $\mathscr{F}$ a quasi-coherent $\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}$-Module of finite presentation, $x$ a point of $\mathrm{X}$, $y=f(x)$.
Suppose that the local ring $\sh{O}_y$ is integral (resp.\ reduced and Noetherian).
For $\mathscr{F}$ to be $f$-flat at the point $x$, it is necessary and sufficient that, for every valuation ring (resp.\ every discrete valuation ring) $\mathrm{A}'$ and every local homomorphism $\sh{O}_y\to\mathrm{A}'$, the following condition be satisfied: setting $\mathrm{Y}'=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A}')$, $\mathrm{X}'=\mathrm{X}\times_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{Y}'$, $f'=f_{(\mathrm{Y}')}$, the $\sh{O}_{\mathrm{X}'}$-Module $\mathscr{F}'=\mathscr{F}\otimes_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{Y}'$ is $f'$-flat in all the points $x'$ of $\mathrm{X}'$ whose projections in $\mathrm{X}$ and $\mathrm{Y}'$ are respectively $x$ and the closed point $y'$ of $\mathrm{Y}'$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The condition being evidently necessary \sref{IV.2.1.4}, it remains to prove that it is sufficient.
One can evidently (\sref[I]{I.3.6.5} and \sref[I]{I.2.4.4}) restrict to the case where $\mathrm{Y}=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A})$ is the spectrum of a \emph{local} ring $\mathrm{A}$ and $y$ is the closed point of $\mathrm{Y}$.
\medskip

(i) \emph{Case where $\mathrm{A}$ is integral.} --- Let $\mathrm{K}$ be the field of fractions of $\mathrm{A}$, $\mathrm{A}_1$ the integral closure of $\mathrm{A}$; if one sets $\mathrm{Y}_1=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A}_1)$, $\mathrm{X}_1=\mathrm{X}\times_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{Y}_1$, $f_1=f_{(\mathrm{Y}_1)}$, it suffices, by virtue of \sref{IV.11.5.5}, to show that $\mathscr{F}_1=\mathscr{F}\otimes_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{Y}_1$ is $f_1$-flat in \emph{all} the points $x_1$ of $\mathrm{X}_1$ whose projection $x$ is in $\mathrm{X}$.
Now, if $f_1(x_1)=y_1$, where $\mathfrak{m}_1$ is the trace on $\mathrm{A}$ (and is moreover necessarily a maximal ideal), then $\mathfrak{m}=\mathfrak{i}_y$ is the trace of $\mathfrak{m}_1$ on $\mathrm{A}$ (which is necessarily a prime ideal of $\mathrm{A}_1$ whence $\sh{O}_{y_1}=(\mathrm{A}_1)_{\mathfrak{m}_1}$; the local homomorphism $\mathrm{A}\to\sh{O}_{y_1}$ being local, so is $\mathrm{A}\to\mathrm{A}'$.
There exists then at least one
\oldpage[IV-3]{160}
point $x'\in\mathrm{X}'$ whose projections in $\mathrm{X}_1$ and $\mathrm{Y}'$ are respectively $x_1$ and $y'$ \sref[I]{I.3.4.7}; as the hypothesis states that $\mathscr{F}'$ is $f'$-flat at the point $x'$, and $\sh{O}_{y_1}$ is integrally closed, one can apply \sref{IV.11.6.1}, and one deduces that $\mathscr{F}_1$ is $f_1$-flat at the point $x_1$, whence the theorem in this case.
\medskip

(ii) \emph{Case where $\mathrm{A}$ is reduced and Noetherian.} --- Let $\mathfrak{p}_i$ ($1\leq i\leq m$) be the minimal prime ideals of $\mathrm{A}$; since $\mathrm{A}$ is reduced, it identifies canonically with a subring of the product of the $\mathrm{A}_i=\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{p}_i$, which are local Noetherian rings; setting $\mathrm{Y}_i=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A}_i)$, $\mathrm{X}_i=\mathrm{X}\times_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{Y}_i$, $f_i=f_{(\mathrm{Y}_i)}$, it follows from \sref{IV.11.5.2} that it suffices to show that for each $i$, $\mathscr{F}_i=\mathscr{F}\otimes_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{Y}_i$ is $f_i$-flat in every point $x_i$ of $\mathrm{X}_i$ whose projections in $\mathrm{X}$ and $\mathrm{Y}_i$ are $x$ and the closed point $y_i$ of $\mathrm{Y}_i$ respectively.
Now, since $\mathrm{A}_i$ is a local Noetherian integral ring, there exists in its field of fractions $\mathrm{K}_i$ a sub-ring $\mathrm{A}'_i$ containing $\mathrm{A}_i$, which is a semi-local Noetherian ring (hence a finite $\mathrm{A}_i$-algebra) and whose local rings are \emph{geometrically unibranch} (\sref{IV.6.15.5} and \sref[0]{0.23.2.5}).
Since the maximal ideals of $\mathrm{A}'_i$ are necessarily above the maximal ideal of $\mathrm{A}_i$, one deduces further from \sref[I]{I.3.4.7} and \sref{IV.11.5.2} that it suffices, setting $\mathrm{Y}'_i=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A}'_i)$, $\mathrm{X}'_i=\mathrm{X}\times_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{Y}'_i$, $f''_i=f_{(\mathrm{Y}'_i)}$, to prove that $\mathscr{F}''_i=\mathscr{F}\otimes_\mathrm{Y}\mathrm{Y}'_i$ is $f''_i$-flat in every point $x''_i$ of $\mathrm{X}''_i$ whose projections in $\mathrm{X}$ and $\mathrm{Y}'_i$ are $x$ and the closed point $y'_i$ of $\mathrm{Y}'_i$ respectively.
Now, let $\mathrm{A}'$ be a discrete valuation ring for $\mathrm{K}_i$ that dominates $\mathrm{A}'_i$, and let $x'$ be a point of $\mathrm{X}'$ whose projections in $\mathrm{X}'_i$ and $\mathrm{Y}'_i$ are $x''_i$ and $y'$ respectively \sref[I]{I.3.4.7}; as $\sh{O}_{y'_i}$ is geometrically unibranch, one can again apply \sref{IV.11.6.1} and one deduces that $\mathscr{F}''_i$ is indeed $f''_i$-flat at the point $x''_i$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remarks}[11.8.2]
\label{IV.11.8.2}
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
  \item In the statement of \sref{IV.11.8.1}, one cannot restrict to assuming that the condition on $\mathscr{F}'$ is satisfied for the \emph{complete} valuation rings $\mathrm{A}'$ and that the residue field is \emph{algebraically closed}.
  Indeed, it is known that every such valuation ring $\mathrm{A}'$ is dominated by a valuation ring $\mathrm{A}''$ \sref[II]{II.7.1.2}, and that if $\mathrm{A}'$ is a discrete valuation ring, it is the same for $\mathrm{A}''$ ($\mathbf{0}_{\mathrm{III}}$,~10.3.1).
  \item The proof of \sref{IV.11.8.1} simplifies when one supposes not only that $\mathrm{A}$ is integral and Noetherian, but that its completion $\hat{\mathrm{A}}$ is also \emph{integral}.
  Replacing $\mathrm{X}$ by $\mathrm{X}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\hat{\mathrm{A}}$ and reasoning as in the proof of \sref{IV.11.5.3}, one can in fact reduce to proving \sref{IV.11.8.1} when $\mathrm{A}=\sh{O}_y$ is integral, Noetherian, and \emph{complete}.
  Now, one knows \sref[II]{II.7.1.7} that such a ring $\mathrm{A}$ is dominated by a discrete complete valuation ring; the conclusion follows therefore directly from \sref{IV.11.5.2}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{remarks}

\subsection{Separating and universally separating families of homomorphisms of sheaves of modules}
\label{subsection:IV.11.9}

\begin{env}[11.9.1]
\label{IV.11.9.1}
Let $\mathrm{X}$ be a prescheme, $(f_\lambda)_{\lambda\in\mathrm{L}}$ a family of morphisms $f_\lambda:\mathrm{Z}_\lambda\to\mathrm{X}$, $\mathscr{F}$ a quasi-coherent $\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}$-Module; for every $\lambda\in\mathrm{L}$, suppose given a quasi-coherent $\sh{O}_{\mathrm{Z}_\lambda}$-Module $\sh{G}_\lambda$ and a homomorphism
\[
  u_\lambda:\mathscr{F}\to(f_\lambda)_*(\sh{G}_\lambda).
\label{IV.11.9.1.1}
\]
\end{env}

\oldpage[IV-3]{161}
We say that the family $(u_\lambda)$ (or the corresponding family of $u_\lambda^{\#}:(f_\lambda)^*(\mathscr{F})\to\sh{G}_\lambda$) is \emph{separating} if the intersection of the kernels of the $u_\lambda$ is \emph{zero}.
In other words, this signifies that for every open $\mathrm{U}$ of $\mathrm{X}$, and every section $t$ of $\mathscr{F}$ above $\mathrm{U}$, such that, for \emph{every} $\lambda$, the section $u_\lambda(t)$ (which, by definition, is a section of $\sh{G}_\lambda$ above $f_\lambda^{-1}(\mathrm{U})$) is zero, then $t$ is itself zero.

\begin{env}[11.9.2]
\label{IV.11.9.2}
With the notations of \sref{IV.11.9.1}, let $\mathrm{M}$ be a second set of indices; for every $\lambda\in\mathrm{L}$, let $(g_{\lambda\mu})_{\mu\in\mathrm{M}}$ be a family of morphisms $g_{\lambda\mu}:\mathrm{Z}'_{\lambda\mu}\to\mathrm{Z}_\lambda$; for every pair $(\lambda,\mu)$, suppose given a quasi-coherent $\sh{O}_{\mathrm{Z}'_{\lambda\mu}}$-Module $\sh{H}_{\lambda\mu}$ and a homomorphism $v_{\lambda\mu}:\sh{G}_\lambda\to(g_{\lambda\mu})_*(\sh{H}_{\lambda\mu})$; set $h_{\lambda\mu}=f_\lambda\circ g_{\lambda\mu}:\mathrm{Z}'_{\lambda\mu}\to\mathrm{X}$ and consider the composite homomorphism
\[
  w_{\lambda\mu}:\mathscr{F}\xrightarrow{u_\lambda}(f_\lambda)_*(\sh{G}_\lambda)\xrightarrow{(f_\lambda)_*(v_{\lambda\mu})}(h_{\lambda\mu})_*(\sh{H}_{\lambda\mu}).
\]
Suppose that, for every $\lambda\in\mathrm{L}$, the family $(v_{\lambda\mu})_{\mu\in\mathrm{M}}$ is separating: then, it is the same for the family $(f_\lambda)_*(v_{\lambda\mu})$ ($\mu\in\mathrm{M}$), as one sees at once.
One concludes that, for the family $(u_\lambda)$ to be separating, it is necessary and sufficient that the family $(w_{\lambda\mu})_{(\lambda,\mu)\in\mathrm{L}\times\mathrm{M}}$ be so.
\end{env}

\begin{env}[11.9.3]
\label{IV.11.9.3}
To verify that the family $(u_\lambda)$ is separating (with the notations of \sref{IV.11.9.1}), one can evidently reduce first to the case where $\mathrm{X}$ is \emph{affine}, the property being local on $\mathrm{X}$.
One can moreover suppose that $\mathrm{Z}_\lambda=\mathrm{X}$ \emph{for every} $\lambda\in\mathrm{L}$.
Indeed, let $\mathrm{M}$ be an index set, sum of a family $(\mathrm{M}_\lambda)_{\lambda\in\mathrm{L}}$, and for every $\lambda\in\mathrm{L}$, let $(\mathrm{Y}_{\lambda\mu})_{\mu\in\mathrm{M}_\lambda}$ be an open affine covering of $\mathrm{Z}_\lambda$; let $j_{\lambda\mu}:\mathrm{Y}_{\lambda\mu}\to\mathrm{Z}_\lambda$ be the canonical injection and posons $\sh{H}_{\lambda\mu}=j_{\lambda\mu}^*(\sh{G}_\lambda)=\sh{G}_\lambda|\mathrm{Y}_{\lambda\mu}$.
If one considers the canonical homomorphism $v_{\lambda\mu}:\sh{G}_\lambda\to(j_{\lambda\mu})_*(j_{\lambda\mu}^*(\sh{G}_\lambda))=(j_{\lambda\mu})_*(\sh{H}_{\lambda\mu})$ relative to $j_{\lambda\mu}$ \sref[0]{0.4.4.3.2}, it is immediate that for each $\lambda\in\mathrm{L}$, the family $(v_{\lambda\mu})_{\mu\in\mathrm{M}_\lambda}$ is separating.
By virtue of \sref{IV.11.9.2}, one is therefore reduced to proving that the family of composite homomorphisms $((f_\lambda)_*(v_{\lambda\mu}))\circ u_\lambda$, where the $f_\lambda$ are \emph{affine}, is separating.
But then the $(f_\lambda)_*(\sh{G}_\lambda)$ are quasi-coherent $\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}$-Modules \sref[I]{I.1.6.2} and, by virtue of the definition, one can replace the $\mathrm{Z}_\lambda$ by $\mathrm{X}$ and the $\sh{G}_\lambda$ by $(f_\lambda)_*(\sh{G}_\lambda)$, whence our assertion.

One will note further that if $\mathrm{L}$ is \emph{finite} and the $f_\lambda$ are \emph{quasi-compact}, one can, in the preceding reduction, suppose that the $\mathrm{M}_\lambda$ are also \emph{finite}, whence in this case one is brought back to verifying that a \emph{finite} family of homomorphisms of $\mathscr{F}$ in quasi-coherent $\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}$-Modules is separating.
\end{env}

\begin{env}[11.9.4]
\label{IV.11.9.4}
Consider therefore the case where $\mathrm{Z}_\lambda=\mathrm{X}$ for every $\lambda$, and where $\mathrm{X}=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A})$ is affine; then $\mathscr{F}=\widetilde{\mathrm{M}}$ and $\sh{G}_\lambda=\widetilde{\mathrm{N}}_\lambda$, where $\mathrm{M}$ and $\mathrm{N}_\lambda$ are $\mathrm{A}$-modules, and $u_\lambda=\widetilde{\varphi}_\lambda$, where the $\varphi_\lambda:\mathrm{M}\to\mathrm{N}_\lambda$ are $\mathrm{A}$-module homomorphisms.
To say that the family $(u_\lambda)$ is separating signifies then that, for every $s\in\mathrm{A}$, the intersection of the kernels of $(\varphi_\lambda)_s:\mathrm{M}_s\to(\mathrm{N}_\lambda)_s$ is \emph{separating}.
One will note that if $\mathrm{L}$ is \emph{finite}, it comes down to saying also that the intersection of the kernels of the $\varphi_\lambda$ is zero.
One says then also that the family $(\varphi_\lambda)$ is \emph{separating}.
One will note that if $\mathrm{L}$ is \emph{finite}, then $\bigcap_{\lambda\in\mathrm{L}}\operatorname{Ker}((\varphi_\lambda)_s)=(\bigcap_{\lambda\in\mathrm{L}}\operatorname{Ker}(\varphi_\lambda))_s$ \sref[0]{0.1.3.5}.
But this relation is no longer exact in general
\oldpage[IV-3]{162}
when $\mathrm{L}$ is \emph{infinite}, and the fact that the intersection of the kernels of the $\varphi_\lambda$ is zero \emph{does not therefore entail},
in general, that the family $(\varphi_\lambda)$ is separating.
For example, suppose $\mathrm{A}$ is a discrete valuation ring of maximal ideal $\mathfrak{m}$, and consider the family of homomorphisms $\varphi_k:\mathrm{A}\to\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{m}^k$, whose intersection of kernels $\mathfrak{m}^k$ is reduced to $0$; this family is nevertheless not separating, for the fibres of all the $\mathfrak{m}^k$ at the generic point $x$ of $\mathrm{X}=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A})$ (which is open in $\mathrm{X}$) are equal to $\sh{O}_x=k(x)$, the field of fractions of $\mathrm{A}$, and their intersection is therefore not reduced to $0$.
\end{env}

\begin{env}[11.9.5]
\label{IV.11.9.5}
We will mainly be concerned in what follows with the problem of \emph{change of base} for separating families.
The notations being those of \sref{IV.11.9.1}, consider a morphism $g:\mathrm{X}'\to\mathrm{X}$ and set $\mathscr{F}'=\mathscr{F}\otimes_{\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}}\sh{O}_{\mathrm{X}'}=g^*(\mathscr{F})$, and, for every $\lambda$, $\mathrm{Z}'_\lambda=\mathrm{Z}_\lambda\times_\mathrm{X}\mathrm{X}'$, $f'_\lambda=f_\lambda\times 1$, $\sh{G}'_\lambda=\sh{G}_\lambda\otimes_{\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}}\sh{O}_{\mathrm{X}'}=g_\lambda^*(\sh{G}_\lambda)$, where $g'_\lambda:\mathrm{Z}'_\lambda\to\mathrm{Z}_\lambda$ is the projection.
For every $\lambda$, we denote by $u'_\lambda:\mathscr{F}'\to(f'_\lambda)_*(\sh{G}'_\lambda)$ the homomorphism obtained as follows: let
\[
  \psi:(f_\lambda)_*(\sh{G}_\lambda)\to(f_\lambda)_*((g'_\lambda)_*((g'_\lambda)^*(\sh{G}_\lambda)))=g_*((f'_\lambda)_*(\sh{G}'_\lambda))
\]
be the homomorphism $(f_\lambda)_*(\rho_{g_\lambda})$, where $\rho_{g_\lambda}$ is the canonical homomorphism \sref[0]{0.4.4.3.2} corresponding to $g_\lambda$.
Then $u'_\lambda$ is defined as the composite
\[
  g^*(\mathscr{F})\xrightarrow{g^*(u_\lambda)}g^*((f_\lambda)_*(\sh{G}_\lambda))\xrightarrow{g^*(\psi)}(f'_\lambda)_*(\sh{G}'_\lambda)
\]
where $\sigma=\sigma_{(f_\lambda)_*(\sh{G}'_\lambda)}$ is the canonical homomorphism \sref[0]{0.4.4.3.3} corresponding to $g$.
We will say for short that $u'_\lambda$ is \emph{deduced from $u_\lambda$ by the change of base} $g$.
When $f_\lambda:\mathrm{Z}_\lambda\to\mathrm{X}$ is an \emph{affine} morphism, $(f_\lambda)_*(\sh{G}'_\lambda)=g^*((f_\lambda)_*(\sh{G}_\lambda))$, and in this case one has therefore simply $u'_\lambda=g^*(u_\lambda)$.

One can also interpret $u'_\lambda$ in the following manner: it suffices to know the value of $u'_\lambda(t')$, where $t'$ is a section of $\mathscr{F}'$ above an open $\mathrm{U}'$ of $\mathrm{X}'$, of the following particular type: $t'$ is the restriction to $\mathrm{U}'$ of the canonical image by $\Gamma(\mathrm{U},\mathscr{F})\to\Gamma(g^{-1}(\mathrm{U}),\mathscr{F}')$ of a section $t$ of $\mathscr{F}$ above an open $\mathrm{U}$ of $\mathrm{X}$ containing $g(\mathrm{U}')$ (these sections generate the $\sh{O}_{\mathrm{X}'}$-Module $\mathscr{F}'$ \sref[0]{0.3.7.1}).
Consider the section $u_\lambda(t)$ of $\sh{G}_\lambda$ above $f_\lambda^{-1}(\mathrm{U})$, and its canonical image $t''$ by $\Gamma(f_\lambda^{-1}(\mathrm{U}),\sh{G}_\lambda)\to\Gamma(g_\lambda'^{-1}(f_\lambda^{-1}(\mathrm{U})),\sh{G}'_\lambda)$; then $u'_\lambda(t')$ is the restriction of $t''$ to $f_\lambda'^{-1}(\mathrm{U}')$.
Consider in particular the case where $\mathrm{Z}_\lambda$ is an open sub-prescheme of $\mathrm{X}$, where $j_\lambda:\mathrm{Z}_\lambda\to\mathrm{X}$ is the canonical injection, and where $u_\lambda$ is the canonical homomorphism $\rho_\mathscr{F}:\mathscr{F}\to(j_\lambda)_*(j_\lambda^*(\mathscr{F}))=(j_\lambda)_*(\sh{G}_\lambda)$ \sref[0]{0.4.4.3.2} corresponding to $j_\lambda$.
Then $\mathrm{Z}'_\lambda$ is induced on an open subset of $\mathrm{X}'$, and the preceding interpretation shows that $u'_\lambda$ corresponds to the canonical injection $j'_\lambda:\mathrm{Z}'_\lambda\to\mathrm{X}'$.
\end{env}

\begin{env}[11.9.6]
\label{IV.11.9.6}
Under the conditions of \sref{IV.11.9.5}, suppose that $\mathrm{X}$ and $\mathrm{X}'$ are both affine, and that one wishes to prove that for every section $t'$ of $\mathscr{F}'$ above $\mathrm{X}'$, whose images by all the $u'_\lambda$ are zero, then $t'$ is itself zero.
Then, one can also restrict to the case where $\mathrm{Z}_\lambda=\mathrm{X}$ for every $\lambda\in\mathrm{L}$.
Indeed, by \sref{IV.11.9.3} and \sref{IV.11.9.5}, if one sets $\mathrm{Y}'_{\lambda\mu}=g_\lambda'^{-1}(\mathrm{Y}_{\lambda\mu})$, the homomorphism $v'_{\lambda\mu}$ deduced from $v_{\lambda\mu}$ by the change of base $g$ is none other than the canonical homomorphism
\oldpage[IV-3]{163}
$\rho_{g'_\lambda}:\sh{G}'_\lambda\to(j'_{\lambda\mu})_*(j_{\lambda\mu}'^*(\sh{G}'_\lambda))$ corresponding to the canonical injection $j'_{\lambda\mu}:\mathrm{Y}'_{\lambda\mu}\to\mathrm{Z}'_\lambda$, as we saw in \sref{IV.11.9.5}.
The assertion follows then from the reasonings of \sref{IV.11.9.2} and \sref{IV.11.9.3}, $\mathrm{Y}_{\lambda\mu}$ and $v_{\lambda\mu}$ being replaced by $\mathrm{Y}'_{\lambda\mu}$ and $v'_{\lambda\mu}$.

One has an analogous reduction when one wishes to prove that the family $(u'_\lambda)$ is \emph{separating} ($\mathrm{X}$ and $\mathrm{X}'$ being affine): this follows again from \sref{IV.11.9.2} and \sref{IV.11.9.3}.
\end{env}

\begin{proposition}[11.9.7]
\label{IV.11.9.7}
With the notations of \sref{IV.11.9.1} and \sref{IV.11.9.5}, suppose $\mathrm{X}=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A})$ and $\mathrm{X}'=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A}')$ affine, and moreover that $\mathrm{A}'$ is a projective $\mathrm{A}$-module.
Then, if the family $(u_\lambda)$ is separating, every section $t'$ of $\mathscr{F}'$ above $\mathrm{X}'$ whose images by all the $u'_\lambda$ are zero, is itself zero.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
One has seen in \sref{IV.11.9.6} that one can restrict to the case where all the $\mathrm{Z}_\lambda$ are \emph{equal to} $\mathrm{X}$.
The proposition is then a consequence of the following lemma:
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[11.9.7.1]
\label{IV.11.9.7.1}
Let $\mathrm{A}$ be a ring, $(\mathrm{M}_\lambda)_{\lambda\in\mathrm{L}}$ a family of $\mathrm{A}$-modules, $\mathrm{M}$ an $\mathrm{A}$-module, and for each $\lambda$, $u_\lambda:\mathrm{M}\to\mathrm{M}_\lambda$ a homomorphism.
Suppose that the intersection of the kernels of the $u_\lambda$ is reduced to zero.
Then, for every projective $\mathrm{A}$-module $\mathrm{N}$, the intersection of the kernels of the homomorphisms $u_\lambda\otimes 1:\mathrm{M}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{N}\to\mathrm{M}_\lambda\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{N}$ is reduced to zero.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Indeed, $\mathrm{N}$ is a direct factor of a free $\mathrm{A}$-module $\mathrm{L}$, and it suffices evidently to prove that the intersection of the kernels of the homomorphisms $v_\lambda:\mathrm{M}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{L}\to\mathrm{M}_\lambda\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{L}$ is reduced to zero, since $u_\lambda\otimes 1$ is the restriction of $v_\lambda$.
But the assertion follows then trivially from the hypothesis.
\end{proof}

\begin{remarks}[11.9.8]
\label{IV.11.9.8}
We do not know if, under the hypotheses of proposition \sref{IV.11.9.7}, the family $(u'_\lambda)$ is \emph{separating}: one would need indeed to prove \sref{IV.11.9.4} that, for every open $\mathrm{D}(h')\subset\mathrm{X}'$ (where $h'\in\mathrm{A}'$) such that the $u'_\lambda(t')$ are all zero, then $t'$ is itself zero.
Now, one cannot apply proposition \sref{IV.11.9.4} to $\mathrm{D}(h')=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A}'_{h'})$, for the fact that $\mathrm{A}'$ is a projective $\mathrm{A}$-module (or even a free one), does not imply that $\mathrm{A}'_{h'}$ is a projective $\mathrm{A}$-module.
For example, one can take for $\mathrm{A}$ a discrete valuation ring and for $\mathrm{A}'$ a valuation ring that is an $\mathrm{A}$-module free of rank $2$.
\end{remarks}

One has however the following result:

\begin{corollary}[11.9.9]
\label{IV.11.9.9}
Let $\mathrm{X}$ be an Artinian prescheme, $g:\mathrm{X}'\to\mathrm{X}$ a flat morphism (note that these two conditions are satisfied if $\mathrm{X}$ is the spectrum of a \emph{field} and $g$ is an arbitrary morphism).
Then, with the notations of \sref{IV.11.9.1} and \sref{IV.11.9.5}, if the family $(u_\lambda)$ is separating, it is the same for the family $(u'_\lambda)$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
One can evidently restrict to the case where $\mathrm{X}=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A})$ is the spectrum of an Artinian local ring $\mathrm{A}$ \sref[I]{I.6.2.2}; one notes then that for every open affine $\mathrm{U}'=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A}')$ of $\mathrm{X}'$, $\mathrm{A}'$ is a flat $\mathrm{A}$-module, hence \emph{projective} ($\mathbf{0}_{\mathrm{III}}$,~10.1.3).
It suffices therefore to apply \sref{IV.11.9.7} to every open affine of $\mathrm{X}'$ to obtain the corollary.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[11.9.10]
\label{IV.11.9.10}
Let $\mathrm{X}$ be a prescheme, $(u_\lambda)$ a family of homomorphisms \sref{IV.11.9.1}, $g:\mathrm{X}'\to\mathrm{X}$ a morphism, $(u'_\lambda)$ the family of homomorphisms deduced from $(u_\lambda)$ by the change of base $g$ \sref{IV.11.9.5}.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
  \item If $g$ is a faithfully flat morphism and if the family $(u'_\lambda)$ is separating, then the family $(u_\lambda)$ is separating.
  \item Suppose that $g$ is a flat morphism and moreover that one of the two following conditions is satisfied:
  \begin{enumerate}[label=\emph{\alph*)}]
    \item $\mathrm{L}$ is finite and the $f_\lambda$ are quasi-compact.
    \item The morphism $g$ is locally of finite presentation.
  \end{enumerate}
  Then, if the family $(u_\lambda)$ is separating, it is the same for the family $(u'_\lambda)$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\oldpage[IV-3]{164}
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
  \item By virtue of \sref{IV.2.2.8}, it suffices to show that if a section $t$ of $\mathscr{F}$ above an open $\mathrm{U}$ of $\mathrm{X}$ belongs to the kernel of each of the $u_\lambda$, its image $t'$ by the canonical homomorphism $\Gamma(\rho):\Gamma(\mathrm{U},\mathscr{F})\to\Gamma(g^{-1}(\mathrm{U}),g^*(\mathscr{F}))=\Gamma(\mathrm{U},g_*(g^*(\mathscr{F})))$ is zero.
  Now the images of $t'$ by the $g^*(u_\lambda)$ are the images of $u_\lambda(t)$ by the homomorphism $\Gamma(\mathrm{U},(f_\lambda)_*(\sh{G}_\lambda))\to\Gamma(g^{-1}(\mathrm{U}),g^*((f_\lambda)_*(\sh{G}_\lambda)))$, whence they are zero, and \emph{a fortiori} $u'_\lambda(t')=0$ for every $\lambda$, whence $t'=0$ by hypothesis, which proves~(i).

  \item The question being local on $\mathrm{X}$ and $\mathrm{X}'$, one can restrict to the case where $\mathrm{X}=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A})$ and $\mathrm{X}'=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A}')$ are affine, $\mathrm{A}'$ being a flat $\mathrm{A}$-module, and to proving that, for every section $z'$ of $\mathscr{F}'$ above $\mathrm{X}'$ whose images by all the $u'_\lambda$ are zero, then $z'$ is itself zero.
  On has seen in \sref{IV.11.9.3} that one can also restrict to the case where $\mathrm{Z}_\lambda=\mathrm{X}$ for every $\lambda\in\mathrm{L}$.
  Let us distinguish now the two cases.
  \medskip

  \emph{a)} Let $\mathscr{F}=\widetilde{\mathrm{M}}$, $\sh{G}_\lambda=\widetilde{\mathrm{N}}_\lambda$, and set $\mathrm{M}'=\mathrm{M}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'$, $\mathrm{N}'_\lambda=\mathrm{N}_\lambda\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'$, so that $\mathscr{F}'=\widetilde{\mathrm{M}'}$, $\sh{G}'_\lambda=\widetilde{\mathrm{N}'_\lambda}$; by abuse of language, let us still denote by $u_\lambda$ the homomorphism $\mathrm{M}\to\mathrm{N}_\lambda$, and by $u'_\lambda$ the homomorphism $\mathrm{M}'\to\mathrm{N}'_\lambda$.
  Given an element $z'\in\mathrm{M}'$ such that $u'_\lambda(z')=0$ for every $\lambda$; it remains to prove that $z'=0$.
  Now, the hypothesis that $g$ is flat and of finite presentation implies, by \sref{IV.11.3.15}, that there exists a finite family $(s_i)_{1\leq i\leq n}$ of elements of $\mathrm{A}$, such that, if one sets $\mathfrak{J}_k=s_1\mathrm{A}+\ldots+s_k\mathrm{A}$, and $\mathrm{A}_i=\mathrm{A}_{s_i}/\mathfrak{J}_{i-1}\mathrm{A}_{s_i}$, the ring $\mathrm{A}'_i=\mathrm{A}'\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}_i$ is a \emph{free} $\mathrm{A}_i$-module for $1\leq i\leq n$, and $\mathfrak{J}_n=\mathrm{A}$.
  The proposition will be established if we prove the following assertion for $1\leq i\leq n$:
  \begin{enumerate}[label=$(*_i)$]
    \item \emph{There exists an integer $m_i>0$ such that $s_j^{m_i}z'=0$ for $j\leq i$.}
  \end{enumerate}
  Indeed, setting $k=m_n$ and noting that the $s_i^k$ ($1\leq i\leq n$) generate the unit ideal of $\mathrm{A}$, the assertion $(*_n)$ will show that $z'$, as a linear combination of the $s_i^k z'$, is zero.

  Let us prove $(*_i)$ by induction on $i$, the assertion being trivially true for $i=0$.
  Suppose $i>0$ and let $m$ be a common multiple of the $m_j$ for $j\leq i-1$.
  We note (for $1\leq h\leq n$) that if $\mathfrak{J}_h$ is the ideal generated by the $s_j^m$ ($0\leq j\leq h$), $\mathfrak{J}_h/\mathfrak{J}_{h-1}$ is nilpotent; replacing the $s_j$ ($1\leq j\leq n$) by $s_j^m$ without changing the properties of the $\mathfrak{J}_h$ and the $\mathrm{A}'_h$, and supposing $m=1$.
  Then $z'$, being annihilated by $\mathfrak{J}_{i-1}$, identifies with an element of $\operatorname{Hom}_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}'/\mathfrak{J}_{i-1}\mathrm{A}',\mathrm{M}')$, and this replacement comes down to replacing, for $1\leq h\leq n$, $\mathrm{A}_h$ by $\mathrm{A}_h/\mathfrak{J}_{h-1}\mathrm{A}_h$, $\mathrm{A}'_h$ by $\mathrm{A}'_h/\mathfrak{J}_{h-1}\mathrm{A}'_h$; since $\mathrm{A}'$ is a flat $\mathrm{A}$-module, it follows from ($\mathbf{0}_{\mathrm{III}}$,~10.1.2) that $\mathrm{B}'_h=\mathrm{A}'\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{B}_h$ is also a free $\mathrm{B}_h$-module.
  One can therefore
  \oldpage[IV-3]{165}
  replace all the $s_j$ ($1\leq j\leq n$) by $s_j^m$ without changing the properties of $\mathfrak{J}_h$ and $\mathrm{A}'_h$, and suppose $m=1$.
  Then $z'$, being annihilated by $\mathfrak{J}_{i-1}$, identifies with a $(\operatorname{Hom}_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J}_{i-1},\mathrm{M}))_t$ and, analogously replacing $\mathrm{M}$ by $\mathrm{N}_\lambda$ since the ideal $\mathfrak{J}_{i-1}$ is of finite type \sref[0]{0.1.3.5}; whence by our assertion \sref{IV.11.9.4}.
  Replacing $\mathrm{A}$ by $\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J}_{i-1}$, $\mathrm{M}$ by $\mathrm{N}_\lambda$, $\mathrm{A}'$ by $\mathrm{A}'/\mathfrak{J}_{i-1}\mathrm{A}'$, we see that one can reduce to the case where, in the initial situation, $l'$ is the element $s=s_i\in\mathrm{A}$ such that $\mathrm{A}'_s$ is a free $\mathrm{A}_s$-module.
  Let $v_\lambda=\operatorname{Hom}(1,u_\lambda):\operatorname{Hom}_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J}_{i-1},\mathrm{M})\to\operatorname{Hom}_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J}_{i-1},\mathrm{N}_\lambda)$, the homomorphism deduced from $u_\lambda$; the family $(v_\lambda)$ is also \emph{separating}.
  Indeed, for every $t\in\mathrm{A}$, $(\operatorname{Hom}_\mathrm{A}(1,u_\lambda))_t=\operatorname{Hom}_\mathrm{A}(\mathrm{A}/(u_\lambda)_t)$ and from this the assertion follows, since the intersection of the kernels of the $(u_\lambda)_t$ is zero and since the intersection of the kernels of $(v_\lambda)_t=\operatorname{Hom}(1,(u_\lambda)_t)$, whence our assertion \sref{IV.11.9.4}.
  Replacing $\mathrm{A}$ by $\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J}_{i-1}$, $\mathrm{M}$ by $\mathrm{N}_\lambda$ and finally $\mathrm{A}'$ by $\mathrm{A}'/\mathfrak{J}_{i-1}\mathrm{A}'$, one sees that one can bring oneself to the case where, in the initial situation, the element $s=s_i\in\mathrm{A}$ is such that $\mathrm{A}_s'$ is a free $\mathrm{A}_s$-module.
  Since $(u'_\lambda)_s=0$, it follows by \sref{IV.11.9.7} that $z'/1=0$ in $\mathrm{M}'_s$, which signifies that there exists an integer $r$ such that $s^r z'=0$ in $\mathrm{M}'$, which finishes the proof of $(*_i)$ by induction.
  \medskip

  \emph{b)} Let $\mathscr{F}=\widetilde{\mathrm{M}}$, $\sh{G}_\lambda=\widetilde{\mathrm{N}}_\lambda$, and set $\mathrm{M}'=\mathrm{M}\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'$, $\mathrm{N}'_\lambda=\mathrm{N}_\lambda\otimes_\mathrm{A}\mathrm{A}'$; by abuse of language, let us denote again by $u_\lambda$ the homomorphism $\mathrm{M}\to\mathrm{N}_\lambda$ and by $u'_\lambda$ the homomorphism $\mathrm{M}'\to\mathrm{N}'_\lambda$.
  Given an element $z'\in\mathrm{M}'$ such that $u'_\lambda(z')=0$ for every $\lambda$; it is a matter of proving that $z'=0$.
  Now, $\mathrm{B}$ is Noetherian, $\mathrm{M}$ and the $\mathrm{N}_\lambda$ are $\mathrm{B}$-modules, the $\mathrm{N}_\lambda$ being flat $\mathrm{A}$-modules.
  Moreover, the question being local on $\mathrm{X}$ and $\mathrm{S}$, one can restrict to the case where $\mathrm{Z}_\lambda=\mathrm{X}$ for every $\lambda$, $\mathscr{F}=\widetilde{\mathrm{M}}$, $\sh{G}_\lambda=\widetilde{\mathrm{N}}_\lambda$, where $\mathrm{B}$ is Noetherian, $\mathrm{M}$ is a $\mathrm{B}$-module of finite type, and the $\mathrm{N}_\lambda$ are flat $\mathrm{A}$-modules, and restrict to proving that, if $t\in\mathrm{M}$ is such that $u_\lambda(t)=0$ for every $\lambda$, then $t=0$.
  To show that $t=0$, it suffices to prove that the image $t_\mathfrak{p}$ of $t$ in $\mathrm{M}_\mathfrak{p}$ is zero for every maximal ideal $\mathfrak{p}$ of $\mathrm{B}$ (Bourbaki, \emph{Alg.\ comm.}, chap.~II, \S~3, no.~3, cor.~1 of th.~1).
  One therefore needs also to show that the intersection
  of the kernels of the $(u_\lambda)_\mathfrak{p}:\mathrm{M}_\mathfrak{p}\to(\mathrm{N}_\lambda)_\mathfrak{p}$ deduced from $(u_\lambda)$ by the change of base $\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{B}_\mathfrak{p})\to\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{B})$ is reduced to $0$.
  Otherwise stated, one is brought back to the case where $\mathrm{B}$ is a local Noetherian ring, $\mathrm{M}$ a $\mathrm{B}$-module of finite type, the ideals of $\mathrm{A}$ whose maximal ideal $\mathfrak{m}$ is nilpotent.
  Suppose therefore that $\mathfrak{J}^{n+1}=0$ ($n$ an integer $\geq 0$) and let us reason by induction on $n$, the assertion being trivially true for $n=0$.
  If $\bar{t}$ is the class of $t$ in $\mathrm{M}/\mathfrak{J}^n\mathrm{M}$, the class of $u_\lambda(t)$ in $\mathrm{N}_\lambda/\mathfrak{J}^n\mathrm{N}_\lambda$ is zero for every $\lambda$, whence, by the hypothesis of induction, $\bar{t}=0$, otherwise said $t\in\mathfrak{J}^n\mathrm{M}$.
  Now $\mathfrak{J}^n=\mathfrak{J}^n/\mathfrak{J}^{n+1}$ is a free $(\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J})$-module; if $(e_\alpha)$ is a base of this module, one can therefore write $t=\sum_\alpha e_\alpha t_\alpha^0$, with $t_\alpha^0\in\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J}$, zero for all but a finite number of indices.
  On the other hand, since $\mathrm{N}_\lambda$ is a flat $\mathrm{A}$-module, $\mathfrak{J}^n\otimes_{\mathrm{A}/\mathfrak{J}}(\mathrm{N}_\lambda/\mathfrak{J}\mathrm{N}_\lambda)$ identifies with $\mathfrak{J}^n\mathrm{N}_\lambda$ and one can therefore write $u_\lambda(t)=\sum_\alpha e_\alpha u_{\lambda 0}(t_\alpha^0)=\sum_\alpha e_\alpha\otimes u_{\lambda 0}(t_\alpha^0)$.
  Since by hypothesis $u_\lambda(t)=0$, one deduces that $u_{\lambda 0}(t_\alpha^0)=0$ for every $\alpha$ and every $\lambda$; whence $t_\alpha^0=0$ for every $\alpha$, and hence $t=0$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[11.9.11]
\label{IV.11.9.11}
\emph{Remark.} --- It suffices to simplify to the case where $\mathrm{Z}_\lambda=\mathrm{X}$ for every $\lambda$.
It should be noted that if the family of homomorphisms $u_\lambda:\mathscr{F}\to\sh{G}_\lambda$ is separating, it does \emph{not necessarily} follow that, for every $x\in\mathrm{X}$, the intersection of the kernels of the homomorphisms $(u_\lambda)_x:\mathscr{F}_x\to(\sh{G}_\lambda)_x$ is reduced to $0$.
For example, let $\mathrm{X}$ be a locally Noetherian prescheme of Jacobson, of dimension $\geq 1$, and $\mathscr{F}$ a coherent $\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}$-Module; for every closed point $x$ of $\mathrm{X}$, and every integer $n\geq 0$, $\mathfrak{m}_x^n\mathscr{F}$ is a coherent $\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}$-Module with support contained in $\{x\}$.
The family of canonical homomorphisms $\mathscr{F}\to\mathscr{F}/\mathfrak{m}_x^n\mathscr{F}$ (where $x$ runs through the set $\mathrm{X}_0$ of closed points of $\mathrm{X}$ and $n$ the set of integers $\geq 0$) is \emph{separating}: indeed, if $t$ is a section of $\mathscr{F}$ above an open $\mathrm{U}$ of $\mathrm{X}$ whose images in the $\Gamma(\mathrm{U},\mathscr{F}/\mathfrak{m}_x^n\mathscr{F})$ are all zero, it follows at once that for every closed point $x\in\mathrm{U}$, $t_x=0$; as the set of closed points contained in $\mathrm{U}$ is very dense in $\mathrm{U}$ \sref{IV.10.2.1}, and if $\mathscr{F}=\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}$, taking $\mathscr{F}=\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}$, and if $y$ is a \emph{non-closed} point of $\mathrm{X}$, one has $(\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}/\mathfrak{m}_x^n\sh{O}_\mathrm{X})_y=0$ for every closed point $x$ of $\mathrm{X}$, but $\sh{O}_{\mathrm{X},y}\neq 0$, and the intersection of the kernels of the homomorphisms $\sh{O}_{\mathrm{X},y}\to(\sh{O}_\mathrm{X}/\mathfrak{m}_x^n\sh{O}_\mathrm{X})_y$ is equal to $\sh{O}_{\mathrm{X},y}$.
\end{remark}

\begin{lemma}[11.9.12]
\label{IV.11.9.12}
Let the notations be those of \sref{IV.11.9.1} and \sref{IV.11.9.5}, and suppose the family $(u_\lambda)$ separating; suppose in addition that $\mathrm{X}$ is an $\mathrm{S}$-prescheme, where $\mathrm{S}=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A})$ is affine, and that $\mathrm{X}'=\mathrm{X}\times_\mathrm{S}\mathrm{S}'$, where $\mathrm{S}'=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A}')$, $\mathrm{A}'$ being an $\mathrm{A}$-algebra; suppose finally that the $\sh{G}_\lambda$ are $\mathrm{S}$-flat.
Let $(\mathrm{A}'_\alpha)_{\alpha\in\mathrm{I}}$ be the filtered family of sub-$\mathrm{A}$-algebras of $\mathrm{A}'$ of finite type, and let $\mathrm{S}'_\alpha=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{A}'_\alpha)$, $\mathrm{X}''_\alpha=\mathrm{X}\times_\mathrm{S}\mathrm{S}'_\alpha$, $\mathrm{Z}''_{\alpha\lambda}=\mathrm{Z}_\lambda\times_\mathrm{X}\mathrm{X}''_\alpha$, and let $\mathscr{F}''_\alpha$, $\sh{G}''_{\alpha\lambda}$ and $u''_{\alpha\lambda}$ be the morphisms, Modules, and homomorphisms of Modules deduced from $f_\lambda$, $\mathscr{F}$, $\sh{G}_\lambda$ and $u_\lambda$ by means of the change of base $\mathrm{X}''_\alpha\to\mathrm{X}$.
Then, if for every $\alpha\in\mathrm{I}$, the family $(u''_{\alpha\lambda})_{\lambda\in\mathrm{L}}$ is separating, it is the same for $(u'_\lambda)_{\lambda\in\mathrm{L}}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
